# Abstraction layers: fractal summary and detail above and below the object

**Status:** Design stage, not started. Seeded 2026-08-05 from conversation. **No code is in scope yet** --- the deliverable of the first several phases is a settled set of answers, recorded here, and only then a vertical slice. Nothing in this file belongs in a durable `AGENT.contract.md` until it ships; see [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md) on wishlist normative text. **Refined 2026-08-11, not reversed:** "no code yet" gates *provisional* decisions, not decisions generally --- see [Graduation tiers: when a decision licenses code](#graduation-tiers-when-a-decision-licenses-code-2026-08-11). A decision already **locked**, or explicitly run as a **Prototype**, may license code ahead of Phase 6.

This document is task-scoped and follows [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md).

**Three companion files.** They are part of the plan, not appendices. The split follows the **companion-file ladder** in [`taskPlanning/AGENT.designVariant.md`](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.md#the-companion-file-ladder) --- by durability class, not by topic --- and **all four files dispose together**; see [Lifecycle](#lifecycle).

| File | Class | Holds | Read it when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **This file** | Control surface | Status, open decisions, recommended order, verification, progress | Always, first |
| [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md) | Proposal record | P1--P6, working hypotheses, the two sweeps | For the design content itself --- **read its header table first**, live and superseded schemes sit side by side |
| [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md) | Evidence corpus | Phase 0's worked cases, C1--C22 | Always, before arguing any row --- Phases 1 and 2 take their input from it |
| [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md) | Evidence corpus | Channel C's reducer cases, LC1--LC6 | Before writing or changing the rebuild --- **a corpus for a channel, not a plan**, and the only one whose anchor is the cache rather than code or play |
| [`AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md`](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md) | **Implementation plan --- base framework, not this variant** | Building ports into `ludicGraph`; consumes PQ verdicts, owns no design | When CC1 is blocked, or when a PQ row about ports graduates --- **it cites rows here and never restates them; design fixes go upstream, into this file** |
| [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md) | Reasoning trail | The chronological record | Only when you need *why* a row is shaped the way it is |

**Reference convention, applied throughout all five files (2026-08-10; the fifth opened 2026-08-14): every clause reference names its construct.** **`clause N` never appears bare** --- because *clause* denotes five different things here, and the overlap is total where it matters: **H1** (1a, 1b, 2--5), **H3** (1--9), **P2** (1--3), **P5** (1--11) and the [locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) (1--3) **all define a clause 1 and a clause 3**, which between them were half of every reference in the file. *P3 and P4 define no clauses of their own*, so a reference inside those sections always points elsewhere. **Possessive and adjectival forms are equivalent** --- *H3 clause 6*, *the locked frame's clause 1*, *locked-frame clause 3*; what is required is that the construct name sit adjacent to the reference. **Do not infer a referent from the enclosing section.** It does not predict, in either direction: inside **P3** the bare references meant **H1** (P3 was written arguing about H1), and inside the **locked frame** section `clause 1b` means **H1**. Two exceptions are deliberate and should not be "fixed": a *metalinguistic* mention, where the token `clause 1` is being quoted rather than used, and a reference already qualified by adjacent prose (*H1's old clauses 4/5*).

**One durable landing has already happened, ahead of any code.** The [locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06)'s two shape claims now live in [`positions/AGENT.concepts.md` --- Wholes, parts, and ports](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) as a **Target** mental model. That is the authority for what a whole, a part and a port *are*; this plan remains the authority for everything still open about them, and nothing has gone to `AGENT.contract.md`.

**Sibling initiative, deliberately separate:** [`AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md`](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md) is the standing index for the **parse and reasoning pipeline** (classify -> Parse -> Plan -> Synthesize -> kernel). That ladder's rungs *consume* the world model; this initiative changes *what the world model is*. They interact --- see [Relationship to the object-manipulation ladder](#relationship-to-the-object-manipulation-ladder) --- but a rung on that ladder is the wrong container for a data-structure question that also reaches WML authoring, perception, and diegetic state.

---

## Getting Started

**Read in this order.** The control surface is short; the design body below it is not, and reading that body out of order is how a withdrawn scheme gets absorbed as current.

1. **The Status line and companion table above** --- what stage this is at, and which of the four files answers which kind of question.
2. **[`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md) once**, then **[`AGENT.designVariant.md`](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.md)**. This is a **design-stage** plan, and three things follow from that: resolved decisions [graduate](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.md#graduating-a-resolved-row) rather than being deleted, open rows may block no slice at all, and code is licensed by [tier](#graduation-tiers-when-a-decision-licenses-code-2026-08-11) rather than by shipping.
3. **[Recommended order](#recommended-order)**, immediately below --- what is next, and which single channel builds anything.
4. **[The corpus](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md)**, C1--C22. Phases 1 and 2 take their input from it; a row argued without it is argued from intuition.
5. **The design body in this file** --- [why the initiative exists](#why-this-initiative-exists), [the five axes](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to), [the relation-kind register](#the-relation-kind-register), and the [locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06).
6. **[Open decisions](#open-decisions-design--plan-only) and the [constraint matrix](#constraint-matrix)**, last. **The rows are written in the vocabulary steps 4 and 5 establish** --- *port*, *whole*, *part*, *the five axes*, *relation kind* --- and are not readable ahead of it. That is why this section, not the decision table, is what a cold reader hits first.

**Reference convention, before you read any row:** `clause N` never appears bare; every clause reference names its construct. The rule and its two deliberate exceptions are in the [header note above](#abstraction-layers-fractal-summary-and-detail-above-and-below-the-object).

### What is live design, and what is retained but superseded

**The [proposal record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md) holds live and withdrawn schemes side by side, and they read alike.** A superseded proposal in that file is dense, confident and internally consistent, because it was written to be believed. **Its header table is authoritative and should be read before any of its sections**; this is the summary.

| Section | State |
| --- | --- |
| [H3 --- ports as the boundary mechanism](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) | **Live.** The mechanism everything downstream assumes. |
| [P4 --- settling, dataflow and narrative heat](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat) | **Live.** |
| [P6 --- `ludicCache`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure) | **Live**, and still provisional. Channel C prototypes one iteration of it under a named rollback trigger. |
| [H1 --- single-host objects](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) and [Proposal P1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) | **Withdrawn 2026-08-07**, tested to falsification and replaced by H3. **Retained as evidence, not as description** --- the falsification is load-bearing, and deleting it invites the same road to be walked twice. |

**The [locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) is not in that file, and that is deliberate.** It is normative --- rows may not re-argue it --- so it sits with the open decisions it constrains rather than in the record of things once considered. Its [exclusion list](#what-is-not-locked-and-must-not-ride-in-on-this) is as load-bearing as the lock itself.

### Test orientation

**Only [Channel C](#recommended-order) builds anything**; every phase is design-only, and no durable doc has acquired normative text. Commands live in [Verification](#verification) and that section is the authority if anything here conflicts with it. The minimum before editing Channel C code, from `lambda/ephemera`:

```bash
cd lambda/ephemera && npm run test -- --watchAll=false dataSource/positions/
```

**`npm run test`, not `npm test`, and `npx tsc --noEmit` is not sufficient** --- `*.integration.test.ts` files sit outside the tsconfig include and mock by module *path*, so a rename can typecheck clean and still break the suite.

**The documentation check is not optional here.** Four files cross-link heavily and a broken cross-file anchor fails **silently**; see [Verification](#verification) for the anchor and contract-diff checks that close each slice.

## Recommended order

Use `[ ]` for pending and `[X]` for complete; mark nested lines `[X]` as each sub-step finishes. **Phases 1 through 3 are one design pass, not three sequential closures** --- see Phase 4.

**Channel C steps are `CC0`--`CC10`, renamed from `C0`--`C10` on 2026-08-13, and the rename is not cosmetic.** The corpus runs [C1--C22](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#phase-0-corpus), so every step id collided with a case id --- **`C7` denoted both *instrument the counterfactual miss* and [Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)**, and both were live in the same discussion within a day of the channel being written. This is [the clause-reference convention](#abstraction-layers-fractal-summary-and-detail-above-and-below-the-object) at the head of this file arriving one construct over: **a bare id must denote one thing.** `CC` is the channel's prefix; a bare `C<n>` always means a corpus case.

**Channel C is the only part of this list that builds anything, and it runs *parallel* to the phases rather than after them** --- added 2026-08-13 with [the iteration ladder](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-ludiccache-iteration-ladder-exhaustive-first-persisted-second-2026-08-13). It is listed first because it is the live work, **not** because the phases are its prerequisites; they are not. **The ordering that must not be broken is the same one the ledger channel carries: the corpus judges the iteration, never the reverse.** A Channel C step that appears to settle AH-3, PC-3 or any C-series row has produced a *measurement*, not a finding.

- [ ] **Channel C (parallel, and the first code this initiative licenses). The `ludicCache` iteration ladder.** Elected a **Prototype** per [Graduation tiers](#graduation-tiers-when-a-decision-licenses-code-2026-08-11); rollback trigger in [P6 Status](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#status). Work from `lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/`.
  - [ ] **CC0. Define the type contract, before any rebuild code.** Added 2026-08-13, and the gap it fills was structural rather than an oversight: **CC1 specifies the *process* of rebuilding and describes the end product only incidentally, while CC2 asserts a discipline "in the type" that no step ever defines.** This is that step. **The schema is settled here deliberately, or it falls out of an implementation** --- and a schema that falls out of an implementation is how PQ-4 gets answered by convenience. **It is a decision agenda, not a build step; the items below are the things that must be *chosen*, with the constraint on each named so the choice is made against something.**
    - [X] **Distinct type, or a reuse of `EphemeraPositionGraph`?** The reuse is tempting --- it is `{ nodes, edges }` either way --- and it is the choice that makes CC2 hardest, because a cache that *is* a `ludicGraph` can be handed to anything expecting one, which is the port-boundary crossing arriving as convenience. **Whichever way it goes, record the reason**; this is the single decision most likely to be made by autocomplete. **Settled 2026-08-13 from conversation: a distinct type, and the reason is not the defensive one this bullet anticipated.** The reuse was never available: **`edges` carry `crossings`** (CC1b) and **`nodes` carry `embedding`** (below), so the two types do not have the same shape and the reuse would not have type-checked. **CC2's port-boundary protection is therefore a consequence of the decision rather than its argument** --- worth stating in that direction, since a protection that falls out of the shape is not one a later convenience can erode. **Name correction, and this bullet was the one place it had teeth:** the shipped type is **`EphemeraLudicGraphData`** (`{ hostId, nodes, edges? }`, [`ephemeraMeta.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts)), with `EphemeraLudicGraphFieldPayload` as its `Omit<..., 'hostId'>` stored form; `EphemeraPositionGraph` is pre-rename vocabulary that the [naming consequence](#the-naming-consequence-positiongraph-will-be-lying) section predicted and the 2026-08-12 rename executed. **Identifier: `hostId` only, no separate `ludicCacheId`.** A UUID would be a differentiator for a fork nobody has --- and it would be the ceremony this plan declines elsewhere on cause. **The re-open trigger is named rather than left to intuition: it re-opens if [clause 1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-five-clauses) admits a second tenant, and not otherwise.** `hostId`-only asserts **one cache per host**, so a second tenant over the same host --- consequence-reasoning acquiring its own cache, say --- is exactly and only what makes `hostId` stop being a key; clause 1's restatement leaves that question open in as many words (*"what is in question is whether reference-location is the cache's only tenant"*), so the trigger is live rather than hypothetical. **This also makes the record-key bullet below consistent rather than independently chosen** --- `LCACHE#<host>` already assumed 1:1, and a differentiator would have silently outvoted it. **One hazard it creates, and it cuts opposite to the graph's:** the locked frame has `ludicGraph`'s `hostId` scheduled for re-typing and probable rename to **`rootId`**, because under [locked-frame clause 3](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) it points **in**, at a member of `nodes`. The cache's `hostId` points **out**, at the room host it consolidates --- the older outward meaning. **Same field name, opposite direction, in two adjacent types that both have `nodes` and `edges`, so the rename change must skip the cache rather than sweep it.** Considered and declined: renaming the cache's field to dodge the collision, which would be minting a differentiator for readability immediately after declining one for cause.
    - [X] **Node shape.** Address, `shortName`, and whatever tag the consumer needs. **The address must encode the node's home shard**, since the cache is flat and its members come from many; and note [`EphemeraLudicGraphNode`'s third-tag obligation](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) is already outstanding for Room roots and negatives-as-nodes. **Settled 2026-08-13 from conversation: extend `EphemeraLudicGraphNode` in place, in `mtw-interfaces`, and build the cache node on it as a superset.** **The reason the extension is safe is that it is not the cache's:** `ludicGraph` needs the wider union on its own account, since [locked-frame clause 3](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) puts a root in every graph's own `nodes` list and a Room root can be neither `'Character'` nor `'Object'`. **This is the locked frame's own outstanding obligation arriving on schedule, not a widening the cache asked for** --- which matters because it is the one thing that distinguishes it from the *convenience rather than a finding* failure CC1b and CC1c are both written against. **The missing tag today is exactly one: `Room`.** Hosts are `EphemeraRoomId | EphemeraCharacterId` ([`ephemeraPositionAdjacency.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraPositionAdjacency.ts)), Character and Object are already union members, so `Room` is the whole of the gap. **`Area` is deliberately not added**, despite being the obvious companion: it is a roadmap comment (`OBJECT# / AREA# later`), not a host, and minting a node kind for a host kind that does not exist is precisely the move locked-frame clause 3's *"decides no inventory"* forbids --- *a reader concluding from locked-frame clause 3 that Features are hosts has read an inventory claim into a structure claim.* Area rides in on the change that makes `AREA#` a host. **That gate fired 2026-08-16 and this note is kept as the record of why it was deferred, not as a live deferral.** `EphemeraAreaId` was minted, `'AREA'` joined `EphemeraId`'s tag union, and `AREA#` is now a membership host with real `Meta::Area.ludicGraph` storage --- so the precondition this bullet named is satisfied. **The obligation is now scheduled rather than discharged:** `EphemeraLudicGraphNode` is still `Character | Object | Room` in [`ephemeraMeta.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts), and adding the `Feature` and `Area` tags is owned by [LP4b](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#recommended-order), which already derives both from LP0 rather than needing a fresh argument. **Nothing here is unowned** --- do not read this bullet as blocking work. **The third-tag obligation has two claimants and this discharges only one:** **AB-42**'s negatives-as-nodes wants a member that *"genuinely cannot be an Object"*, which is not `Room`; that half stays outstanding and must not be marked done by this. **The cache node is a superset, not the same type** --- `EphemeraLudicGraphNode` is `{ tag, universalKey }`, and the cache adds **`shortName`** (CC3's pool must name its candidates), the **home shard** (`universalKey` is globally unique so it identifies the node, but it does not say which shard to traverse from, and that is what CC3's traversal-from-address needs), and **`embedding?`**. **The shared tag vocabulary is single-sourced without the cache node becoming substitutable for a graph node** --- CC2's hazard was the *graph* type being handable to anything expecting one, and a superset node inside a distinct graph type does not reach it. **`embedding` is optional at CC0's scale, and the reason is that optional-to-required is a *population* decision, not a type one:** required obliges every rebuild to produce one, which is the cost CC1c priced. ~~**Two venues for it to become required, and they are not equivalent** --- iteration 2 (consistent with CC1c as written, on CC6's numbers) or CC1c itself (a deliberate **re-open** of a named gate, now written into that step with a trigger rather than left as a standing option to change our minds).~~ **Superseded the same day, and the venue question is closed: CC1c reversed on evidence** --- the parse ingress already fetches these embeddings unconditionally, so the deferral's premise was a cost claim the code does not support. **The field stays optional in the type and *is populated* in iteration 1**, filled by a separate `attachEmbeddings` pass rather than by the rebuild, which is what keeps CC1 pure; see CC1c for the evidence and for the struck trigger, which was aimed at the wrong layer. **Optional-not-required still holds** --- the type must admit a cache whose embeddings have not been attached yet, since rebuild and attach are two steps. **One obligation, PQ-10 category, landing *in* the change and never after:** `isEphemeraLudicGraphNode` and its neighbouring predicates are hand-rolled, and a widened union that skips them goes stale silently. **Corrected 2026-08-14 from conversation: *the* home shard is a cardinality error, and this plan had already settled the other way.** A whole is **multi-hosted whenever its ports bind into more than one host** --- `OBJECT#string` lying across `OBJECT#table`, through `ROOM#Room`, into `OBJECT#box`, present in all three by three distinct ports, with its parts (`stringEnd1`, `span`, `stringEnd2`) nodes in its own graph throughout. **[Finding 1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#finding-1-parts-on-different-hosts-is-a-pre-lock-premise-and-one-row-is-built-entirely-on-it) does not rescue the scalar, and was misread in conversation as doing so** --- it moves multi-hosting *off the parts and onto the whole* (*"the **whole** has ports into different hosts"*), which is exactly the case a scalar cannot hold. **The settled position is AB-8's**, re-framed 2026-08-09 toward (a)-with-ports: *presence is **the set of graphs holding a qualified reference***, `Theseus -Held-> thread:1` and `room -In-> thread:2` over **one** thread graph. This bullet wrote *the home shard*, singular, five days later without consulting it. **So the field is `homeShards`, required and possibly singleton, never scalar** --- the identical uniformity taken for `crossings` one bullet down, and for the identical reason: the common case is a one-element list and nothing branches on cardinality. **Shipped code already agrees, which is what makes the scalar a self-inflicted wound rather than a hard call:** `getMembershipContainers` returns `EphemeraMembershipHostId[]`, and [`compileRelationalFromSkeleton`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/compileRelationalFromSkeleton.ts) guards `containers.length === 1` and **declines to resolve a host at all** otherwise rather than picking one. **What the scalar would have cost is extent, silently** --- the [no-silent-omission](#correction-same-day-reasoning-wants-no-silent-omission-not-losslessness) property the terminal bullet protects on the edge side, broken on the node side. **One consequence routed rather than absorbed: the type now admits what a shipped gate refuses.** AB-8 already books `evaluateComplexityPreGates`' terminal `multiPresent` error as wrong-as-written with the reconciliation owed to Phase 1; this is that obligation arriving again from the cache side, not a new one.
    - [X] **Edge shape, with `crossings`.** Source, target, and the per-hop record of **relation-text presented at that level** and **host entered**. **Settled 2026-08-13 from conversation: the `ludicGraph` edge shape plus `crossings`, typed `{ edgeText: string; into: <host id> }[]`** --- one entry per **consolidated** boundary the edge passes through, in order. **`into` is a host id (`EphemeraMembershipHostId` today), not a cache id**, which is CC0's identifier decision arriving one field over: having declined a separate `ludicCacheId`, a `crossings` entry that named one would have re-minted it by the back door. CC1b's own wording already agreed --- *the relation-text presented at that level and **the host entered***. **The correction that made the rest of this bullet work, recorded because it reads as obvious afterward and was silently re-assumed once:** an edge is **not a single-boundary object**. Consolidation is **per-host and ragged** --- a room may consolidate a bookshelf's contents and *not* the contents of a box on that bookshelf, so an edge has a **chain** whose consolidation status varies along its length. **The invariant is per-hop, not per-edge:** at each hop, a consolidated host becomes a `crossings` entry and traversal continues; the first host that is not consolidated is where the edge **terminates**, as a port address. An earlier formulation had one flag per edge determining both node and terminal representation; that assumed consolidation was a **uniform frontier**, which is precisely what [clause 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-five-clauses) is not --- attention-scoping tracks what has been *looked into*, not what falls within N levels.
    - [X] **Is a same-shard edge a zero-crossing edge, or a different thing?** **Take the uniformity unless something forbids it** --- this plan already ran this argument and won it: *[a loop that sometimes iterates zero times is one mechanism](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs)*, which is what rescued position 2 and what PQ-13 leans on. Two edge kinds here would re-create the whole/part split one layer down. **Settled 2026-08-13 from conversation: zero-crossing edge --- and the user's argument is structural where this bullet's was by analogy, so it is the one to keep.** **`crossings` is a *supplement* to the existing edge shape, so a same-shard edge is the unchanged `ludicGraph` edge with an empty array.** There is no second construct to design, and **the zero case is not a degenerate corner --- it is what every shipped edge already is.** The *loop that iterates zero times* precedent still holds and is still the right family, but it is now confirmation rather than the load-bearing reason. **It also falls out of CC1b's algorithm rather than being imposed on it:** *concatenate, then join* emits zero-crossing edges at the concatenate step, and the join rewrites only port-paired edges --- so uniformity is what the construction produces, not a rule applied afterward. **Implementation consequence, and it cuts against local convention deliberately: `crossings` is required and possibly empty, never optional.** The shipped precedent is the other way --- `edges?` on `EphemeraLudicGraphData` is *"absent or `[]` when none"* --- and that is affordable there but not here: **an optional field puts a two-case branch at every read site, and `if (edge.crossings)` is the two-edge-kinds split re-appearing in the consumer instead of the type.** The uniformity is only cashed if nothing branches on presence. **What this gives CC2, which is the part worth having:** the invariant **`crossings` is empty iff the edge is same-shard** is a one-line property over the returned graph, and **a cross-shard edge can present as zero-crossing only if a crossing was discarded** --- so it is a direct, cheap test of CC2's assertion (i), *no relation-text merged or tie-broken across a crossing*, which is the assertion CC2 says to hold hardest and had no obvious way to check. **The collapse defect cannot hide, because collapsing is the only way to produce the illegal shape.**
    - [X] **How an unresolved terminal is represented** --- a target that is a **port address into a host the cache does not hold**, rather than a resolved node. Under iteration 1's exhaustive fill this is nearly unreachable, which is precisely why it must be in the type from the start: it is [CC5's dead-code hazard](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#three-hazards-recorded-so-they-are-designed-for-rather-than-discovered) in schema form. **Settled 2026-08-13 from conversation, and the settlement is that there is nothing to invent here.** **The terminal is a port address --- the graph's own boundary construct, not a cache-local representation of *we stopped*.** The box is a separate host whether or not we consolidate it, and the port into it already exists in `ludicGraph`; **consolidating is nothing more than following that port.** This is [locked-frame clause 2](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) doing the job it was locked for --- *an explicit binding the interior owns*. **The rejected alternative is named because it is the tempting one:** a cache-local terminal marker would be **a second mechanism for a boundary the graph already has one for**, which is AB-36's failure verbatim --- *"a special-purpose index built to recover what nesting supplies structurally: a second mechanism for a subclass, which is position 3."* **The fill rule for iteration 1 is the closed one: nodes present in this cache, edges among them, plus port terminals where a *consolidated* shard's edge targets a *resident but unconsolidated* host.** **This narrows the case count from two to one.** CC1b's *"where only one side is resident"* was read as a terminal into a host with **no** node in the cache; working the join through shows otherwise --- one side missing means the room was consolidated and the rope was not, and the rope's node **is** in the cache as a member of the room's `nodes` list. **That is the box case.** The only construction producing a terminal with nothing local to point at needs a *multi-hosted object we did consolidate*, one of whose interior egress ports pairs to a host outside this cache --- **and the iteration-1 fill rule drops that edge**, per CC1b's other sentence, *"for multi-hosted objects, only the parts present in this host contribute."* **The two CC1b sentences genuinely disagreed and this is which one won.** **Taking the closed rule costs nothing in schema**, since the box case needs the unresolved terminal anyway --- and it **upgrades CC5's *nearly unreachable* from an observation to a construction**, which is a better footing for a dead-code hazard than a frequency estimate. **What the terminal means, and it is a positive signal rather than an absence:** *not consolidated here* --- and, per the user, **strong evidence that there probably (not definitely) *is* something further, in that host's own `ludicCache` record.** Not definitely, because a port may bind an interior that is empty or holds nothing relevant. **This makes the cache network mirror the graph network**: following a terminal It is now three, and they are not equally expressible: **(i) no relation-text merged or tie-broken across a crossing** --- expressible, and the one to hold hardest; **(ii) the terminal is an address, never an expanded neighborhood**; **(iii) hop-count not readable by referent-matching** --- which is **PC-5**, may not be expressible in a type at all, and must be **routed there explicitly rather than quietly weakened into a review norm.** Add tests that fail if a merge or an expansion leaks into the returned shape. **This is still the rollback-bounding step** --- everything else in Channel C is churnable behind it, and a crude implementation will break it by convenience because the nested contents are already in hand. ~~**Depends on CC0**, which is what gives it a type to assert against.~~ **Re-pointed 2026-08-13: depends on CC0b**, the step that actually declares the type --- CC0 settles the decisions, and this step needs a declaration to assert against rather than an agenda.

Wire the referent path to consult it.** Candidate-pool construction in [`resolveCatalogSpanToPool.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/resolveCatalogSpanToPool.ts). **Preserve the exact-unique-match short-circuit** --- it is why the common commands never reach the LLM, and it is the reason residency is the hot axis. **Added 2026-08-13 with CC1c's reversal: the embedding fetch inverts against the cache rebuild, and this step owns the inversion.** Today [`handleParseRequested`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/index.ts) builds the room and held-inventory catalogs, derives `catalogObjectIds` from them, and *then* batch-fetches embeddings. Once the cache is the source of referrable nodes it determines that id set, so the order becomes **rebuild -> batched `ObjectEmbedding.get` -> `attachEmbeddings`**. **This is a plumbing change at ingress, not inside the rebuild** --- CC1 stays pure and embedding-free --- but it is a real edit to a shipped hot path and it is named here rather than discovered while wiring. **Note the two catalogs do not have the same host kind:** the room catalog is a Room host and held inventory is a Character host, while the cache is keyed per host --- so a single ingress fetch today spans what will be **two** cache rebuilds, and this step must decide whether the embedding batch stays unioned across both (preserving one round trip) or splits. **Take the union unless something forbids it**; splitting a batched read into two to match a structural boundary is cost paid for tidiness.

See **AH-12**.
  - [ ] **CC5. Build and test clause 4's fall-through deliberately.** Under an exhaustive cache **nothing ever misses**, so this path is dead code that will first run on the day the cache stops being exhaustive. Write tests that **force** a miss the ordinary path cannot produce. **Added 2026-08-13 with CC0's terminal decision: terminal *semantics* need the same forcing, and for the same reason.** A port terminal means **not consolidated here**, and is positive evidence that something further probably exists --- never *nothing further exists*. This is [clause 4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-five-clauses)'s *a miss is never an answer of "no"* extended from lookups to terminals. **Under exhaustive fill no terminal is ever produced**, so nothing exercises the difference and a consumer that reads a terminal as an absence would pass every test. **Force a non-consolidated host and assert the fall-through**, alongside the miss tests. **The asymmetry that makes this worth a test rather than a comment:** a graph walk's stop is the caller's own decision, per-request and recorded nowhere, so the caller knows why it stopped; **the cache's stop is durable data read later by a consumer who does not know why** --- the same construct, but the cache turns a transient choice into a fact on disk.

Convergence property test, once CC8 exists.** Cold rebuild (CC1) and warm incremental (CC8) must reach the same steady state. **Nearly free here because both implementations exist**, and it is what keeps [clause 5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-five-clauses) intact.

  - [X] **The parts-and-ports back-sweep.** **Executed 2026-08-10 --- [all three tranches](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), 64 rows, 36 implicated.** Re-read every open row against **locked-frame clauses 1 and 2** --- *a whole has its own graph with a root node*, and *boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns* ([Locked frame: parts and ports](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06), 2026-08-06) --- and record what falls out. **This is the retroactive half of [the dependent-set discipline](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09):** locked-frame clause 3's dependents were enumerated at lock time and locked-frame clauses 1 and 2's cannot be, because *"the reasoning that shows which one it is exists while the answer is being written and is gone afterwards."* **So this is a re-read, not a reconstruction**, and it should not produce a list claiming to be the set that was never captured. **Expect it to be productive rather than tidy-up:** the one session that swept rows against the whole/part dissolution found **six already-decided-but-unrecognized out of roughly eight examined**, three or four of them invalidated by a locked clause on the day it landed. **Named on the lock's section rather than its clause numbers** because `clause 1` is ambiguous across at least three constructs in this plan.
  - [ ] **Count the `H1-dependent` set and record the number**, per [Working hypotheses](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#working-hypotheses). This is the point at which the set is finally known, and the count is the measurement the tagging exists to produce: **small** means H1 was cheap and most of the design stands without it; **large** means it is load-bearing and deserves more scrutiny than a hypothesis has had. Either result is informative; an untagged or over-tagged set produces neither.

Optionally folds in Finding 5's port-preservation for `defer`-classified edges (H3 clause 5), still unimplemented anywhere. **Gated on AB-3's root concept landing** --- **which is now a scheduled step with a name: [LP4a](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#recommended-order) of the ludicGraph ports plan, which adds `rootId` and collapses `CarryClosureFragment` into it** (noted 2026-08-17; the parked table's *root concept* row is the same item) --- **and on [LP4c](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#recommended-order), added 2026-08-17 by [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up)'s Finding 6:** candidate (c)'s independence criterion is *reduce the room-side attachment to exactly one `In` edge*, so the slice cannot be built before the containment kinds exist. **Both gates updated 2026-08-19, and this slice is closer to startable than the text above implies.** (i) **The AB-3/root gate is discharged: LP4a landed 2026-08-19** --- `rootId` shipped and `CarryClosureFragment` was collapsed into `EphemeraLudicGraph`, so `computeCarryClosure` already returns a rooted graph rather than a bespoke fragment; the sentence above still describes it as a scheduled step. ---

## Why this initiative exists

The shipped position model has exactly one granularity: the human-convenience scale of Object and Character nodes on a host graph, related by peer-to-peer edges (`On`, `Under`, `Against`, `Custom`) within a single host. That model is a fine starting point and is not in question. What is in question is whether it can carry complexity **without becoming unworkable to reason about** --- for an LLM in particular, but the burden is not only the LLM's.

The gap is structural, not ergonomic. **Membership is the only level-crossing relation the model has, and it is flat**: a node is in a host or it is not, with no way to be in it *as part of something*. Every other relation is intra-host by construction --- [`applyStepSequenceCore`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/applyStepSequenceCore.ts) derives a relational step's shared host live and **throws** on mismatch.

**Membership is not, however, single-host at the storage layer** --- a point that was overstated in an earlier draft of this file and is worth stating precisely, since AB-8 turns on it. See [Multi-host presence: what is actually true today](#multi-host-presence-what-is-actually-true-today) below.

So there is no way to say "these five things together are a thing," or "this thing has parts," or "this thing extends across those rooms," except by encoding it in a vocabulary that has no levels in it --- which pushes the level structure into every consumer's head, to be re-derived by inspection each time. That is the burden.

### The four seed examples

These are the origin cases, recorded verbatim in intent because the corpus in Phase 0 is built *from* them and must not lose their variety:

1. **Place setting.** Plate, cup, fork, knife, spoon `On` a table. At some point it should be possible to reason about and display "table with a place setting" rather than six nodes and five edges.
2. **Dismantled flashlight.** Improvise "parts" --- battery, bulb --- at a level *below* the object, on demand.
3. **Powered door.** A door that opens only when power is on, where power is an area-wide fact affected by repairs to a generator elsewhere. Demonstrates that abstractions likely convey **state** as well as position.
4. **Rope through several rooms.** Pulling it is mediated not by an individual room but by an Area or by an abstraction.

### They are probably not one structure

The examples separate along at least three axes, and conflating them is the main design risk this plan exists to manage:

| Axis | Seed examples | Character |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Composition (part/whole)** | 1, 2 | Level-crossing, rooted, spatially coherent. The two examples are mirror images of **one** relation --- the difference is only which level pre-existed and which was improvised into being. Place setting: members primary, whole minted. Flashlight: whole primary, parts minted. |
| **Functional aggregate carrying state** | 3 | Members deliberately **not** co-located; membership is participation in a system, not containment. Carries state; the state gates behavior elsewhere. |
| **Multi-host extent** | 4 | One thing present across several hosts --- **representable today**, contrary to an earlier draft; see [Multi-host presence](#multi-host-presence-what-is-actually-true-today). Distinguished by needing **adjacency-mediated connections** among its parts (AB-26 --- *not* a path ordering; see that row), and by making the aggregate a candidate **manipulation host** peer to Room and Character. |

**Do not assume these collapse into one `Abstraction` type, and do not assume they stay separate.** Phase 1 decides it, against the corpus. Everything downstream reads differently depending on the answer, which is exactly why it is the root question.

### Clean fractal, dirty implementation

**Named 2026-08-06, alongside the *Abstraction Fractal* candidate on AB-0.** The fractal framing promises **scale-invariance** --- the same part-of relation at every level, no privileged one --- and the corpus does not fully deliver it. That is not one problem but two, and conflating them would either discard a good model or excuse a real gap.

| | What it is | How to treat it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Implementation dirt** | The clean model holds, and the implementation deviates from it deliberately, for efficiency: human-scale anchoring, caches, fast paths, shorthands | **Expected and legitimate.** Keep the clean fractal as the *specification of correctness*, and enumerate each deviation as an optimisation over it. A deviation that cannot be stated as "the clean answer, computed faster" is not dirt --- it is a model change wearing dirt's clothing |
| **Conceptual limit** | The clean model itself is not scale-invariant, independent of any implementation | **A real limit on the metaphor**, which the name must not paper over |
| **Scale-relative truth** *(added 2026-08-06)* | The model gives **different** answers at different levels and **both are correct** --- the coarse one is not an approximation of the fine one | **Neither dirt nor limit --- this is what scale-invariance should mean.** Requires only that the answers be *consistent*, never that they be the same. A deviation qualifies only if the coarse answer is right at the level read; if it is a lossy stand-in for a finer truth, it is dirt |

**The third row exists because the first two presumed a shape the design has since outgrown.** As originally written, the table admits only *one true answer, plus caches over it* --- so anything level-dependent had to be filed as an optimisation or as a failure of the metaphor. **[Port coarsening](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06) is neither.** A reader at the room level sees "the rope is tied to the boulder"; the interior knows which end. The coarse fact is not a cheaper version of the fine one and not a break in the fractal --- it is the answer at that scale, and it is **lossless at the level read**. **This is the second time this section has been short an outcome** (the first was AB-16, where the missing answer was "no difference"), which is worth noting as a pattern: a two-column classification invites forcing, and the forced answer has been wrong both times.

**Already-known instances of each.** *Dirt:* AB-15's default binding level is exactly human-scale anchoring --- the ontology privileges no level, the **pragmatics** privilege the authored one; and the render cache is a literal cache over the clean answer, so this is not hypothetical. *Conceptual limit:* **AB-13's DAG.** Multiple parents means the structure is not a tree-like recursion, and no amount of implementation care makes it one --- that is a property of composition, not of storage. ~~**AB-16 is unclassified** and worth deciding deliberately.~~ **Classified and closed 2026-08-06: neither.** Homogeneous extent versus heterogeneous separable parts is **not a break in scale-invariance and not an implementation convenience** --- it is not a distinction the position model draws at all. See the retired **AB-16**. Kept here because the *question* was the right one to ask; the answer just came back "no difference," which this section had not offered as a third outcome and should have.

**A worked run of the test, added 2026-08-09, and the first time it has been used to *decide* something rather than to classify something already decided.** `EphemeraPositionGraphData.hostId` splits under it: as **"which node in `nodes` is the root"** it is textbook dirt --- the clean answer is derivable by traversal (the unique node with no incoming containment edge) and storing it computes that faster --- while as **"which collection this graph belongs to"** it is drift, [P2's phantom edge](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge), an untypeable link with no node to be an endpoint. **One field, two jobs, opposite verdicts**, which is P2's own *one construct doing two jobs* diagnosis arriving a second time one level down. **Two things this establishes about the test itself, beyond the instance.** (1) ***Deliberate* is not *dirt*.** [AB-33](#open-decisions-design--plan-only) offered the adjacency row's missing third column as *"evidence that this was a chosen shape rather than an oversight"* and treated that as settling the question. It does not: the frame's whole point is that a deviation can be perfectly deliberate and still be a model change wearing dirt's clothing. **A deliberateness argument is not a dirt argument**, and the two are easy to swap because both sound like *someone thought about this*. (2) **The unit of the test is the job, not the construct.** Asking "is `hostId` dirt" has no answer; asking it of each job the field does has two, and they differ. **Apply the test per job wherever a construct is suspected of doing more than one.**

**Why the split earns its keep:** it lets the clean model be held as correctness while the implementation is permitted to deviate, with every deviation named as an optimisation rather than quietly absorbed into the model. The failure mode it guards against is the opposite --- efficiency shortcuts accumulating until nobody can say what the model *was*, which is the state the position lane's four routes reached with single-host (see [C2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c2-the-rope-in-two-rooms-refused-at-the-gate)).

### Fiction is a fault-tolerant medium --- and what that does not excuse (2026-08-09)

**Recorded from conversation as a standing frame, not as a clause inside the proposal that prompted it.** It already has three consumers --- [coarsening](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation), [P4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#two-defects-inherited-with-the-model)'s glitch policy (**PS-5** offers *accept transient wrong values* as a live option), and [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#licence-transfer-import-is-a-copy-and-provenance-is-deliberately-not-traced-2026-08-09)'s no-cascade decision --- which is the test for a frame rather than a local argument.

**The claim.** Reasoning over coarse summaries can produce errors and that is accepted. The same tolerance applies more broadly: **some player-visible contradiction is fine.** We are telling a story, not assembling a logical proof. This is **a property of the medium, not a concession extracted from it** --- audiences of long-form fiction do active repair work, tolerate continuity errors in enormous quantity, and frequently invent explanations more interesting than the consistency would have been. A tabletop GM contradicting themselves and moving on is not failing at the job; it is the job.

**And the alternatives are worse, which is the argument that actually decides it.** Guaranteeing coherence requires one of: refusing to improvise (generic worlds), tracing every derivation (unscalable --- the cost P5 declined), or blocking reads until everything settles (unresponsive). **Each costs more than an occasional napkin**, and the first costs the thing this initiative exists to provide.

#### What a *fault* even is, given the medium (2026-08-09)

**The paragraphs above still smuggle in a software instinct, and this corrects it.** A fault here is **not** *the system produced the wrong fiction* --- **there is no wrong fiction.** Any napkin is as good as any other napkin; no output is incorrect by failing to match a truth that existed before someone wrote it. **A fault is something that strains the integrity of the story.**

**Two consequences, and the second is why this is worth restating rather than filing as philosophy.**

1. **Accuracy is the wrong standard, so validating against truth is the wrong mechanism.** There is nothing to validate against. What *can* be checked is **consistency with what the fiction has already committed to** --- a different and far smaller obligation than correctness, and one that suggests different machinery.
2. **Strain is a matter of degree, and it tracks *salience*.** This is the discerning lens the fault-tolerance argument was missing. If nobody mentioned the napkins, contradicting them costs nothing. If the scene was *about* the tavern's poverty, the same contradiction is expensive. **Same fact, same contradiction, completely different cost.**

~~**And nothing structural distinguishes the two cases**, which is exactly why it cannot be read off the graph.~~ **Withdrawn 2026-08-09, challenged the same day it was written, and the correction is more useful than the claim.** **A scene about the tavern's poverty goes hand in hand with minting licence restrictions** --- under [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), poverty *is* the closing of a licence to improvise abundance. The incidental case has no record at all, only the unbounded default. **So salience does leave structural traces, and the primary trace is licence being *touched*.**

**Stated as a correlate rather than an identity, because it is lossy in both directions:**

| | Example | Why it breaks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Salient, licence untouched** | A scene about the tavern's *warmth and generosity* | Contradicting it (a surly innkeeper) strains badly with no narrowing to detect. **Partly repaired by reading licence *change* rather than *narrowing*** --- generosity plausibly **widens** --- but tone that changes no licence at all still exists |
| **Licence touched, not salient** | [C19](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c19-filling-out-the-place-setting-and-the-tavern-that-ran-out-of-napkins) beat 4's throwaway stock question | Narrows the tavern's licence and matters to nobody. A detector keying on narrowing alone would over-report |

**Three candidate structural signals, none assessed:** whether licence was touched at all; whether a change belongs to a **cluster** of related changes (poverty narrows napkins *and* ale *and* linens; an incidental answer narrows one), which is the most promising and the cheapest to compute; and **heat at the moment of establishment**, recorded on the change.

**The consequence worth chasing, flagged as speculative:** if a place's *character* --- poor, lavish, decayed, orderly --- is represented largely as the shape of its licence, then **licence is the generative half of setting character**: positive detail is what is there, licence is what the place will produce when asked. That would make P5 considerably more central than *a guard on improvisation*, and it is not what the proposal currently claims. **Not asserted, and it should be tested before P5 is leaned on for it.**

**So the damping rule sharpens from *damp faults* to *damp faults in proportion to salience*.** Global consistency is not the target and never was; consistency **where the story has invested** is. **And per the correction above, "where the story has invested" is partly readable from where licence has been touched** --- which makes the rule cheaper to act on than a purely judgment-based salience would be.

**And that is a third job for [narrative heat](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#narrative-heat-the-optimization-that-is-probably-not-only-an-optimization).** Salience is close to what heat already measures --- what the fiction and its players are attending to. **[PS-4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) asks whether heat is one artifact or two; this makes it potentially three**, and that row's own warning applies unchanged: **do not assume the cheap, approximate scheduling heat can carry a correctness-adjacent consumer.** Recorded as feeding PS-4, not as answering it.

#### The line this produces, and a terminology collision to fix rather than absorb

***Integrity* is now doing two jobs in this document, and they are not the same job.** The user-facing sense above --- *the integrity of the story* --- is the broad one and the more natural English. The sense used elsewhere in this plan is structural. **Qualify both rather than letting context disambiguate**, since a reader hitting the bare word will pick whichever meaning their background supplies:

| Term | What it covers | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Narrative integrity** | **Whether participant contributions are reincorporated** --- see the definition below, which narrows this considerably from *whether the story holds together* | **The measure.** This is what a fault strains, and it is a matter of degree |
| **Structural well-formedness** | One referent per thing, membership matching adjacency, no orphans, no double-mints, no dangling edges | **Non-negotiable**, and see below for why the unified measure does not soften it |
| **Coherence** | Whether the established facts contradict each other | **One component of narrative integrity, and usually the cheapest.** Not a synonym for it |

**Why structural well-formedness stays non-negotiable under a single graded measure.** A dangling edge nobody traverses strains nothing *today* --- so on the measure alone it would be tolerable. It is not, because **structural defects are latent strain: they cash out later, unpredictably, and usually as the world failing to behave like a world**, which is the most expensive kind. Two place settings where there was one is not a charming inconsistency; it is the reality illusion breaking, and everything else rests on that illusion. **Treat structural defects as high-strain-with-a-delay rather than as a separate category exempt from the measure.**

**P5's no-cascade decision spends *coherence* and preserves *structural well-formedness* entirely, which is exactly why it is safe** --- the napkin is one napkin, correctly keyed and correctly placed; only its provenance sits in tension with a fact about the storeroom that no scene had leaned on.

#### Narrative integrity is in service to reincorporation (2026-08-09)

**A narrower definition than *whether the story holds together*, and it is the one to build against.** In a story game, narrative integrity serves the **reincorporation of participant contributions** --- the improv discipline (Keith Johnstone, *Impro*) of taking what a participant introduced and bringing it back so that having contributed **mattered**. **Note the lineage:** almost all of this plan's prior art is computational --- dataflow, truth maintenance, importance sampling, capability attenuation. **This is the first load-bearing frame taken from theatre craft**, which for a fiction system is arguably where it should have come from first.

**What the narrowing buys, on the case that motivated it.** A tavern described as well-stocked one day and poorly stocked the next is, technically, a major consistency violation. **In story-gaming terms it is close to unimportant** --- if nobody put anything into either state, no contribution is betrayed by either. **The violation that matters is when the contradicted state was (a) *authored* as well-stocked, or (b) *worked for in play* by players who hauled supplies in.** Same contradiction, and the difference is entirely who invested in it.

**So this supersedes salience as the criterion, and demotes it to a proxy.** **Salience measures attention; contribution measures investment**, and investment is what integrity protects. The two usually agree and come apart in both directions: a player's throwaway remark that their character hates cats is low-salience and *high*-value to reincorporate, while a system-improvised atmospheric detail a scene happened to dwell on is high-salience and nobody's contribution.

**And it replaces the structural signal proposed above with a better one.** [The licence-clustering heuristic](#what-a-fault-even-is-given-the-medium-2026-08-09) was a guess at salience. **Contribution has a structural trace that already exists and is exact rather than heuristic:** the three-way split of **authored** (assets and WML, recorded in the participation stack), **player-driven** (actions routed through the manipulation kernel, producing committed step sequences and events), and **system-improvised** (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION`). **That split is already in storage**, and it maps directly onto how much a contradiction costs. Prefer it to clustering.

**Which makes this the *third* dent in one claim, and three is enough to restate rather than patch.** AB-14 holds that *"`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, so provenance is invisible downstream."* [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about) Finding 7 dented it, [AB-45](#open-decisions-design--plan-only) dented it again, and now the weighting of narrative integrity depends on it. **The accurate statement is narrower: provenance is invisible in the *merged view*, and fully recoverable from storage.** Those were being conflated, and only the first was ever argued.

**The division of labour this produces is cleaner than either rule alone.** Reincorporation covers authored and player commitments, tone and genre (the author contributed those too), and stakes. **Structural well-formedness covers the reality illusion** --- two place settings where there was one is not best described as a contribution violation. **Together they cover the ground; neither does alone.**

**What it argues for, at some future point: narrative weight carried alongside the permissive structures.** A licence record would carry not only *what* was established but **how much investment stands behind it** --- authored, worked-for-in-play, or improvised in passing. That gives the damping rule a real dial: protect high-weight commitments, let low-weight ones drift. **And it composes with P5's no-cascade decision rather than fighting it, for a specific reason: weight is a *value carried on the record*, not a pointer to its source.** Copy, not reference --- the same discipline [licence transfer](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#licence-transfer-import-is-a-copy-and-provenance-is-deliberately-not-traced-2026-08-09) already adopted. Tracked as **AB-46**.

**Recorded with its negative statement, on [AB-0](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s precedent**, because *fiction is fault-tolerant* is a sentence that will be quoted to excuse real defects:

0. **"There is no wrong fiction" does not mean anything goes.** It **relocates** the standard, it does not remove it: the measure becomes *strain on narrative integrity* rather than *accuracy against a truth*. A horror scene interrupted by a knock-knock joke is not defensible on the grounds that no fiction is wrong --- it is high strain, and the framing says so. **This clause exists because *no wrong fiction* is quotable in exactly the way the section title is**, and both need their limits attached.
1. **It does not excuse structural defects.** Two place settings where there should be one is not a charming inconsistency; it is the reality illusion breaking, and AB-21 exists for it. See *latent strain* above.
2. **It does not excuse a contradiction a player *acted on*.** Reasoning *there are no napkins, so I will use my handkerchief* and then finding a napkin is **agency undermined**, not flavour. **This is why re-open triggers belong at *consequence*, not at visibility** --- a contradiction nobody built on is cheap; one that invalidated a decision is not.
3. **It does not obviously extend to LLM context, and that is open rather than settled.** The tolerant audience is a *reader*. A reasoning step is a **second consumer with different tolerance**, and contradictory context does not produce a charming inconsistency --- it may produce a confidently wrong plan. How much this matters is unmeasured; it should not be assumed benign because the human half is.

**The general rule for triggers, applied to P5 and to be applied to PS-5:** set them at **consequence**, not at detectability. Theoretical incoherence, and even noticed incoherence, is inside the tolerance. Incoherence that broke something is not.

#### The feedback regime is the thing to measure, and it decides which solutions to look for (2026-08-09)

**Stated in conversation as a principle for the system generally, not only for this plan**, and it is the sharper half of the section above. Point 3 above says LLM tolerance is unmeasured. **The measurement that matters is not a rate --- it is a *regime*.**

| Regime | Behaviour | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Negative feedback / damping** | Minor faults tend to be corrected, absorbed, or papered over as the story continues | **Fine.** Fault tolerance is real and the medium does the work |
| **Positive feedback / amplifying** | Faults spawn progressively larger and more consequential faults | **Problematic**, and no amount of per-fault tolerance saves it |

**The design principle that follows, and it is the load-bearing sentence:** **look for solutions that promote the damping regime, rather than solutions that leave a positive feedback cycle in place and try to prevent error from ever occurring.** Prevention is the instinctive move and it is the wrong tool here --- an LLM in the loop *will* produce mildly incoherent details, and a system built to stop that costs the improvisation that is the point.

**This maps onto the line above, and the mapping is the practical form of the rule:**

- **Structural well-formedness -> prevention.** Gates, asserts, invariants. `removeObject`'s assert-and-throw, classify-before-legal, the compile gates. **Correct discipline, keep it** --- and note prevention is affordable *there* precisely because well-formedness has a definition to check against, which narrative integrity does not.
- **Coherence, and narrative integrity generally -> damping.** Prevention is unavailable (nothing to validate against) and unaffordable; the question is whether faults attenuate as they travel, **weighted by salience**.

**What damps, including two arguments for existing mechanisms that had not been made:**

1. **Narration entrenches, and entrenchment is *stabilizing* here.** Once a detail is narrated it is canon, and both the player and later reasoning build on it. In a database an error becoming permanent is alarming; **in fiction it converts a fault into shared truth and the divergence stops.** This is the *papering over* half, and it is a mechanism rather than a hope.
2. **Coarsening is a low-pass filter.** A fault in fine detail may simply not survive summarization to the next step. **[The port as summarization seam](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#a-third-argument-on-a-third-axis-the-port-is-the-summarization-seam-2026-08-07) gains a fourth argument on a fourth axis: it is a fault-damping seam.** **With a real caveat:** [C12 Finding 3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-3-coarsening-is-truth-preserving-on-containment-and-truth-destroying-on-causality) found coarsening *invents* conclusions on causality, so it damps detail-faults and can amplify causal ones. Not uniformly a filter.
3. **Bounded context is fault isolation.** This initiative exists to hand a reasoning step the right-sized packet. A fault outside the packet cannot influence it. **The scalability thesis and the stability thesis are the same mechanism, which is a second payoff nobody was charging for.**
4. **Players repair.** They notice, ask, object, and incorporate --- free correction, concentrated exactly where attention is, which is also where [heat](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#narrative-heat-the-optimization-that-is-probably-not-only-an-optimization) says consequence concentrates.

**What amplifies --- and the first one is this architecture's own commitment, which is why it is listed first:**

1. **Summaries of summaries.** The fractal asserts **no privileged level**, so arbitrary-depth summarization chains are structurally normal --- [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s four-level chain is the shape. Each hop is lossy and occasionally inventive, and drift compounds across hops. **This is the concrete positive-feedback risk the Abstraction Fractal buys along with its scale-invariance**, and it belongs in AB-0's departures list rather than being discovered later.
2. **Causal cascades with cycles.** [P4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#two-defects-inherited-with-the-model) already flags oscillation on cycles as a known cost. **A fault entering a cycle is not merely oscillation --- it is amplification**, and the guard P4 wants for termination is not the same guard fault-amplification needs.
3. **Licence widened on improvised grounds.** [C19](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c19-filling-out-the-place-setting-and-the-tavern-that-ran-out-of-napkins)'s import supplies an intensional description that unlocks further improvisation. **If the description supplying it was itself improvised, invention compounds** --- recorded honestly because it lands on a mechanism designed the same day, and because P5's no-cascade decision means nothing walks back an over-wide licence.

**Not a sixth axis, and the distinction is not pedantic.** [The five axes](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to) ask *what does the graph buy, and for whom*. This asks *how does a proposed mechanism behave under perturbation* --- a **dynamics** question about a design, not a value it serves. [State was declined as a sixth axis](#considered-and-declined-state-as-a-sixth-axis-2026-08-07) for a different reason; this is declined because it is a different **kind** of question, and folding it in would make the axis tally incoherent.

**What to instrument, since this is a measurement claim.** [P4 iteration 1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) is already committed to being *built to measure* --- fan-out, diamond frequency, settle time. **Add a fourth: fault propagation.** Direct measurement is hard; the proxies are not. How often does a later reasoning step **contradict** an earlier established fact? How deep do summarization chains actually run in play? Does contradiction frequency **rise** with world age --- which is the regime question stated as something countable?

**A graduation candidate, deliberately not graduated.** This is a claim about how to build the system generally, not about position graphs, so it will outlive this initiative. **Nothing goes to a durable doc until it ships** ([`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md)), and nothing here is built --- so it stays, flagged, and should be revisited for graduation when P4 iteration 1 returns its first measurements.

### Multi-host presence: what is actually true today

Verified against the code 2026-08-05, because the rope case (AB-8) had been written up as breaking a hard invariant, and that is not accurate. **Multi-room object presence is representable today with no schema change.** What stands in the rope's way is narrower and more specific.

| Layer | Reality |
| --- | --- |
| **Adjacency storage** | Natively multi-host. `getMembershipContainers` returns an **array**; adjacency rows are `(OBJECT#, POSITION#ROOM#...)` with nothing unique-constraining them. `MembershipDiff.froms` is plural and [`AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md) explicitly permits `froms.length > 1`. |
| **Membership compile gate** | **The hardest pressure, and the earliest.** Corrected 2026-08-05 while mining C2 --- an earlier draft named the end-state planner as "the" pressure. [`complexityPreGates.ts:29`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/complexityPreGates.ts) returns `complexityClass: 'multiPresent'` for `containers.length > 1`, and `preGateOutcomeToTerminalError` maps it straight to player-facing error copy. A multi-present object is **refused before planning is reached**. |
| **End-state planner** | A second, softer pressure. [`computeEndStateRoomDiff`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/adapters/computeEndStateRoomDiff.ts) computes `froms = priorContainers.filter(!== to)`, so ordinary place / spawn / remove scrub every other room as **normal operation**, not as repair. A planner behavior, not an invariant. |
| **Relational compile** | A third, and different again: [`compileRelationalFromSkeleton.ts:168`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/compileRelationalFromSkeleton.ts) populates `hostByObjectId` only `if (containers.length === 1)`, so a multi-present object is **silently hostless** to Expansion rather than refused. |
| **Cross-host move route** | Bypasses the gate *and* the planner --- `executeObjectMove` grounds through the Synthesize executor from a host pair. So the constraint is **not one rule applied unevenly across routes; it is three different rules on three routes.** See [C2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c2-the-rope-in-two-rooms-refused-at-the-gate) for the full table. |
| **Kernel step shape** | `MutationKernelTransferStep` has `fromHostIds: ReadonlySet<...>` but a **singular `toHostId`**. There is no add-to-many primitive. Three-room presence needs three pure-add steps or a widened field. |
| **Relational edges** | Strictly intra-host regardless. A rope in room A cannot relate to a bollard in room B under any current shape. |
| **Presentation** | Affordance compose reads `graph.objectIds` per room, so a multi-room rope renders as a **whole object in each room** with no notion of partial presence. Likely the first thing a player would notice. |
| **Drift repair** | [`repairObjectPlacementDrift`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/membership/repairObjectPlacementDrift.ts) **has no production caller** --- only its own test. The `Object Placement Drift Finding` sweep that would feed it is deferred and does not exist in [`lambda/diagnostics/`](../../../../../lambda/diagnostics/). Nothing is eliminating multi-room presence at runtime. |

**A four-way disagreement to resolve deliberately, not silently** (three when first written; the compile gate is the fourth, added 2026-08-05). The durable docs currently assert the opposite of the intended design: [`AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md) says "**Steady state:** at most one room per `OBJECT#`; multi-room adjacency is drift," and [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md) says "Objects follow the same steady-state rule (nodes only)." **Storage** permits multi-host while the **membership compile gate** refuses it outright and the **relational** route silently ignores it; the stated design (2026-08-05) is that a rope should eventually be present in several rooms. Either the clause was scoped to **characters** and over-generalized to objects when object membership shipped, or the design moved and the docs did not. **Fix the docs as part of Phase 1**, whichever way it resolves --- an unwired repairer plus a contract clause forbidding what the design intends is exactly the kind of stale normative text that gets obeyed by a future reader who has no reason to doubt it.

### Improvisation is the objects lane's mechanism, generalized

Settled 2026-08-05, and it changes what several rows cost. **`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is not a flag on a special kind of object --- it is an asset layer in the participation stack.** Improvised bodies merge through composite `ComponentData` like any other layer, and spawn-time `DEFAULT` situation prose deliberately routes objects through the **same** `ensureAuthoredCatalog` render path Feature / Knowledge / Character use rather than a bespoke stub. So the mechanism's actual purpose is already the general one this initiative needs: **mint a component nobody authored, such that nothing downstream can tell the difference.** What it lacked was a name for that purpose. Full detail: [`objects/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/objects/AGENT.md).

**What arrives already built:** the four-way split (merge body / play meta / semantic embedding / placement), a two-step existence-then-placement sequence, the **S1** compensating delete, the orphan sweep and its delete-only repair, and a best-effort **LLM-in-the-loop discipline** (Bedrock embeddings and enrich-generated prose, both failure-tolerant and non-blocking) that AB-18's cost and latency policy should start from rather than re-derive.

**This sharpens AB-12 from a philosophical question into a cost question.** If Objects are already abstractions at every level, minting a battery or a place setting **is** minting an `OBJECT#` --- the lane needs no generalization at all, only use. The Room / Feature half is different in kind: every row key, module, and type in that lane is `OBJECT#`-shaped. The two bets therefore differ by roughly *no new storage* versus *a new storage shape*, which is far more decidable than the ontology argument.

**Three gaps, all bounded, all real:**

| Gap | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| **No composition relation** | Improvisation mints a **free-standing** object and places it in a room. A *part* needs the level-crossing edge as well --- so spawn+place becomes spawn+place+compose, or the compose **is** the place if AB-4 resolves part-of as a host relationship. Either way the S1 compensating-delete story needs revisiting, since it currently compensates a two-step sequence. |
| **Recognition fits less cleanly than Improvisation** | Improvisation creates a thing from nothing, so its existence rows are the whole story. Recognition creates an aggregate **over things that already exist**: its body is nearly empty and its **member set is the content**. The lane has no concept of a component whose identity is a set of other components. |
| **`EMBEDDING#IMPROMPTU` is `shortName`-derived** | Reasonable for a battery, questionable for "the place setting on the table." Whether an aggregate gets a semantic vector, and over what text, is undecided. |

**Naming consequence --- ~~carried as AB-0's concrete form~~ now its own row, **AB-38** (split out 2026-08-07 when AB-0's naming half locked).** If Improvisation names the *mechanism*, then Improvisation and Recognition are **not peers**: Recognition is a trigger that invokes the mechanism, and operational necessity is the other trigger. The word is currently doing both jobs, which is the same conflation AB-14 was restructured to remove. Two ways out, unresolved: **(a)** Improvisation and Recognition stay the two triggers and the shared mechanism takes its own name; **(b)** Improvisation names the mechanism and the downward trigger is renamed.

**One speculative consequence worth keeping.** Because `ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a *layer*, an improvised thing can later be **authored over**. A recognized snare trap could graduate into canon --- a real partial answer to AB-19's cost objection that an authored pattern vocabulary limits players to what someone anticipated.

### Coyote as prior art: bounded LLM reasoning that is not bounded structurally

Identified 2026-08-05 as the **existing first-iteration attempt** at reasoning about player-assembled contraptions --- deliberately flawed and ad hoc, and the closest thing to a working answer for AB-19. Read [`coyoteGame/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/coyoteGame/AGENT.md), [`coyoteGame/AGENT.tropes.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/coyoteGame/AGENT.tropes.md), and [`coyotePlanAffinities.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/coyotePlanAffinities.ts) before designing anything in this area.

**What it does, structurally:** a `CoyoteStagedObject` snapshot assembled from `positionGraph` + `Meta::Object` + the improvisation pair, grouped by room; each object carrying `tropeAffinities` (roles with graded `aptness`, plus `environmentAffordances` / `affordancesProvided`) written at **Acme enrich time**; then a multi-hop LLM pipeline (candidates -> planSelect -> narrativeBeats -> outcome) with structured parse between hops and an internal-only `gimmick` narrative spine.

**What generalizes --- keep these:**

| Pattern | Why it matters here |
| --- | --- |
| **Annotate at mint time, not at reasoning time** | One Bedrock call at object creation writes durable structure; the reasoning step then *consumes* it. This is the direct answer to AB-18's cost concern and the reason AB-22 exists. |
| **Closed structural vocabulary, open narrative content** | Roles and tropes are enumerable; `gimmick` is deliberately free text with no closed enum. Matches the standing rule that only genuinely enumerable vocabulary should be hardcoded. |
| **Graded `aptness`, not booleans** | Affordance is a matter of degree, and downstream hops can rank rather than filter. |
| **Multi-hop with structured parse between hops** | Each hop's output is validated JSON, so a later hop reasons over structure rather than prose. |
| **Internal-only reasoning handoff** | `gimmick` never reaches the client. Reasoning scaffolding and player-facing narration are separate artifacts. |

**What is ad hoc --- and it is precisely the gap this initiative would fill:**

- **There is no persistent assembly-level object anywhere in it.** Every prediction reconstructs "what did the player build" from a **flat bag** of staged objects plus per-object role affinities. The contraption is never a thing.
- **Context is scoped to rooms, not to the contraption** (`loadCoyoteRoomObjectsByRoom`, across all Coyote game rooms). The LLM receives everything staged, relevant or not.
- **Therefore its boundedness is circumstantial, not structural.** It comes from the world being tiny and the genre closed --- six tropes, a hardcoded `boulder | cactus | ...` affordance enum, a handful of rooms. Remove any of those and nothing bounds the context.
