## Locked frame: parts and ports (2026-08-06)

**A third status, distinct from both "open row" and "working hypothesis."** [P2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) and [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) were written up as proposals explicitly *not* promoted, because [Working hypotheses](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#working-hypotheses) permits only one hypothesis in flight --- two would make the dependent sets overlap and a row tagged twice cannot be rolled back as a unit. **Locking is not promotion.** It does not consume the hypothesis slot; it takes these out of the queue entirely, so the remaining questions --- H1 clause 1b above all --- are decided *inside* this frame rather than alongside it.

**Explicitly not `H3`.** Giving parts and ports hypothesis status would file them back into the rollback machinery, which is the opposite of the move. Nothing below is `H3-dependent`, no tag is minted, and [the tagging test](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) is untouched.

### What is locked, at the grain that survives

**Graduated to [`positions/AGENT.concepts.md` --- Wholes, parts, and ports](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) on 2026-08-07, and that is now the durable statement.** The two claims stay below because the rest of this plan argues from them, but the concepts entry is where a reader outside this initiative meets them, and where they survive this file's deletion. **Nothing went to `AGENT.contract.md`** --- none of this is shipped, and contract clauses for unbuilt behavior are the wishlist-normative-text anti-pattern [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md) names. The concepts tier accepts it because its gate is **stability, not shippedness**: it already carries a **Target mental model** section with a graduation rule, and its stated anti-pattern is *unresolved engineering*, which the exclusion list below deliberately keeps out.

**Three shape claims as of 2026-08-09** (two until then). None names a record format, an identifier scheme, or a hosting model.

1. **A whole has its own graph, with a root node.** Parts are nodes in it; part relations are edges in it.
2. **Boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns** --- not by direct addressing of interior nodes from outside.
3. **Every `positionGraph` has the same internal structure, whatever kind of host it belongs to.** Exactly **one** root node, **present in the graph's own node list** and therefore available as an endpoint of relations like any other node, with the graph carrying a **designation of which node that is**.

**Locked-frame clause 3 added 2026-08-09, and it is a generalization of locked-frame clause 1 rather than a new bet.** Locked-frame clause 1 fixed this shape for wholes; [whole and host are the same construct](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09), so locked-frame clause 3 says only that the shape does not vary by host kind --- Object, Room, Character, Area, Feature, or anything that later acquires a graph. **It is conditional on having a graph at all**, which is what keeps it cheap: it decides no inventory, so **AB-30**'s wall-as-edge candidate and `EphemeraMembershipHostId`'s `OBJECT# / AREA# later` roadmap are both untouched.

**What locked-frame clause 3 fixes and what it leaves to calibration.** *Fixed:* uniformity across host kinds; exactly one root; the root is a member of `nodes` rather than a scalar beside them; the root is relation-endpoint-capable; a designation exists. *Not fixed:* what the designating field is called or typed (`hostId` retained versus renamed to `rootId` --- see the obligation below), what id scheme roots use, and **what relation joins a root to its members**, which is a kind that does not exist and is **AB-48**.

**The negative statement, on AB-0's and the port scheme's precedent.** **A root node is not a privileged kind of node.** It is a node occupying the root position in one graph, and the same object is a root in its own graph and an ordinary member in its container's --- which is [whole and part are roles, not kinds](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09) restated at the node level. **Do not mint a root node type**, and do not build a rule that gives root nodes different behaviour, for the same reason no rule may give parts and wholes different behaviour: it would assign two behaviours to one object.

**Two obligations locked-frame clause 3 creates**, both in the PQ-10 category --- implementation work the lock produces rather than design questions it answers, and both must land **in** the change that introduces the root, never after:

| Obligation | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| **`EphemeraPositionGraphNode` needs a third tag** | It is `'Character' \| 'Object'` today, and a Room root can be neither. **This is the second independent arrival of that need** --- **AB-42** already found negatives-as-nodes want a third tag, and called it *"possibly the first thing this initiative has found that genuinely cannot be an Object."* **Note which way it cuts: both arrivals pressure AB-12's everything-is-an-Object reframe and neither threatens locked-frame clause 3**, because *every graph has a root* is strictly weaker than *everything is an Object*. **The lock survives that reframe failing** |
| **`hostId` re-typed, and probably renamed to `rootId`** | Today it points **out** of the graph, to the collection the graph belongs to; under locked-frame clause 3 it points **in**, at a member of `nodes`. [`CarryClosureFragment`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) already has the target shape and the target name --- `{ rootId, members, edges }` against `{ hostId, nodes, edges? }` --- so this is a re-typing with a worked precedent in shipped code, not a widening. See **AB-33** |

**Why these two are safe to fix while everything around them moves:**

| Ground | Where it is argued |
| --- | --- |
| The requirement is **hosting-independent** --- reaching *out* to a whole at a non-local level and reaching *in* to its parts are the same problem, and no hosting decision removes either | [The requirement, stated independently of any hosting model](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-requirement-stated-independently-of-any-hosting-model) |
| The root node has **three independent derivations** --- AB-3's pre-registered trigger fired from composition, from hosting, and from ports, on three separate lines of reasoning | [The root node is required, not incidental](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-root-node-is-required-not-incidental) |
| Locked-frame clause 2's payoff is **encapsulation, not traversal**, and it lands on the two hardest lifecycle rows: interior repartitioning stops being externally breaking | [The strongest argument for ports is not the traversal one](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-strongest-argument-for-ports-is-not-the-traversal-one) |

**Whole and part are roles, not kinds (added 2026-08-07 from [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)).** Locked-frame clause 1's wording invites a reading in which an object *is* a whole or *is* a part. It is both, relative to different levels, simultaneously --- a string is a part of a machine and a whole of its spans. **This is a foreclosed misreading, not a lifted clause:** C11 is representable by both clauses and the [re-open criterion](#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause) is not met. But the misreading is not hypothetical --- it produced [H1 clause 1a's falsification](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h1-is-a-conjunction-and-its-clauses-are-tested-separately) --- so **any rule that gives parts and wholes different behaviour is not a rule**, since it assigns two behaviours to one object. Carried into the [concepts entry](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports).

**Lock the scheme with its negative statement attached**, not the sketch alone. [What a port number is not](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) exists because the construct produced two misreadings of the same family within a day --- a reusable-interface-with-fan-out reading, then a port-number-as-part-identity reading, both of which propagated into assessments before being caught. **The design was stable before its durable statement was.** A future reader who takes locked-frame clause 2 without that section is likely to make the third.

### What is not locked, and must not ride in on this

**The lock is on shape. Everything below is calibration inside the shape, and every one of them is genuinely open.**

| Not locked | Why it stays out |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-12's second bet, part (b)** --- Room/Area/Feature as **levels in the part-of ladder**: rooms nesting in rooms, Area as a bigger Object, Feature as part-of-Room | ***The* exclusion locked-frame clause 3 exists alongside, and the one most likely to be read as included.** Added 2026-08-09 with the clause. AB-12's Room/Feature bet is two separable claims: **(a)** hosts and nodes unify --- a host is a node in its own graph --- and **(b)** Room/Area/Feature join the composition ladder. **Locked-frame clause 3 locks (a) and only (a).** (b) is where the **provenance seam** lives: the room boundary is an authored asset-stack merge, not a play-time graph mutation, and [the concepts entry's warning](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) about claims *earned for object interiors and aspirational above the room* is a warning about (b) --- so it survives the lock **intact and still needed**. **A uniform graph interior is not a uniform containment ladder**, and AB-12's own instruction (*do not let the second ride in on the first*) applies to this boundary too. **Status of (b) as of 2026-08-10, since "open" is now too coarse:** *Feature-as-part-of-Room* is in active play at **AB-30**; *Area-as-bigger-Object* is untouched (and note `Area -> Room` containment already exists, authored); *rooms-nesting-in-rooms* is **parked** --- schema-representable, modeller- and UI-unsupported, with improvisation **redirected** to mint a Feature rather than prohibited. See AB-12 for the gate and its unpark trigger. **Walled off explicitly 2026-08-16, on the user's call, because the tempting inference now exists:** all five membership host kinds (Room, Character, Object, Feature, Area) now have real `Meta::<Kind>.ludicGraph` storage. **That is an inventory fact and bears on (b) not at all.** Locked-frame clause 3 locks (a) --- hosts and nodes unify --- and storage breadth is (a) being implemented, nothing more. **(b) remains not-locked, and its provenance-seam argument survives the landing intact:** the room boundary is still an authored asset-stack merge rather than a play-time graph mutation, which is exactly what a uniform storage layer does not touch. *A uniform graph interior is not a uniform containment ladder*, and neither is a uniform serde. **Do not cite the host-storage work as evidence for (b) in either direction** |
| **Which things have a `positionGraph` at all** | Locked-frame clause 3 is **conditional** --- *whatever kind of host it belongs to* --- so it constrains graphs that exist and mints none. **AB-30**'s wall-as-edge candidate stays live, and ~~`EphemeraMembershipHostId`'s `OBJECT# / AREA# later` roadmap stays a roadmap~~ **--- superseded 2026-08-16: the roadmap landed.** `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is now `Room \| Character \| Object \| Feature \| Area`, and each of the five has real `Meta::<Kind>.ludicGraph` storage and cache-gateway support end to end (LP0, then the membership-host-kernel slices; rule now durable at [Host storage](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md#host-storage-one-shared-serde-one-documented-exception)). **This row's question is thereby answered for hosts and only for hosts.** **A reader concluding from locked-frame clause 3 that Features are hosts has read an inventory claim into a structure claim** --- and the converse now needs saying just as loudly, since the inventory claim has since become true: **that all five kinds host graphs is not evidence that all five are levels in a part-of ladder.** See the AB-12(b) row above, whose status this landing does **not** move |
| **What relation joins a root to its members** | Locked-frame clause 3 makes the root **available** as a relation endpoint and says nothing about what attaches to it. `HostRelationalEdgeKind` is `'On' \| 'Under' \| 'Against' \| 'Custom'` --- there is **no containment kind at all** --- so the vocabulary the clause needs does not exist yet. That is **AB-48**, which the lock makes urgent rather than answers, and it is the closest thing locked-frame clause 3 has to a precondition |
| **[PQ-4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)** --- port identity and reuse | AB-21's identity obligation one layer down; unchosen between monotonic allocation and keying by what is bound |
| **PQ-9** --- declared vs derived | Numbered-vs-named retired 2026-08-13, absorbed by [PQ-4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s opaque id. **The separator graduated to a decision 2026-08-17**, scoped to the serde/Dynamo string-form boundary --- see [PQ-9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) --- and is no longer calibration. Declared-vs-derived alone remains open; a shape lock says nothing about it |
| **PQ-13** --- does a plain object carry a presence port | The uniformity argument is good and the ceremony objection survives; deciding it by "well, ports are locked now" would be exactly the momentum this plan warned against. **Re-weighted 2026-08-07 by [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine): still open, but no longer calibration.** If a lantern is present by membership node and a rope by port, the level at which an object switches mechanisms *is* the whole/part line C11 dissolved --- so this is the **base case locked clause 1 rests on**, and it should be decided before anything downstream. **Axis reading, 2026-08-07:** positioning and apprehension are indifferent, mutation charges a small cost, and **reasoning wants one mechanism rather than two** --- which leans yes on a stronger basis than the ceremony objection opposes it. ~~A lean, not a decision~~ **Superseded 2026-08-09: forced yes by entailment, not by the balance of argument** --- see [the whole/part dissolution](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). This row named its own decider (*the level at which an object switches mechanisms **is** the whole/part line C11 dissolved*) and then went on weighing uniformity against ceremony; there is no category *plain object* to switch from |
| **PQ-8** --- where sibling links' licensing labels live | Ports do not help here and may make discovery worse |
| **PQ-12** --- must relocation follow adjacency | Widens AB-26 onto the write side; belongs to that row |
| **PQ-1's record split** --- ***the least locked part of ports*** | Marked settled **provisionally, after one reversal on the day it was made**, and its answer rests on the ingress list living in `positionCache` --- which is **AB-35**, unresolved. If the cache's derivation story comes out differently this reverses again. See [The port record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06). **That trigger fired 2026-08-12 and came back the other way: [P6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure) re-bases the cache as the *reference-location* structure, and the ingress list turns out to be reference data --- so this does *not* reverse a second time, and the row's standing improves.** *(P6's first form asserted the opposite and was corrected the same day.)* **The argument, in full at [Which axes want the ingress list](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#which-axes-want-the-ingress-list-and-why-pq-1-does-not-reverse-2026-08-12):** traversal never consults the list, because a room edge naming `ROPE:4` addresses that port directly; the list's only unique contribution is **ports no edge names**, and enumerating referrable things nothing else names is reference-location by definition. **Mutation reads egress plus membership and never this**, which also permanently kills the original *too hot to depend on cache warmth* objection. **What this does to the row: the 2026-08-06 decision was reached on a *storage* argument (weak 1:1 versus strong N:1) and is now independently confirmed by a *job* argument six days later.** Two arguments agreeing from different directions is a materially stronger footing than the single reversed-once storage claim that earned this the *least locked part of ports* label --- **but the label stays for now**, because the confirming argument comes from a proposal that is itself provisional and untested against the corpus |
| **PQ-10** --- the matcher fix | An implementation obligation the lock creates, not a design question it answers. Must land *in* the change that introduces ports, never after |

**The single-use property ([PQ-1's basis](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06)) and the scale-boundary reading ([each side stores its own kind](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06)) sit between the two lists.** They are not shape claims, but nothing currently open depends on their being wrong, and both deflated questions rather than adding them. **Treat them as locked-with-a-lower-bar:** re-openable on a case, not merely on a preference. **The bar has now been met once, 2026-08-10: [C22](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c22-the-ships-wheel-and-the-lighthouse) re-opens single-use as PQ-14.** **Re-opened is not withdrawn** --- the arguments for single-use stand and nothing has replaced it --- but the property is no longer *settled with a low bar*, it is an open question with a case attached. **The scale-boundary reading is untouched** by this and keeps its previous standing. **Worth noting how the bar behaved:** it was met by a **scalability** objection reachable under ordinary fiction, not by a representability failure --- which is a class this list did not anticipate when it wrote *"nothing currently open depends on their being wrong."* Something now does.

### Consequences accepted by locking, recorded so they are owned rather than rediscovered

**AB-6's option space changes rather than narrowing.** [PQ-2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) found that if a whole's ports enumerate its presence, presence is stored **in the whole** --- a carrier neither of AB-6's two framings (a stored member set, or a predicate derived over graph edges) contemplated. Locking puts a *third* option on that row's table, and it is the one that puts presence and member sets in the same carrier. The row still needs splitting; the split now has three candidates on the presence half, not two.

**AB-25 stays deflated, permanently rather than provisionally.** Sibling links living inside the whole's graph gave AB-26 a home *below* the rooms, which removed most of what Area was being summoned to do above them. That deflation is now a property of the frame. **PQ-8 is the acknowledged loose end in it** --- and it becomes a loose end in something fixed rather than in something still moving, which raises what it costs to be wrong about. Note AB-25 does **not** close: it remains H1's only live falsification trigger, and its character-as-interior-node widening is untouched by anything here.

**The thumb on the clause-1b scale must stay legible.** Ports were designed and priced under "assume multi-host for the moment," and [the motivation section](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-motivation-stated-plainly-2026-08-06) states plainly that the assumption is not neutral --- ports answered C7's where-along objection without single-hosting, which is one of the four pressures on 1b. **Locking does not add a fifth pressure and must not be read as adding one.** The risk is not that the argument is wrong; it is that a locked-in frame stops looking like an argument at all. Whoever decides 1b should re-read that section knowing ports are fixed, and weigh the pressure at exactly the weight it had before the lock --- no more.

### What the lock delivered for scale-invariance, and where it stops (assessed 2026-08-06)

**The question asked:** [Clean fractal, dirty implementation](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) recorded that the corpus does not fully deliver scale-invariance, and the first pass read it as **more conceptual than practical**. Re-assessed after the lock. **It is now practical below the room boundary and still conceptual above it**, and the two halves should not be reported as one verdict.

**What became practical:**

| Gain | Why it is practical rather than conceptual |
| --- | --- |
| **The recursion is in the type** | Before, a room's graph was a *shard* and an object was a *node* --- different kinds of thing, so a level-crossing walk changed representation as it went. Locked clause 1 makes a whole a graph **and** a node in another graph. `EphemeraPositionGraph` is the single recursive type; "the same relation at every level" stops being a claim about the model and becomes a property of the data |
| **The recursion is composable, not merely repeated** | A fractal with no re-entry construct cannot be traversed. [PQ-5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) answers yes to chaining and yes to a port being addressable as a part, so a rope in a box in a room resolves by the same hop taken twice --- with `computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has` as the cycle-guard precedent |
| **Invariance across *time*, not only across levels** | ***The largest gain, and it was not what the lock was bought for.*** A thing can change scale --- decompose, reabsorb --- without any external reference knowing what scale it was. AB-17 and AB-31 were failing precisely because external code was bound to a decomposition state. Level-uniform *reading* is the weaker property; this is the stronger one |
| **The clean/dirty discipline gained teeth** | It was a rule with no mechanism behind it, so nothing stopped a shortcut becoming a model change. Coarsening supplies the mechanism --- and in doing so exposed that the discipline was missing a category, now added as **scale-relative truth** |

**Where it stops. Three residues, none of them softened by the lock:**

1. **The fractal is invariant below the room and something else above it.** `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is `Room | Character`. The lock says *objects* are ported graphs; it says nothing about rooms, and **AB-25**'s Room/Area containment is a structure of different provenance --- authored asset-stack merge rather than play-time graph mutation. A walk upward from a battery still changes representational regime at the room boundary. **AB-25's character-as-interior-node widening sharpens this rather than softening it:** characters are hosts, an LCA of a room and a character is needed, and nothing says a character is a ported graph. **This is the next boundary that would have to be locked for the fractal claim to be general.** **Partially softened the same day by [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system):** a moonbase computer system ladders *above* human scale while remaining an `OBJECT#`, which suggests the fractal extends upward without Room/Area needing to be ported at all. **Read this residue with that case beside it** --- the room/area *provenance* problem is untouched, but "the fractal stops at the room" is stated more absolutely here than the corpus now supports.
2. **AB-13's DAG is untouched**, exactly as the clean/dirty table already classifies it. Chaining plus multiple parents makes traversal an unambiguous DAG walk rather than a tree recursion --- workable, and a permanent qualifier on the word *fractal*.
3. **Write-side scale change has no vocabulary at all.** One identity changing scale is now clean; one identity **becoming two** is undefined, and `divide`/`merge` are [handed to the ladder](../actions/AGENT.objectManipulationIterations.planning.md#deferred-not-rung-sized) and blocked on **AB-31**.

**And the base case is not decided.** [PQ-13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) is deliberately outside the lock, and it *is* the practical invariance test: does a lantern run the same code with zero hops? The uniformity argument there is the one that rescued [position 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) --- *a loop that sometimes iterates zero times is one mechanism* --- so it is likely to land, but until it does the bottom of the ladder is as open as the top.

**The risk the lock introduces, recorded so it is watched rather than discovered.** Encapsulation means external code cannot see what scale a thing is at --- which is exactly what invariance requires, and also exactly what would let a real level asymmetry go unremarked. **The room/area seam is now less visible, not less real.** The failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained. Residue 1 is that reader's warning, and it should survive into `AGENT.concepts.md` whenever this graduates. **Discharged 2026-08-07** --- it travels with the [concepts entry](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) as a required warning rather than waiting for Phase 6, since the entry is exactly what would otherwise be read as a uniformity claim.

### What would re-open a locked clause

Not a preference, not a cheaper-looking alternative, and not a rival proposal that happens to also work. **A case in the corpus that either clause cannot represent** --- the standard [C7 met](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) --- or a demonstration that locked-frame clause 2's encapsulation claim fails where it was bought, which means an interior repartitioning that still breaks external references. Either goes in [Discussion record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#discussion-record) with the case attached, and the lock is lifted by name rather than eroded by exception.

### Locks need a dependent set too (2026-08-09)

**An existing discipline pointed at a construct it was never aimed at.** [The tagging test](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) exists so a hypothesis's dependent rows are **known rather than rediscovered** --- `H1-dependent`, `H3-dependent`, `P4-i1-dependent`. **Locks have no equivalent, and locks are what have actually been invalidating rows.**

**The evidence, measured 2026-08-09 rather than asserted.** A single session's work across roughly eight rows found **six already-decided-but-unrecognized**: AB-42's *room-or-whole* fork, AB-32's non-discriminating decider, AB-4's record option, AB-4's endpoint-address horn, PQ-13's *lean not a decision*, and AB-33's entire framing. **Three or four of those were invalidated by a locked clause on the day it landed** and sat live for days afterward. That is not a tail of stragglers; it is most of what was examined.

**Why the asymmetry existed, and why it should not survive.** Tagging was built to make **rollback** cheap, and a lock is not expected to roll back --- so the machinery was never pointed here. But the dependent set answers the opposite question just as well: not *what do I revert if this falls* but ***what do I re-check now that this stands***. Same enumeration, opposite use, and the second use is the one that has been costing time.

**The mechanic, and it differs from hypothesis tagging in one deliberate way.** A hypothesis tags **the rows**, because when it dies you need to find them. **A lock should enumerate at the lock** --- one list, written where and when the knowledge exists, rather than N edits to rows the author is not currently reading. Rows move; a locked clause does not. **The resolved rows already point back**, so the pointer only needs to exist in one direction.

**Do it at lock time, because the [tagging test's own warning](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) applies here verbatim:** *"tagging retroactively does not work --- the reasoning that shows which one it is exists while the answer is being written and is gone afterwards."* That warning explains why locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 have no recoverable dependent set today and why today's sweep had to be done by reading every row that used a word.

**Locked-frame clause 3's dependent set, enumerated at lock time --- the first application.**

| Row | What locked-frame clause 3 does to it | State |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **AB-33** | Resolves it --- host-as-node, uniform across host kinds | Closed same day |
| **AB-12** | Splits its second bet: (a) locked, **(b) excluded and still open** | Updated same day |
| **AB-48** | Creates it --- the root needs a containment kind to be an endpoint of | Opened and closed same day |
| **AB-2** | Node and host stop being alternatives; *projection* was already AB-9's | **Closed 2026-08-09** --- and AB-30's *addressable but not a member* found surviving as a fourth answer the row never offered |
| **AB-3** | Discharges the root-node trigger --- five independent arrivals in the end | **Closed 2026-08-09** --- `CarryClosureFragment` turned out to be the precedent rather than the casualty |
| **AB-30** | Gives a wall's relation-target requirement a root to attach to | **Re-checked 2026-08-10** --- re-framed toward wall-as-`PartOf`; the edge candidate turned out unable to satisfy C5, which the row had never tested it against. Still open, gated on H3 clause 6 |
| **AB-42** | *Room or the whole* collapses to one slot | Corrected same day |
| **AB-13** | DAG multi-parenthood is across graphs, not multiple roots within one --- confirms single-root | Consistent, no change |

**Locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 get a sweep rather than a set**, since their reasoning is gone: the honest recovery is to re-read rows against them once, record what falls out, and not pretend a retroactive list is the same artifact. **That work has a name and a home: the *parts-and-ports back-sweep*, scheduled as a [Phase 3](#recommended-order) sub-step.** **Named on the lock's section rather than on its clause numbers deliberately** --- `clause 1` denotes at least three unrelated things in this plan (locked frame, H3, P2), and *[Locked frame: parts and ports](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06)* is a unique heading, so the name survives a cold read where *"clauses 1 and 2"* would not. ***Back*-sweep, not sweep**, to distinguish retroactive recovery from the forward dependent-set discipline this section otherwise describes.

**Two failure modes this does not catch, named so the mechanic is not oversold.** (i) **A proposal that retracts its own option mid-write-up** --- AB-4 cited P2's endpoint-address horn, which [P2 withdrew in its next section](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#where-p2-clause-3-risks-recreating-the-very-defect-it-fixes); rows cite proposals by summary, so the summary outlives the retraction. (ii) **A row malformed at birth** --- AB-33 was never invalidated by anything, it simply had no requirements twin and argued entirely on cost-of-change. **Neither is a staleness problem, and a dependent set would not have found either.**

## Parked extension points this initiative would unpark

**Framing (2026-08-05, from the author of most of these):** these were laid down deliberately, in anticipation of a wider-reaching reasoning effort that would eventually need them. **This is that effort.** So unparking is the *expected shape* of implementation here, not a cost to be minimized or a sign the slice is too big --- and a candidate slice that unparks nothing is more likely mis-aimed than cheap.

Every row below was verified in this repo during the design conversation. Each is a **deliberate** deferral with a stated trigger, not an oversight.

| Parked capability | Where it is recorded | What unparks it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`OBJECT#` / Area membership hosts** | [`positions/AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#fractal-ludic-graphs-container-scale-and-edges) --- "deferred until needed" | Composition, if part-of resolves as a host relationship (AB-4). Nesting is the case that makes them needed. |
| **A root concept on `EphemeraPositionGraph`** | [`ludicGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/AGENT.md#known-limitation-deferred) --- pinned in two places, with `CarryClosureFragment` set to collapse into it | AB-3. The reciprocal note exists precisely so whoever adds a root here finds it. |
| **Features as graph nodes** | Same --- node `tag` union is `'Character' \| 'Object'` only | AB-12's Room/Feature half. Note Features are designed as *narrower* participants, not Object peers. |
| **Character-relation widening (BD-36)** | Same --- `HostRelationalEdge` endpoints are object-only; `removeCharacter`'s assert is vacuously satisfied today | Relations over non-object nodes; the assert is the placeholder already installed for that day. |
| **Reverse edge-reference index / post-clear sweep** ***(most load-bearing --- three independent consumers)*** | Same --- adjacency indexes *node* placement, not edge-only references | Cross-host abstraction membership (AB-6, AB-8); part-connections referencing a licensing relation (AB-26); and the backwards discovery a licence-removing mutation needs (AB-29). Three separate lines of reasoning arrived here independently on 2026-08-05. |
| **Render-cache `n` / mark-state axis + `situationId`** | [`renderCache/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/renderCache/AGENT.md); pinned to `[]` and `SITUATION#DEFAULT` by iteration 10 | AB-23. Situational *meaning* is this axis raised from rendering to interpretation. |
| **`thinking` `claimed` status + a claimer** | [`thinking/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/thinking/AGENT.md); status shipped, no producer, retention already protects queued work | AB-18, AB-24 --- deferred recognition and the ambient context tier. |
| **`EMBEDDING#PERSPECTIVE#...` rows** | [`objects/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/objects/AGENT.md) Follow-ups --- "when perspective-scoped similarity is needed" | AB-23. Perspective-scoped semantic similarity *is* situational meaning, expressed in the embedding layer. |
| **WML `Relational` tag / `EdgeList` consolidation** | [`positions/AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wml-convergence-future) --- deferred pending heterogeneous room/container edge lists | AB-10, AB-15 --- authored composition patterns and authored interpretive frames. |
| **`Object Placement Drift Finding` sweep** | [`positions/AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md) --- deferred; `repairObjectPlacementDrift` has no caller | AB-8, and the contract-contradiction cleanup already queued in Phase 1. |
| **Backtrack channel (BD-18)** | [`AGENT.backtrackChannel.planning.md`](../actions/AGENT.backtrackChannel.planning.md) --- iteration 4, named not started | AB-20: an LLM that proposes a wrong recognition needs a structured critique path, not a silent guess. |
| **Moving an object into or out of another object's interior graph** | Nowhere yet --- newly named 2026-08-07. `MutationKernelTransferStep`'s singular `toHostId` and `applyStepSequenceCore` were built against ordinary hosts (rooms, characters); neither has been asked whether it can source from or target a node nested inside a whole's own `positionGraph` | **AB-40(a)**, from [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork). **Recognized, not designed:** this is not an open fork between candidate answers, it is a scoped capability H3 requires and today's kernel has never been asked to supply --- the ordinary "which of these is actually a decision" test this table's framing note applies. **Narrowed 2026-08-10 by AB-5's A-and-B framing:** an *ordinary* mint sources from a room host and does not need this; it is required for **nested** wholes and for C13's fork-taking, which is a smaller trigger than first stated. |

Mint is `transferMembership` **plus** `establishRelation` --- members move, edges do not move with them --- so the zero-new-step-kinds claim rests on this one verification. If it refuses, one step kind is added: a cost, not a shape change. **Recognized, not designed**, on the row above's test |

A mint's `toHostId` is the whole being created --- **knowable**, since the planner mints the id, but absent from every graph at snapshot time. Verify rather than assume: this is the one place the op's shape meets the kernel's hardest standing constraint |

**Use this table in Phase 5.** A slice's cost is substantially "which of these does it unpark, and is that one ready to be unparked" --- several have their own prerequisites, and at least one (the backtrack channel) is a sibling initiative that would need scheduling rather than absorbing.

### Shipped code this initiative would retire

**The mirror image of the table above --- not a capability to unpark, but one to decommission.** Named here so Phase 6 (graduate the slice) doesn't rediscover it from scratch.

| Shipped mechanism | Why it's a retirement candidate | What replaces it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Carry closure** --- `computeCarryClosure`, `classifyInteractionUnderTransfer`, `CarryClosureFragment` ([`interactionUnderTransfer.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts)) | Opened 2026-08-07 from [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up). Two sub-mechanisms, both independently found to already have H3-native equivalents: the BFS-walk-and-bundle half collapses into ephemeral-whole construction (**AB-41**); the boundary-edge classification half already reuses closely-related vocabulary (`dissolve`/`carry`/`defer` --- three outcomes, not the four this plan has been citing; see AB-28) to what ports' own boundary-edge discipline uses (AB-28's fifth independent arrival). **Not a parallel path that resembles the abstraction framework --- it is the abstraction framework, called before the framework existed.** | Ephemeral-abstraction construction (AB-41) for the bundling half; `boundaryEdgeOutcomes` directly for the classification half, once H3's whole/port machinery is what a transfer operation is built against |

**Do not act on this before H3 ships.** Carry closure is today's only working mechanism for this problem; retiring it is a Phase 6 consequence, not a prerequisite for anything earlier in this plan.

## Out of scope: parked capabilities and what unparking them would look like (2026-08-10)

**This section exists to stop a park from becoming debt *and* to stop this plan from becoming a roadmap.** A park needs enough of an exit sketch that it can be built *toward* rather than merely deferred --- but the moment those sketches become designs, this plan acquires responsibility for everything downstream of its own work, which is not a burden one task plan should carry.

**The rules that keep it small, and they are the point of the section rather than preamble:**

1. **Sketches, deliberately shallow.** An entry says *what would have to change and which lane owns it*. It does not design anything. If an entry starts acquiring open questions with IDs, it has outgrown this section and wants its own plan.
2. **Only capabilities this plan actively *parked*.** Not everything it enables, not everything it makes easier, not anything merely adjacent --- otherwise the list is unbounded by construction. **[Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) is the section for things this initiative *unparks*; this one is its mirror.**
3. **Each entry carries the constraints the park must respect to stay exitable.** That is the half a plain "parked, revisit later" note loses, and the half that actually costs something if it is not written down now.
4. **None of this is in scope.** No phase builds it, no row owns it, and an entry here is **not** a commitment that it will ever be built.

### Rooms nesting in rooms

**Parked at [AB-12(b)](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)** --- schema-representable, modeller- and UI-unsupported, improvisation redirected to mint a **Feature `PartOf` the room** rather than a nested Room. **Unpark trigger:** a case a Feature cannot carry, or [H3 clause 8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s apprehension scale being built for other reasons.

**What unparking would involve, and the shape of it is the reassuring part:**

| Piece | What changes | Lane |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Representation** | **Nothing.** Locked-frame clause 3 already makes a Room's graph structurally identical to any other host's, and a Room node inside a Room's node list is an ordinary containment edge | This one --- already done |
| **Existing sub-spaces** | Re-tag Feature -> Room. Same graph shape; the difference is host-kind union membership and what apprehension does with it | This one --- already done |
| **The gate** | Remove one labelled mint-site type check | This one --- one edit by construction |
| **Apprehension policy** | H3 clause 8's reserved field gets an answer for rooms: what a character in one sub-space perceives of another. **This is the actual cost of unparking** | Perception / H3 clause 8 |
| **Perception + projection** | Room-scale perception becomes sub-room-scale --- affordance compose, description, LLM context assembly | Perception lane |
| **Player-facing UI** | How a player sees, enters and navigates sub-spaces | Client |

**The result worth stating plainly: structurally the park is free to exit, and everything expensive about it belongs to other lanes.** Which means this was never really this plan's park --- it is a decision **not to hand a problem to the perception and client lanes** before they have reason to want it. That is also why it can be parked without guilt: nothing here is deferred *work of this initiative*.

**Three constraints the park must respect to stay exitable**, and these **are** in scope, because breaking them is how a cheap unpark becomes an expensive one:

1. **No code may assume a Room's graph contains no Rooms.** The park is a **policy**, not an invariant. `evaluateComplexityPreGates`' `multiPresent` error is the worked example of what ignoring this costs --- the schema always permitted multi-presence, some code decided it could not happen, and correcting it is now a five-site, player-visible obligation.
2. **The gate stays a single labelled site.** It is a type check at the mint point today; if it diffuses into scattered assumptions, the one-edit exit is gone and the exit becomes a survey.
3. **Feature sub-spaces stay structurally identical to Rooms.** Do not "optimise" Features into something narrower than a general host --- the re-tag exit depends entirely on them already being one, and this is the constraint most likely to be broken by someone with a good local reason.

### Measuring when to mint and dissolve an abstraction (2026-08-13)

**Parked at [AB-41](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s promotion half** --- *ephemeral to durable*, which [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up) routed to [P4's narrative heat](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#narrative-heat-the-optimization-that-is-probably-not-only-an-optimization) (iteration 3, not built) --- and touching **AB-5**, **AB-14**, **AB-18** and **AB-24**. **Unpark trigger: a built and iterated attention ledger**, per the sub-plan's [Channel B](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#recommended-order). **Not before.**

**The intuition, recorded because it is worth keeping and is not yet worth designing.** The [attention ledger](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md) answers *"how often, recently, would we have benefitted from this optimization?"* and lets `ludicCache` form and dissolve by measured demand rather than by a tuned policy. **The same shape plausibly underpins the *recognition* layer:** measure how often a **temporary** object-abstraction recurred --- a carry closure, an [AB-41](#open-decisions-design--plan-only) ephemeral whole, or an LLM repeatedly treating the same set as one pattern --- and let *that* decide when a durable abstraction should be minted and when it should dissolve. **The pinned `CarryClosureFragment` note** in [`ludicGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/AGENT.md#known-limitation-deferred) --- *a composition abstraction is a named, persistent carry closure* --- **is the same move seen from the structural side.**

**Why it is parked rather than opened as a row: the two are not equally addressable, and the gap is practical rather than conceptual.**

| | Attention ledger | The recognition parallel |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Built out of** | Deterministic code already running --- the presentation kernel's narrate branch | Nothing running; recognition itself is unbuilt (**AB-18**) |
| **Its consumer** | `ludicCache`, already designed against it | No designed consumer |
| **The miss signal** | **Nearly free** --- the slow path's own walk, weighted by hop count | **An LLM inference.** Judging that an abstraction *would have* helped is itself the expensive thing being optimized |
| **Weight's unit** | Work avoided, commensurable with residency cost | **No measured unit.** Bounded LLM context and narrative quality are the payoff, and neither is a number yet |
| **Cost of being wrong** | [Degrades to *slow*, never *wrong*](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#the-one-inherited-clause-that-most-changes-how-this-plan-should-be-read) | **Not obviously.** A mint is a graph write, and **AB-20** already grades on-demand minting a **correctness obligation** |

**The last row is the one that would decide the design, and it inverts the sub-plan's founding premise.** Everything the attention ledger is licensed to be --- lossy, sampled, approximate, aggressively compacted --- is licensed by *wrong degrades to slow*. **Recognition may not inherit that licence**, which makes [AH-12's hazard](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#attention-near-the-accept-gate-can-produce-fast-wrong) the default case here rather than the one exception. **So the parallel is real at the level of the question and unestablished at the level of the answer**, and designing it now would mean designing against the ledger's premises rather than against measurements.

**Three constraints the park must respect to stay exitable:**

1. **Keep the ledgers separate if both ever exist.** [AH-7](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) already leans hard that reasoning-path traversal must **not** confer player-facing salience --- different consumer, and it leaks detail nobody in the fiction learned. Feeding recognition attention into the attention ledger would also corrupt the demand signal `ludicCache` reads. **One shape, two ledgers.**
2. **At the ledger's graduation, record which properties were load-bearing and which were incidental.** That is the whole lesson harvest, and it is cheap **only** while the implementation experience is fresh --- see the hook in the sub-plan's [Phase 4](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#recommended-order). Reconstructing it later from a graduated contract is archaeology.
3. **Do not close AB-41's promotion half with a bespoke counter in the meantime.** A hand-tuned *"seen N times, therefore mint"* threshold is precisely the mechanism this park exists to replace, and building one first spends the decision before the evidence arrives.

**No AB ID is assigned, deliberately.** Per rule 1 above, an ID would make this look answerable now, and the answer depends on measurements that do not exist. **When the ledger has been built and iterated, this entry either becomes a row or is deleted** --- both are fine outcomes.

## Phase 0 corpus

**Moved 2026-08-07 to [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md).** The corpus grew to ten cases and ~270 lines, and it is the block most often read on its own and least often edited --- so it lives beside this plan rather than inside it, per [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md)'s guidance on keeping bulk out of a plan body. **It is not archived and not optional reading:** Phases 1 and 2 argue from it directly, and its per-case annotation tallies are their input.

Cases to date: C1 (Room/Feature participation), C2 (the rope refused at the gate), C3 (the snare Coyote cannot name), C4 (the Faerie Court place setting), C5 (the chain mounted at both ends), C6 (the impromptu timer), C7 (Ariadne's thread), C8 (the flashlight that vanished), C9 (coiling the rope back in), C10 (the moonbase computer system), C11 (the Rube Goldberg machine), C12 (the moonbase power system and the cut cable), C13 (taking and returning the fork). Remaining cases are listed under [Phase 0](#recommended-order).

## Open decisions (design --- plan only)

Plan-only: decisions being made in order to design this initiative. When one ships, record it in the relevant durable doc ([`positions/AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md) for normative rules, [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md) for graduated vocabulary).

**A resolved row is not deleted from here --- it graduates to the [Settled register](#settled-register) below.** Changed 2026-08-14: a design row closes into *other rows that argue from it*, so deleting it strands them. The verdict stays in the register; the derivation moves to the [discussion record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#settled-decisions-full-record). **A row graduates only when nothing live remains inside it** --- split the remainder to a new ID first, since a live question buried in a closed row is a dangling pointer with no section name.

**Say where the row stands in the first clause of the Status cell.** Added 2026-08-14 on measurement: five rows here led with a supersession event (*"H1 withdrawn 2026-08-07"*) and stated the actual disposition --- *Row returns to Open pending re-tag* --- two thousand characters later, at the end. A reader skimming the column, and the first graduation pass, both read them as closed. **The lead is the status; everything after it is history.**

**IDs are stable and never reused**, following the ladder's BD-N convention. Highest assigned: **AB-51**.

**Requirement rows and representation rows are different, and most rows here are the second kind.** Noticed 2026-08-06 when AB-4 turned out to be conflating both. *What must the model be able to express?* is answerable from the fiction with confidence. *What is the representation?* is not, and depends on the first. A row asking both makes the answerable half unreachable --- so when a row resists, **check whether it is really two rows** before treating it as hard. AB-4 / AB-32 is the worked example; AB-1 is the main pre-existing requirements row. **Splitting a conflated row is not the same as adding scope:** the count rises, the surface does not.

**A second distinction, added 2026-08-10 and cutting across the first: *not every constraint in this design got here the same way*, and how one arrived decides what re-opening it costs.**

| Kind of constraint | How it got here | What re-opening costs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Derived constraint** | A corpus case forces it --- [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s DAG, [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)'s *where along* | **Highest.** There is evidence to overturn, and a case to explain away |
| **Minimal primitive** | The simplest thing that worked; never compared against an alternative, because no alternative was ever on the table. Justified by **absence of demand** rather than by argument | **Lowest.** The reasoning has not been done, so re-opening *does* it rather than undoing it. Its justifications are typically **post-hoc** and cannot be cited as grounds |
| **Placeholder** | Known-inadequate, retained pending design --- `Custom -> defer`, which *"has never had a resolution path"* | **None to overturn**, but it owes a design; re-opening is scheduling work, not revisiting a choice |

**Why this earns a place beside the requirement/representation split: it is how *provisional, acted on as settled* actually happens.** A minimal primitive quietly accumulates post-hoc justifications until it **reads** like a derived constraint, and then defends itself with reasoning that was never the reason. **[Single-use](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) is the worked example** --- see **PQ-14**, which characterizes it as a minimal primitive and records the tell: *both* of its justifications plausibly survive the relaxation they are cited against, which means they were not doing the work. **PQ-9's numbered-versus-named is a likely second instance**, and is still genuinely undecided rather than quietly settled. **The diagnostic:** ask what evidence produced a constraint, not what arguments now support it.

**Rows whose answer is *derived from* a working hypothesis carry an `H1-dependent` marker in their Status cell**, per the tagging test in [Working hypotheses](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#working-hypotheses). Merely being *compatible* with H1 earns no marker. The set of markers is the rollback unit if H1 is withdrawn, and its size is a deliberate measurement --- do not apply it defensively.

| ID | Question | Constrained by | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **AB-52** | **Reverse-index depth: what is `EphemeraPositionAdjacency` *for*, once hosting is recursive across five component kinds?** The index answers **immediate** host. Today that is one or two levels --- object in room, character in room, object in a character's inventory --- so *what room is this in* is a single lookup, and [PQ-1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06)'s strength table justified keeping it in truth on exactly that **N:1** saving. **Once Object, Feature and Area are hosts** ([the ports plan's LP0](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#recommended-order)), a spring is in a box, in a niche, in a wall, in a room: **the reverse walk becomes multi-hop and unbounded, one Dynamo read per hop.** The index keeps working and stops being fast at the thing it existed to be fast at. **Opened 2026-08-15 from conversation, and deliberately stated without a recommendation.** Two answers are *not* to be read in as defaults: storing transitive closure (the drift-prone denormalization this plan has rejected twice, most recently as [position 3 growing back](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06)), and *chains will be short in practice* (unevidenced). **Note what this row is not:** it is not caused by containment kinds --- [LD-5](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) settled those onto graph edges and the index stays kindless and countless. **It is caused by the host widening alone.** **Leading candidate, recorded 2026-08-15: a *parallel* reverse index measured into [`ludicCache`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md), not a more expressive adjacency measured into `ludicGraph`.** Node -> the cache host(s) whose consolidated view contains it. **The precedent is this plan's own:** [PQ-1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06) judged the ingress list *weak at 1:1*, pulled it out of truth and put it in `positionCache`, stating the general form --- *a denormalization earns a place in truth by what it saves*. **A depth index fails that bar in truth and passes it in a cache**, so this is the rejection's own remedy rather than a way around it. **Three things fit rather than merely permit.** (i) **The consumer is already there** --- CC3 wires the referent path to consult the cache, so the lookup costs no new read. (ii) **Drift is bounded by a rebuild that already exists**, rather than needing transactional maintenance. (iii) **[P6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure) clause 4 already licenses the miss** --- *an error costs slow, never wrong* --- so a stale or absent entry falls through to the walk, which a truth-side index could never promise. **The limit is a boundary, not a hole:** the cache is attention-scoped and raggedly consolidated, so anything past the frontier is absent from the index --- and a thing past the apprehension frontier is not a thing anyone is asking *which room is it in* about. Coverage and domain have the same shape. **Second candidate, from the same conversation, and it composes with the first rather than competing:** even for a record outside every cache, **walking up `ludicCache` entries may beat walking up `ludicGraph` entries.** Walking up graphs is really walking the *adjacency* index --- a graph names its children, not its parents --- so each hop is a Dynamo read and the chain is as long as the containment depth. Walking to the **nearest consolidated ancestor** instead bounds the walk by **distance below the frontier**, then resolves the room in one cache lookup. **Attention makes that distance small for anything anyone cares about.** (A third reading --- cache-to-cache laddering --- depends on whether caches nest, which depends on Area-as-host; see [LD-9](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only).) **One split this row must make before it can be answered, identified 2026-08-15: two questions have been travelling as one.** *Which room?* is **shortcut-able** --- any ancestor already in a cache answers it. *What is the containment chain?* is **not** --- narration wants *the spring in the box in the niche in the wall*, where every hop is content rather than a step toward an answer. **A cache-side index serves the first and does nothing for the second**, so the enumeration below must record which of the two each consumer needs. | AB-8, PQ-1, PQ-13 | **Open, with a leading candidate and a named gate.** The gate is an **enumeration, not a design pass**: *who needs depth resolution, and does each want the room or the chain?* The candidate is right-sized if every consumer is apprehension-shaped; **a *write* path needing upward resolution would break it**, since a write cannot depend on cache warmth --- and PQ-1's reversal of that very objection does **not** transfer, having rested on the room's own edges sufficing. First read suggests writes stay local (membership mutation works within one host; `RoomStack`/eviction is character-to-room at depth 1), but that is two call sites, not a survey. **Blocks no step in the ports plan**; the index remains correct throughout and only its *cost* changes, and [LD-5](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)/LP4f are untouched either way --- adjacency stays coarse and kindless under every candidate here, which is evidence the decomposition is right |
| **AB-6** | **Membership derivation and decay.** Is an abstraction's member set **stored** (an explicit set) or **derived** (a predicate over graph edges)? Someone takes the fork --- does the place setting survive annotated, dissolve, or get recomputed? Trim-to-valid is the structural precedent (hook 5). Stored sets imply a drift-repair story; derived sets imply a cost and stability story. **Pushed toward *stored* 2026-08-05 by [part-mediated reference](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger), on a mechanical rather than a preferential argument:** evaluating an operation over a spanning whole needs every part **and its host** known *before* the walk begins, because `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered. A *derived* member set cannot supply that without already knowing where to look --- the derivation would need the answer it is computing. **What stays open:** the drift-repair story a stored set implies, and whether decay is trim-to-valid (hook 5) or something stronger. **This row is being asked its question twice, possibly with opposite answers --- noticed 2026-08-05 via [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread).** Part-mediated reference forces **member sets** toward *stored* (the footprint must be known before the walk). Ariadne's thread pulls **presence** --- which rooms a spanning whole occupies --- toward *derived*, since a flat container set cannot supply "the thread now also occupies the room the walker entered" without the same inference either way. Those are different things and the answers may legitimately differ, but the row as written reads as though it has **one** answer. **Split it explicitly in Phase 2** rather than resolving it as a single question and discovering the other half later. **A third answer to the presence half arrived 2026-08-06 from [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06), in a carrier neither option contemplated: presence stored *in the whole*, as its port list.** Under single-use ports a port's exterior end is a graph plus an optional endpoint, so a presence port is simply one with no endpoint --- and the rope then enumerates the rooms it occupies from its own graph. **That is neither "a stored container set on the part" nor "a predicate derived over room edges"**, which is why the row's two-option framing did not reach it. **Consequence to weigh when splitting: it puts presence and member sets in the *same* carrier**, which is the outcome the double-question worry was written to avoid --- so the split may resolve more cheaply than expected, or may reveal the two answers were never really in tension. **The third carrier stopped being contingent 2026-08-06 when [the frame was locked](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06):** it arrived via P3 and was therefore only as available as P3 was, but the port list follows from locked clause 2 rather than from any open PQ. **And the presence half acquired a *qualifier* 2026-08-06 from [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system), which is a different thing from a fourth carrier.** Whatever carries presence, C9's rule for reading it --- at least one port egressing here --- **is falsified as stated**: a moonbase computer system and a rope have identical port topology and require opposite room-scale answers, so presence is **not derivable from the graph at all** and needs a declared property (working name: *apprehension scale*). That does not change where presence is stored; it changes what the stored thing has to say. **Split the row with this in view** --- the presence half is now two questions, carrier and derivability, and the second was invisible while the rule looked sound. **So this row must now be split against three presence candidates, not two** --- and the third is not a variant of either, which is why the original framing could not reach it. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): half the remaining open scope is discharged by locked-frame clause 1.** The Status's *drift-repair story for the stored half* has no drift to repair --- with **AB-4**'s `PartOf` resolution the member set **is** the `PartOf` edges of the whole's own graph, one representation rather than a stored set standing beside a graph it could disagree with, and trim-to-valid (hook 5) is structural since a destroyed part takes its edge with it. **What survives is *decay policy*, not drift** --- does a whole with one member remain a whole --- which belongs to AB-17's reabsorption criterion rather than to this row. | AB-1, AB-4, AB-5, AB-34 | ~~Open --- member sets forced toward stored (`H1-dependent`); presence has a third carrier (port list), now standing rather than contingent; row still needs splitting~~ **Reverted 2026-08-07: H1 withdrawn.** The stored-leaning argument rested on `computeStepSequenceFootprint` needing every part and host known before a single up-front walk --- entailed by H1's clause 4/5 whole-migration machinery, which dissolved with H1. **What survives:** the port-list presence carrier is H3's mechanism, not H1's, and is unaffected by this reversion; AB-1's pull toward *derived* and P4's settling reading stand as before. **Same day, closed by P4-iteration-1's acceptance:** the "contested" note above asked whether AB-1's *derived* pull or the footprint's *stored* pull wins, and P4 was flagged as the candidate that dissolves the conflict rather than picking a side. It does, concretely, now that iteration 1 is decided rather than provisional --- **the two pulls were never actually about the same fact.** The **structural member set** a synchronous transfer step needs (AB-40(a)'s territory --- the fork's node must be findable before the footprint is fixed) stays **stored**, mechanically, independent of P4. The **consequence facts** AB-1's state facet was pulling toward *derived* (is the door powered, what does a whole's summary say) are **settled**, via P4-iteration-1, which is neither of the row's original two options. **Row is Open only on the drift-repair story for the stored half and the settle-state vocabulary for the derived half (PS-3) --- not on which of stored/derived wins, which no longer has a single answer to give.** |
| **AB-8** | **Multi-host extent.** The rope. **[Proposal P1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) (2026-08-05) proposes resolving this by *removing* multi-host objects entirely** and modelling spanning things as parts with the whole hosted at the containing node --- which would settle this row as framing (c), vindicate the contract clause rather than rewriting it, and move the difficulty into AB-25. Recorded as a proposal, not adopted; the framings below remain live. Three candidate framings: (a) one node with presence in many hosts --- **less cheap than an earlier draft of this row claimed.** Adjacency is natively multi-host, but "nothing enforces single-room today" was wrong: the membership compile gate **terminally errors** on `containers.length > 1` before planning is reached (corrected 2026-08-05 from [C2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c2-the-rope-in-two-rooms-refused-at-the-gate); see [Multi-host presence](#multi-host-presence-what-is-actually-true-today)). Framing (a)'s real costs are therefore that gate, end-state planning collapsing it, a singular `toHostId` on the transfer step, intra-host-only relations, and per-room affordance compose rendering a whole rope in each room --- **five sites on four routes, each of which decided single-host independently.** That multiplicity is itself evidence about the framing: a design the codebase has re-derived against five times is being pushed uphill. **It strengthens P1 symmetrically** --- a single-host design makes the gate correct as written, exactly as it vindicates rather than rewrites the contract clause --- though P1 remains a proposal, not adopted; (b) the abstraction is itself a manipulation host above Room; (c) per-room segment nodes composed by a **composition** abstraction, which would **collapse AB-8 into AB-4**. Note (a) and (c) differ mainly in *where the seam is drawn*, not in capability --- weigh them on which one a player-facing description and a `pull` operation read more naturally from. **Strongest evidence for (c), 2026-08-05 --- [C7, Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread).** Framing (a) represents a spanning thing as **one node** with presence in many hosts, which means there is nowhere to record *where along it* an attachment sits: three attachments on three hosts, a container set that is unordered, and a route that cannot be recovered from exits because a labyrinth has multiple paths. For an object whose **purpose is the route**, (a) is not merely awkward --- it cannot express the object. **This is the clearest discriminator the corpus has produced against (a), and it is about node-versus-parts rather than about hosting**, so it argues for (c) without by itself settling single-host. **Earlier evidence for (c), same day:** a rope *tied to a rock* puts a relation on one end, not on the whole. Under (a) that edge is ambiguous about which host it lives on, and the kernel derives a relational step's host live and throws on mismatch; under (c) it is cleanly cliff-top-segment-to-rock, same host, no new machinery. ~~**Settled sub-point:** extents spanning hosts of *different kinds* (two adventurers holding opposite ends in different rooms) is **not** a concern --- confirmed 2026-08-05.~~ **Reopened the same day, and the reason is instructive: the words did not change but the question did.** That point was settled while framing (a) was live, where it is genuinely a non-issue --- a rope simply has presence in two hosts, of whatever kinds. **Under H1 it becomes a question about the shape of the containment tree**: the whole must host at an LCA of a room and a character, so characters have to be interior nodes of a structure AB-25 frames purely as Room/Area. Same sentence, different question. **Recorded as a caution beyond this row:** adopting a hypothesis silently re-poses settled questions that were settled against the *previous* model, and nothing flags them --- so the settled notes predating [H1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) are worth a deliberate sweep rather than trusted by default. **Also carries a doc-correction obligation:** the contract's at-most-one-room-per-`OBJECT#` clause contradicts the intended design and must be reconciled in Phase 1. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): framing (b) has been absorbed, and this row still lists three live framings.** Its *host* half is now true of **every** framing --- locked-frame clause 3 makes every host a node in its own graph, so *is the whole a host* stopped discriminating --- and its *ladder* half (**above Room**) is **AB-12(b)**, on the lock's exclusion list and being decided on AB-30's evidence rather than on this row's. **That sharpens [PQ-7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s same-day sign-off**, which bounded this row as *still open between (a)-with-ports and (b)*: (b) is not an AB-8 fork either. With (c) already recorded as a modeller's option rather than a rival, **one framing is left**, and the Status should say so or say why not. | AB-1, AB-2, AB-4, AB-16 | ~~Open --- provisionally framing (c) under [H1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node); `H1-dependent`, and the first member of the rollback set. The row stays Open deliberately: H1 is a hypothesis being tested, not a decision, so (a) and (b) remain live and this row reverts to unframed if H1 is withdrawn.~~ **Reverted 2026-08-07: H1 withdrawn, exactly as this row's own text anticipated.** Framing (c) --- decomposition into parts --- survives as a modeller's *option* under [H3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice), not as a forced framing; see [C11 Finding 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-2-decomposition-is-demoted-from-correctness-to-convenience). (a) and (b) remain live alongside it.
