| **AB-9** | **Which level gets presented, and is that manipulation truth or a projection?** The stated motivation is LLM reasoning scalability, which is a *read-side* problem: the value is in being handed the right level without seeing all levels. Projections are already filters, not the graph (hook 6). **This row most controls total scope** --- if summary is a projection concern, much of axes 1 and 3 never becomes stored truth. Also: is the chosen level perspectival (a novice sees a place setting, an inspector sees five utensils)? **Guardrail, relocated here from AB-14 on 2026-08-05:** description **selects among levels that already exist and must never create one**. If merely looking at something can mint durable structure, then description-time LLM inference becomes a write path, and this row stops controlling scope at all. **Escalated 2026-08-05 by [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends), and the change is one of frequency, not kind.** [P1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) already noted it makes multi-level graphs acute; C5 shows co-located parts persist **permanently** whenever they are load-bearing, so a chain mounted at one end and attached to a chest at the other is three nodes in the cell's graph indefinitely. Multi-level is therefore the **steady state of anything anchored**, not a transient during manipulation --- so level selection is a default read-path concern that every describe must answer, which raises this row's weight without changing its question. **The guardrail now has a case that contests it directly --- [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it), 2026-08-06.** "Look *carefully* at the flashlight" mints its interior. That is **description acting as a write path**, which this row forbids outright; but AB-14 holds that operational necessity licenses minting and AB-20 that the player's own reference *is* the licence --- and a player asking for detail plausibly qualifies. **Both rows are defensible and they disagree about this case**, which is the first time the guardrail has been contested by anything other than an argument. Candidate resolutions, none chosen: the guardrail holds and close examination is not description but a *command* that happens to read; or the guardrail admits an exception for player-initiated detail requests, which weakens its scope control; or minting is deferred (AB-18's tier 2) so the look itself creates nothing. **Substantially deflated 2026-08-06 by [P2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) clause 3, though not answered.** "You see a rope, a rope end, and a rope end" is a problem only because parts must be **nodes** to carry relations. With part-addressed edge endpoints the rope is one node and its ends are endpoint qualifiers, so **there is nothing at the membership layer to select between** --- and C5's "permanent multi-level steady state," which is what escalated this row, turns out to have been an artifact of that same constraint rather than a fact about anchored things. **What survives untouched:** the perspectival question (novice versus inspector), and the guardrail collision with AB-14 --- both are about which level to *describe*, which no representation change settles. **Track the deflation honestly: this row was escalated on a premise P2 removes, so re-weigh it before treating it as scope-controlling.** **The guardrail gains a structural enforcer and owes a carve-out, 2026-08-10 from AB-5(A).** *Enforcer:* if minting is reachable only as a planner-emitted mutation op, description cannot create a level **by construction** --- the presentation kernel filters to `describe` / `narrate` and never enters the mutation walk --- so the guardrail stops being a rule someone must remember. **That is a strengthening, and it makes the remaining exception cheaper to state precisely.** *Carve-out owed:* AB-5 adopts [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it) as **this row's own first candidate resolution** --- *look carefully* is a **command that happens to read**, routed through the planner, not description --- **on a stated product intent rather than on the balance of argument**, which stays even. **This row owns the wording**, and the wording matters more than usual: a carve-out phrased as *"description may mint when the player asks for detail"* re-opens the write path the guardrail exists to close, where *"a detail request is a command, and commands mint"* does not. **Also gains a concrete consumer for the perspectival half:** AB-5 case (1) renders *Contents: Snare trap* rather than *Contents: Rope, Stick, Loop*, which is a level-selection rule landing in affordance compose. **Carve-out decided and committed to 2026-08-10. Stated normatively: a *perception command* that is actively seeking detail beyond what is currently represented --- at abstraction levels either above or below the coarseness now held --- may mint the level it seeks.** **The line this draws is passive rendering versus active seeking, and that is why it does not re-open the write path.** Ordinary description --- room rendering, affordance compose, anything the system emits without being asked for more than it holds --- **still mints nothing, ever**. What mints is a **command**, player-initiated and planner-routed, which keeps AB-5(A)'s structural enforcement intact: minting remains reachable only as a planner-emitted op, and the presentation kernel is still incapable of it. **Stated in terms of player intent rather than of call site**, because intent is what the parse can actually see, and a call-site rule would have to be re-derived at every new read path. **Bidirectional deliberately, and this is not a detail.** A carve-out permitting only *downward* detail-seeking would install exactly the asymmetry AB-0's departure 3 was repaired to avoid --- *things may decompose but may not compose* --- inside the row that controls what gets presented. Above and below are the same act at different signs. **It unifies two AB-5 cases that were listed separately:** *look carefully at the flashlight* ([C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it)) and *does the kitchen have a chef's knife* are **one rule**, not a precedent plus an exception --- which is the strongest evidence the carve-out is drawn at a real joint rather than fitted to C8. **Naturally idempotent, and worth recording as a property rather than leaving to be noticed:** the licence is to mint *what is not yet represented*, so a second look at an already-decomposed flashlight **selects** rather than mints. Repeated seeking cannot fork the world, which is AB-18's convergence obligation satisfied from a second direction. **The cost, stated honestly rather than minimized: this does weaken this row's scope control**, exactly as the candidate resolution above predicted. Perception commands can now create stored truth. **Bounded, though, on three axes at once** --- active seeking only, beyond-current-coarseness only, planner-routed only --- so what is given up is a clean *never*, not the scope argument itself. **One gap this wording does **not** close, and it must not be cited as though it did.** The rule licenses **seeking**; nothing here bounds **what may be found**. *Does the kitchen have a chef's knife* and *does the kitchen have a nuclear reactor* are the same command shape, and only the coarse representation already in place (*a kitchen with all the expected fixings*) distinguishes them. **That is a plausibility question, not a perception question** --- it belongs to AB-19 (bounding the LLM step) and to the P5 licence surface AB-41 names as open. **Note it differs from AB-20's on-demand rule** rather than inheriting it: AB-20 holds that the player's *reference* licenses existence (*they said "the north wall," so there is one*), and a **question is not an assertion** --- so the licence here comes from the existing coarse representation, not from the asking. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): the deflation is right in conclusion and its stated ground is retracted.** *The rope is one node and its ends are endpoint qualifiers* is the phrasing **AB-4** recorded P2 itself withdrawing on 2026-08-09 --- [`rope: rope-end` is **an address into the rope's own graph**](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#where-p2-clause-3-risks-recreating-the-very-defect-it-fixes) --- and locked-frame clause 1 settles it: **the ends are nodes.** **The deflation survives on a different ground:** they are nodes in the rope's *own* graph, so the **room's** membership layer still holds one node and still has nothing to select between. **One clause of it is now false, though.** C5's permanent multi-level steady state is *not* an artifact of a representational constraint --- under locked-frame clause 1 it is exactly a fact about anchored things, and what encapsulation removes is its **visibility** at room scale, not its existence. **Practical consequence is a status change rather than a re-argument:** the re-weighing this row owes is owed under the locked frame instead of under P2, it comes out the same, and it is dischargeable now. | AB-1, AB-5, AB-14, AB-19, AB-20, AB-41 | Open --- scope-controlling claim needs re-weighing under P2; guardrail contested by C8; **the `H1-dependent` tag on the C6 escalation lapses 2026-08-07 with H1's withdrawal** (the question itself was never H1-dependent, so the P2/C8 tensions stand unaffected). **C8's collision closed 2026-08-10 by the committed carve-out** --- active seeking, either direction, planner-routed --- which also unifies AB-5's flashlight and chef's-knife cases into one rule and discharges AB-5's last open fork. **Row remains Open on its own two questions:** the perspectival half (novice versus inspector), and the scope-controlling claim's re-weighing under P2. **What may be found** --- as distinct from what may be sought --- is explicitly *not* here: AB-19 and the P5 licence surface |

The resulting multi-host mutation also has a shipped shape: `MultiKeyUpdate` folds N effects into one `transactWrite`. **So the kernel does not change; a new expansion is written instead.** Notable that the scheme was not designed to answer this row and answers it anyway --- weak evidence the joint is real. **What stays open:** whether *every* abstraction-level operation decomposes this way, or only propagation-shaped ones like `pull`. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): right about the members, silent about their edges.** *The membership half genuinely does not grow the step vocabulary* holds for the nodes; under locked-frame clause 2 the boundary edges those nodes carry must become **ports**, which is a third write in the expansion and a fourth outcome in a shipped classifier that has three. See **AB-5**. | AB-1, AB-4, AB-8 | ~~Open --- candidate answer recorded (`H1-dependent`); generality untested~~ **Reverted 2026-08-07: H1 withdrawn.** The Expansion-based answer was argued from H1's whole-hosted-at-Area framing; plausibly re-derivable under [H3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) --- a "pull" still needs to reach each port's local part --- but not re-argued here. Row returns to Open. **Partial answer restored 2026-08-10 from AB-5(A), on independent grounds --- for the *lifecycle* operations only.** Establish-whole and dissolve-whole are **compile-layer abstract ops** in the [`compilePositionKernelOp`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/compile/compilePositionKernelOp.ts) family, so `MutationKernelStep` stays closed --- reaching this row's cheap answer **without** part-mediated reference, which is what the H1 withdrawal took away. **Explicitly not a re-derivation of the `pull` answer:** AB-5 argues from the compile layer's op/step split, not from Expansion, and says nothing about whether propagation-shaped operations decompose. **Verifications owed, not forks --- and one of the two first listed here is retired.** AB-40(a)'s interior addressing is **not** needed for an ordinary mint (the source is an ordinary room host), only for nested wholes and C13's fork-taking; `establishRelation` carrying AB-4/AB-48's `PartOf` kind **is** still owed, since `transferMembership` moves members and does not create edges. Both sit in [Parked extension points](#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark); if the second refuses, one step kind is added. **Row stays Open**, now on a narrower question: this row's *original* subjects (`pull`, `clear the place setting`) are untouched. **Sharpened the same day by AB-5's A-and-B framing, and it lands squarely in this row's territory.** Mint is *interpose a level between host A and a subset B of its members*, which compiles to an ordinary `transferMembership` (`entityIds` = B, `fromHostIds` = {A}, `toHostId` = the new whole) --- so **the *membership* half of the lifecycle operation genuinely does not grow the step vocabulary**. **Do not read that as the whole answer** --- an earlier draft of this note did, and it contradicted the verification recorded two sentences above: members move by transfer, but the `PartOf` edges do **not** move with them, so mint remains transfer **plus** establish and the zero-new-step-kinds claim still rests on `establishRelation` accepting a containment kind. **The work relocates to exactly what this row asks about second: the planning and compilation pathway** --- identifying A and B, expanding *interpose* and *release*. **Do not import improvisational object spawn as the model**; that is the **pure-add** shape (nothing pre-existed) and its coordination-layer problems are not these. |
| **AB-12** | **Is there a privileged level at all --- and are `<Object>` / `<Room>` / `<Feature>` already abstractions?** Candidate reframe (2026-08-05): the battery / flashlight / casing regress has no natural floor or ceiling, so pinning `<Object>` as origin with `<Part>` below and `<Abstraction>` above would build two type hierarchies for one relation pointed in opposite directions. If instead everything is an Object at *some* level, the composition case needs **no new type**, and nothing converts when a part is separated --- only the composition edge changes. **The reframe is clean for Object and much larger for Room and Feature:** Room is a *host*, so treating it as another level unifies hosts and nodes (AB-2's most radical answer, rooms nesting in rooms, Area as a bigger Object). It also makes Feature a part-of-Room, which would *explain* rather than merely assert the documented "Features are never membership-moved" narrowing --- while contradicting that documented decision. **Take Object and Room/Feature as separable bets; do not let the second ride in on the first.** **The second bet is itself two bets, split 2026-08-09, and only one of them is now settled.** **(a) Hosts and nodes unify** --- a host is a node in its own graph --- is **locked** as [locked-frame clause 3 of the locked frame](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06). **(b) Room/Area/Feature as levels in the part-of ladder** --- rooms nesting in rooms, Area as a bigger Object, Feature as part-of-Room --- **stays open, and is explicitly on the lock's exclusion list.** **The "contradiction of the documented Feature narrowing" is withdrawn 2026-08-10: there was no representation rule to contradict.** This row (and AB-30) read *Features are never the subject of a relation* as a constraint on **edge legality**. It is not --- the source states it as participation in *"only a **subset** of the **instruction structure**"*, alongside *look-at-able* and *not `transferMembership`-able*. **It is an affordance rule: so scenery can be described without the engine offering to put a mountain in a backpack.** A world-model reading was inherited and never checked against what the restriction was drawn *against*. **Consequence: Feature-as-`PartOf`-subject contradicts nothing**, the graph layer imposes no Feature restriction, and the guard that *is* worth keeping moved to **AB-41** (ephemeral wholes may not absorb Features). **Doc-correction obligation, booked on AB-8's precedent rather than left implicit:** [`ludicGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/AGENT.md) states the narrowing in representation-sounding terms and should be re-scoped to say plainly that it governs the **offer surface**, not edge legality. Not edited during design; Phase 6. **The split is the useful part of this row's history:** (a) is about graph *internals* and (b) about the *containment ladder*, and everything that made this bet risky --- the authored-merge provenance seam above the room --- is in (b). **A uniform graph interior is not a uniform ladder.** **The everything-is-an-Object reframe is separately under pressure from two directions** (**AB-42**'s negatives and locked-frame clause 3's Room root both want a node tag that is not `'Object'`), and **neither threatens locked-frame clause 3**, since *every graph has a root* is strictly weaker than *everything is an Object*. **Part (b) itself splits by thirds, and they are moving at different speeds (2026-08-10).** ***Feature as part-of-Room* is in active play** --- **AB-30** now leans wall-as-`PartOf` and supplies the evidence (b) was always going to need. ***Area as a bigger Object* is untouched**; note `Area.positionGraph -> Room.positionGraph` **already exists** and is authored, so the ladder above the room already has one rung and (b) is not the same as *no ladder*. ***Rooms nesting in rooms* is parked --- representable, unsupported, and gated by redirection rather than prohibition.** **The park, stated so it is an outcome rather than debt.** Room-in-room structure is genuinely useful in prospect (a ballroom with a lemonade table, a dance floor, a musicians' stand) but its **player-facing UI and its apprehension reasoning** --- *how much can people on the dance floor perceive of action at the lemonade* --- are daunting, and **no corpus case demands it: zero of twenty-one.** So the **schema may store it; the modeller and UI neither create it nor promise to handle it well.** **The mechanism is a redirection, not a licence narrowing, and that distinction is the whole of why it works.** Under [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item) licence is unbounded by default, so *not building* room-nesting would leave it **permitted** and improvisation would produce it --- *a ballroom with all the expected areas* -> *is there a quiet nook?* is [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about)'s kitchen pattern one scale up, and the licensed move. **A narrowing to zero would fix that at the cost of the fiction** and would install exactly the level asymmetry (objects decompose, rooms do not) that AB-0's departure 3 is already carrying. **Instead: the modeller is prompted, and then deterministically gated, to mint an improvised sub-space as a *Feature* `PartOf` the room rather than as a nested Room.** **Three properties this has and a prohibition does not.** (i) **The gate is a type check, not a semantic judgment** --- *is the minted entity a Feature or a Room* is decided at the mint site, where *does this count as a sub-space* would require the compiled-fictional-judgment move **AB-19** rejects. One place, fully enforceable. (ii) **It reuses AB-30's construct rather than adding one** --- a wall is a Feature `PartOf` a room and so is a nook; one mechanism, and it is fractal-consistent (rooms decompose into parts like everything else) where a ban would not be. (iii) **The exit ramp is a re-tagging, not a re-modelling**, since under locked-frame clause 3 and roles-not-kinds a Feature with a `positionGraph` and a Room with one are structurally identical --- the difference is the host-kind union and what apprehension does with it. **One rule the park depends on: characters take a *spatial* relation to the sub-space, never a containment one.** `character In room` stays **direct**, with `character Near/Against nook` as a spatial qualifier. If a character were `In` the nook and the nook `PartOf` the room, character-room membership would become a **two-kind derived chain** on the system's hottest fact (perception, movement, navigation). Spatial kinds are not shard-defining, so this leaves that path untouched --- [the family split](#the-relation-kind-register) doing load-bearing work. **What the park does not buy, and must announce:** a nook exists but does not gate perception, so *who can hear this* answers *everyone in the ballroom* --- correct at room scale under scale-relative truth, but the nook's **existence** is a finer fact and the **no-silent-omission** rule applies. The reasoning path must be able to say *"there is a nook by the lemonade; whether it affects audibility is not modelled."* **Silence here is the specific hazard the lock's own warning names** --- a reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained. **Unpark trigger, named:** a case demanding sub-room structure that a Feature cannot carry, **or** apprehension-scale ([H3 clause 8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)) being built for other reasons. **The unpark *shape* is sketched at [Out of scope](#out-of-scope-parked-capabilities-and-what-unparking-them-would-look-like-2026-08-10), and explicitly not owned by this plan** --- structurally the exit is free (re-tag Feature -> Room, remove one gate), and everything expensive about it (apprehension policy, perception, UI) belongs to other lanes. **What *is* in scope is keeping the exit cheap:** the three constraints recorded there --- no code may assume a Room's graph contains no Rooms, the gate stays one labelled site, and Feature sub-spaces stay structurally identical to Rooms. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): the room-nesting park prices apprehension and not addressing.** A nook that is *"structurally identical"* to a Room has a graph and a root under locked-frame clause 3, so under locked-frame clause 2 **anything placed in it sits behind a port from the room's graph** --- and the three keep-the-exit-cheap constraints are about nesting rather than about crossing. **The question the park does not ask: when the ballroom is described, do the lemonade table's contents come back?** Either they live in the nook's graph and a room-scale read must cross a port to reach them, or they live in the room's graph and *the schema may store it* is weaker than it sounds. **This row's own no-silent-omission obligation is why it matters** --- a nook that quietly swallows its contents out of room-scale description is precisely the failure that rule exists to catch. | AB-0, AB-19, AB-29, AB-30, H3 clause 8, P5 | **Open --- reframes AB-1; second bet split 2026-08-09, part (a) locked as locked-frame clause 3, part (b) open and excluded from that lock. Part (b) further split by thirds 2026-08-10:** Feature-as-part-of-Room in play at AB-30, Area-as-bigger-Object untouched, **rooms-nesting-in-rooms parked with a redirection gate and a named unpark trigger** |
| **AB-13** | **Partial order, not a ladder.** "Levels of abstraction" invites a scalar, but composition has overlap and multiple parents: the fork is part of the place setting, of the silverware set, and of grandmother's inheritance, none ranked against the table the setting sits on. Designing for "level N" would build an integer where a DAG is needed. The codebase already carries the artifact --- `computeCarryClosure` guards with `closureSet.has(...)` exactly because a node is reachable by more than one path, which is why `rootId` is **recorded, never derived** (hook 1). Decide whether multiple parents are permitted, and if so what that does to closure, transfer, and description. **Worked example added 2026-08-05 --- [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer), and it is stronger evidence than the fork.** The fork's three wholes (place setting, silverware set, grandmother's inheritance) are *categories* of the same kind, so a reader can talk themselves into a nesting. C6's two wholes are different **in kind**: the rope is a physical object, the impromptu timer is a mechanism, and neither contains the other. A DAG is not a modelling preference there --- a tree cannot express it at all. **One shape decision can be taken ahead of the question, 2026-08-05:** [part-mediated reference](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger)'s parent index should be **plural from the start**. A part belonging to both a rope and a knotted pair makes "pull the rope" ambiguous --- but the *ambiguity* can be deferred while the *field shape* cannot, since widening a singular field later is the more expensive order and is exactly the mistake `MutationKernelTransferStep`'s singular `toHostId` now represents. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), and both halves move.** *The question:* whether multiple parents are permitted is **already answered** --- [H3 clause 6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) states membership **is** a DAG as a hard requirement forced by C6. *The decision:* the plural **parent index** is H1 machinery, and this row was not among the six `H1-dependent` rows [rolled back](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07), so it was never re-homed. **The insight outlives its carrier** --- under locked-frame clause 2 the field it applies to is the **port list**, plural by construction, so the decision is *discharged by the design* rather than relocated. | AB-3, AB-12 | ~~**Open --- field shape decided ahead of the question**~~ **Open --- DAG half answered by H3 clause 6; field-shape decision discharged; what remains is what multi-parenthood does to closure, transfer and description** |
| **AB-14** | **One mechanism, two minting modes --- and the axis is criticality, not timing.** Refined 2026-08-05 (twice; see Discussion record for both supersessions). **The action is improvisation in both cases:** a sure-enough recognition that cups, plates and flatware form a table setting mints a `Table Setting` component of which the others become (at least temporarily) parts --- the same act as minting a north wall. What differs is **whether a pending operation is blocked on the mint.** **On-demand:** "lean the table against the north wall" cannot resolve until the referent grounds, so the mint is synchronous and on the command's critical path. **Recognition:** a loop of rope laid on a road may be understood as a snare immediately, thirty seconds later, or never, without materially harming responsiveness. Synchrony is a *consequence* of criticality, not the axis itself --- which matters because it predicts the three rows below. **Same persistence layer** (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, so provenance is invisible downstream), **potentially very different decision processes**, which sit entirely upstream of persistence. **The narration obligations also differ:** on-demand narration *is* the minting event (the parts come into existence for the player by being described); recognition's narration is a **contract with the player** --- how they learn the world accepted their construction as meaningful --- so it can never be a silent internal summarization pass. **Collides with AB-9's guardrail on a concrete case as of 2026-08-06 --- [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it).** "Look carefully at the flashlight" is on this row's account an on-demand mint (the player asked for detail, so detail is licensed) and on AB-9's account forbidden (description must never create a level). **Resolve the two rows together rather than separately**, since each is individually defensible and they give opposite answers. See AB-9 for the candidate resolutions. | AB-5, AB-9, AB-16, AB-17, AB-20 | **Open --- shape proposed; collides with AB-9 on C8** |
| **AB-15** | **What does authoring privilege, if not ontology?** If no level is ontologically primary, an authored `<Object>` is plausibly the level the author asserts is worth having **by default** --- the level unqualified reference binds to, revisable by circumstance. ("Barring specific instructions, you pull out the whole coil.") That would give authoring a real role that survives AB-12's reframe, and answers part of AB-10's template/instance question without a template/instance split. Decide whether default-binding is the whole of what authorship contributes, or whether authored levels are also privileged for persistence, description, or legality. **A concrete instance arrived 2026-08-05 --- the naming seam, and it is the one step of [part-mediated reference](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger) that does not fall out of existing machinery.** The referent catalog matches on `normalizedShortName`. A player says "pull the **rope**"; what is present in the room is a part. If the part's short name is "rope end," the whole's name is **not in the catalog at all** and grounding has nothing to bind to. So under H1 the authored level --- exactly the level this row proposes unqualified reference should bind to --- is precisely the thing that is *not locally present*. Three candidates, none chosen: **(a)** parts alias the whole's short name; **(b)** a two-phase match consults the parent index after a local miss; **(c)** the catalog widens to include reachable wholes. ~~Note (c) reintroduces the non-present things in the catalog that part-mediated reference exists to avoid.~~ **Corrected 2026-08-05 by [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread): (c) is better than that dismissal allowed, and it needs splitting rather than rejecting.** A thread passing through a labyrinth room **is** present there --- physically, describably, trippable-over. What part-mediated reference exists to avoid is surfacing Area-hosted **bookkeeping** wholes in rooms they do not touch. Those are different sets. So (c) resolves into two candidates: widen the catalog to wholes that **pass through this room** (defensible, and required if presence is derived rather than stored), versus widen it to **all reachable** wholes (which is the version that reintroduces the problem). **The naming seam dissolves entirely under multi-host + ports --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in), 2026-08-06.** The room's node **is** `OBJECT#ROPE`: the rope is present *by name* in every room it passes through, and the ports carry the where-along. So the catalog needs no widening, no aliasing and no two-phase match --- **all three candidates (a)/(b)/(c) become unnecessary rather than one of them being chosen.** Note precisely what that costs: the seam was created by single-hosting the whole, so **its dissolution is evidence about H1 clause 1b, not about this row's own question**, which (what authoring privileges, if not ontology) is untouched and stays open. | AB-10, AB-12 | **H1 withdrawn 2026-08-07 --- no change needed.** This row's text already anticipated multi-host + ports ([H3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s mechanism) and scoped the `H1-dependent` tag to the concrete instance only, already noted as dissolving under that scheme. Tag lapses; the row's own question stands Open exactly as before |
| **AB-17** | **Must the transition run backwards, and how far?** Haul the rope up, coil it, stow it: it must re-collapse to one object. The flashlight hides this (nobody un-dismantles often enough to notice), so designing from that example alone would produce a one-way ratchet accumulating orphaned parts. **The rope is therefore the better design driver for AB-5/AB-6 than the flashlight.** Decide the reabsorption criterion (candidate: no part is separately referenced or separately positioned), whether reabsorption is lossy, and whether a part that acquired its own history or relations can be reabsorbed at all. **Split into two criteria 2026-08-05 by [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends), and note this row's disjunction was right all along --- it was [P1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) that collapsed it.** The two conditions are independent, so **migration-down and reabsorption are different events** and neither implies the other. **(i) Hosting** keys on *separately positioned* only: the whole hosts at the node containing all its parts, and migrating down happens the moment they co-locate. **Mechanized 2026-08-06 by [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in): under ports, "all parts co-located" is "all ports share an egress host"** --- a read off the egress list rather than a judgment, in the same way the relational half of criterion (ii) turned out to be a graph query. **And criterion (ii) gains a second mechanical clause from the same case:** "separately referenced" now means `nodeHasRelationalEdge` **plus** *does any port carry an exterior edge* --- a presence-only port does not block reabsorption, an attached one does. **(ii) Reabsorption** requires *both* conditions false --- and the **relational half of "separately referenced" is already enforced by shipped code**: `removeObject` throws `RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError` on a node a Relational edge still references (BD-33/35, no silent-strip variant), so the check is a graph query via `nodeHasRelationalEdge`, not a judgment, and the unsafe reabsorption is already impossible. **Substantially cheapened 2026-08-06 by [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings), if ports hold.** Under direct part-addressing, coiling the rope dissolves `RopeEnd1` and every external edge naming it must be found and rewritten **across hosts** --- which is also AB-36's missing index. Under ports, port 1 rebinds to the root and **nothing outside the rope changes**: reabsorption stops being a cross-host graph rewrite and becomes one internal edit. **Note ports were not designed for this row**, which is the weak evidence-of-a-real-joint signal this plan has twice taken seriously. **This is the half of the encapsulation claim that survives the [single-use](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) correction**, and it survives *because* reabsorption is interior-only --- the rope keeps its identity, so no external reference has to change. AB-31's fission does not have that property and lost its version of this payoff. **What remains genuinely open** is the non-relational half --- what "separately referenced" means for a part carrying no edge but recently named by a player, or one that acquired its own history --- plus whether reabsorption is lossy. **And a third gap found 2026-08-05 by [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer): dissolution is *per-whole*, not per-part.** This row currently reads as though a part is reabsorbed or it is not. With multiple parents (AB-13) that is not expressible: the impromptu timer dies when the candle burns out while the rope persists, so the shared span is **released by one parent and retained by another**. The criterion therefore has to be evaluated per (part, whole) pair rather than per part, and "reabsorb the whole" has to mean "release my claim on these parts," not "delete them." **Both halves of that sentence stop being cautions 2026-08-10, via AB-5's A-and-B framing.** Mint *interposes* a level between a host A and a subset B of its members; dissolve is **the exact inverse, A and B swapped in role** --- so the ratchet this row was opened against cannot form, because there is no asymmetric pair of operations to get out of step. **And *release, do not delete* becomes structural**: dissolve **transfers** members from the whole back to A, and transfers do not delete. **What survives as a real obligation is smaller and sits at the call site**, not here: dissolve also retires the whole's **own** identity rows, so *deletes itself / transfers its members* must be stated together or the adjacency will get them conflated. **Unaffected:** the non-relational half of *separately referenced*, and whether reabsorption is lossy. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): *exact inverses* does not hold as the pair is currently described.** Dissolve is port-aware --- AB-5 derives its plan-time footprint from *a whole's port list, which enumerates every host referencing it* --- and establish, as specified, mints no ports at all. **An operation and its inverse cannot disagree about whether ports exist.** This row has the most at stake in the repair, since its ratchet worry is declared dissolved **by** the inverse claim; the fix is one clause in AB-5's op shape, not a re-argument here. | AB-5, AB-6, AB-16 | ~~Open --- hosting criterion settled under H1 (`H1-dependent`); reabsorption criterion open, but its relational half is discharged by shipped machinery rather than by this initiative~~ **H1 withdrawn 2026-08-07.** [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) already reframed hosting criterion (i) as a ports read --- "all ports share an egress host" --- which is [H3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s mechanism rather than H1's specifically; plausibly survives re-tagged `H3-dependent`, but not asserted here without re-checking against H1's original wording. The reabsorption criterion (`nodeHasRelationalEdge` plus an exterior-port check) is unaffected, resting on shipped machinery. Row returns to Open pending re-tag. |
| **AB-18** | **Recognition's scope, trigger, and convergence.** The two modes in AB-14 are **not** symmetric in difficulty. **On-demand minting is bounded by its operation** --- a screwdriver applied to a flashlight, or a referent that must ground, scopes exactly what to mint. **Recognition is an unbounded search**: noticing that several operations, possibly separated in time, have composed into a thing. Decide the scope (one host? a room? a carry closure?), the trigger (evaluated on each state *transition*, or periodically over *state* --- note an authored room whose table already bears six utensils has no transition to fire on), and the cost/latency policy when an LLM is in the loop. **Convergence requirement, found 2026-08-05:** the mode is a property of *the moment, not the thing*. "Check the snare" puts recognition on the critical path, so the same mint is reachable from a **blocking referent-resolution path and a deferred background path**, and the two **must produce the same component** --- otherwise a snare noticed one way differs from the snare noticed the other, or both fire and the world gets two. Ties directly to AB-21's identity obligation. Failure-cost asymmetry moved to AB-20. **The convergence requirement gains a mechanism 2026-08-10 from AB-5(A), and it had none before.** If minting is a **planner-emitted compile-layer op**, then the blocking path and the deferred path **emit the same op through the same expansion**, so producing the same component is structural rather than an obligation on two independently-written paths. **This is the single largest gain AB-5(A) delivers to another row**, and it should be weighed as evidence *for* the op reading rather than only recorded as a consequence of it. **What it does not touch --- and it is this row's harder half.** Scope, trigger and cost/latency are untouched: an op needs an **emitter**, and recognition's emitter is exactly what AB-5 leaves Open (its own producer, or [P4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)'s settling lane). The authored-room-with-six-utensils problem --- **no transition to fire on** --- is unaffected by anything here. | AB-5, AB-14, AB-19, AB-20, AB-21 | **Open --- convergence mechanized via AB-5(A); scope, trigger and the emitter itself untouched** |
| **AB-19** | **Recognition for description, or for mechanism --- and what must an abstraction carry to bound an LLM step?** Reframed 2026-08-05; an earlier version of this row proposed that recognition-for-mechanism be tractable via an **authored pattern vocabulary with behavior attached**, and that is **rejected**: it is the compile-fictional-physics-into-deterministic-structure move, which is both the wrong tool and not what the fiction is about. **Settled direction:** most player-assembled contraptions will require an **LLM step** to reason about consequences. The abstraction's job is to make that step **bounded and manageable** --- to be the right-sized packet of context --- **not** to replace it with a lookup. **Description vs. mechanism therefore separates by stakes, not by machinery:** description-recognition is a nice-to-have first pass with no causally complicated consequences (a place setting, Jane Austen aside) and stays pure projection under AB-9's guardrail; **mechanism-recognition is essential** --- reasoning about a snare *as* a snare, in the abstract, is the whole point, and deriving its result from first principles every time is a fool's errand. **The real question this row now asks:** what must a recognized abstraction carry so that an LLM step over it is bounded? Prior art and its limits: [Coyote as prior art](#coyote-as-prior-art-bounded-llm-reasoning-that-is-not-bounded-structurally). **This row's rejection was applied as precedent 2026-08-06, in a place it was not written for.** [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06) asks whether relation kinds across a port boundary must be *compatible* --- `ThreadsInto` outside, `SolderedTo` inside --- and the answer is no compatibility matrix, **because a matrix would be the compile-fictional-physics move at a smaller scale.** Worth noting as a use of this row rather than a new decision: the rejection generalises, and its next few applications are likely to look like small, reasonable-seeming validation tables. **Half answered 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), and it is the structural half:** *bounded* has two components, and locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 settle the **extent** --- the packet is one level of interior graph and its boundary is the port set, which is [H3 clause 4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s grounding invariance read from the reasoning side. **The *content* question --- what the abstraction must carry, in what vocabulary --- is untouched and is now the whole of this row.** | AB-7, AB-9, AB-10, AB-14, AB-15, AB-22 | ~~**Open --- direction settled, shape open**~~ **Open --- direction settled; extent answered by the locked frame; content open** |
| **AB-20** | **Failure semantics and authorization: the two consequences of AB-14's criticality axis.** **Failure:** an on-demand mint is a **correctness obligation** --- if it fails the command fails and the player is owed an error; recognition is **discretionary world enrichment** --- if it fails nothing happens and nobody is owed anything. Both disciplines already exist side by side in the objects lane (blocking-and-compensated existence+placement under **S1**, versus best-effort non-blocking embeddings and enrich prose), so this is a choice between shipped patterns, not new machinery. **Authorization:** on-demand minting needs **no confidence gate at all** --- the player's reference *licenses* the existence; they said "the north wall," so there is one. Recognition is **unlicensed**: the system volunteers an unprompted claim about what the player built. **This is why AB-18's false-positive cost lands only on recognition** --- the asymmetry follows from who authorized the mint, and should not be re-derived as an arbitrary policy choice. | AB-14, AB-18 | **Open** |
| **AB-21** | **Latent vs. invented, and the identity obligation.** The north wall may not be *invented* at all --- a room has walls whether or not anyone wrote them down, as the flashlight always had a battery. That is different in kind from conjuring something genuinely new, and it carries an obligation recognition largely does not: **identity**. Two commands referencing "the north wall" in one room must get the **same** wall, and so must a re-reference after a Lambda cold start; otherwise repeated reference silently multiplies the world. `stableKey` on `Meta::Object` exists for exactly this correlation problem (Coyote) and is the obvious hook. Decide whether latency-vs-invention is a real distinction worth modelling or only a narration nuance, and where the idempotency key comes from when the thing was never authored. **A second instance one layer down, 2026-08-06 --- [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)'s PQ-4.** Numbered ports invite reuse: release port 1, mint port 1 again, and a stale external reference silently rebinds to a *different* attachment --- repeated reference multiplying the world, in the same shape this row already names for the north wall. Candidates there are monotonic never-reused allocation, or keying ports by what they bind rather than by ordinal. ~~**Worth resolving with this row rather than separately**, since the obligation is identical and only the scale differs.~~ **Withdrawn 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): the obligation is *not* identical, and the merge is what makes PQ-4's weaker candidate look natural.** The north wall needs a **nominal** identity, so two descriptions reach one thing --- `stableKey`'s problem. A port needs an **allocation** discipline, so one boundary slot is not silently reused. **They rhyme; their fixes do not**, and a port number is [not a name for the node behind it](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06). Keep them as an analogy, resolve them apart. | AB-14, AB-15, AB-20, AB-34 | ~~**Open --- second instance at port scale (PQ-4)**~~ **Open --- PQ-4 is an analogy at port scale, not the same obligation** |
| **AB-22** | **When is affordance annotation computed, and in what vocabulary?** Coyote's answer, and the part that generalizes: **at mint time, once** --- `tropeAffinities` are written by the Acme enrich call and live durably on `Meta::Object`, so the prediction pipeline consumes structure rather than re-deriving it. That directly answers AB-18's cost worry. **(i) Timing:** annotate at mint, lazily on first reasoning need, or continuously --- **and see AB-23, which shows mint-time can only be a partial answer.** **(ii) Vocabulary:** Coyote's structural roles (`terminal`, `trigger`, `delivery`, `autonomous_agent`, `prep`, `creation`) and six tropes are genre-specific; `EnvironmentAffordanceObject` literally enumerates `boulder`, `cactus`. Keep the closed-structural-vocabulary / open-narrative-content split (`gimmick` is deliberately free text) and graded `aptness` over booleans. **Bonus mechanism:** member annotations are a cheap **filter** for whether an arrangement is worth recognizing at all --- fork + plate + cup carry no structural roles and are causally inert; a rope with `trigger` aptness beside a branch with `delivery` is worth a look. That attacks AB-18's unbounded search **without** an authored pattern vocabulary. **Caveat from AB-23: the filter is itself context-dependent** --- at the Faerie Court the place setting stops being inert. | AB-14, AB-18, AB-19, AB-23 | **Open** |
| **AB-23** | **Relevant meaning is situational, not a fixed world vocabulary.** Reframed 2026-08-05, superseding AB-22's implicit "is the vocabulary general or authored per world?" --- a **static** question with no good answer. The real question is **"what kind of meaning is relevant *right now*?"** Coyote may shortcut this (as a first iteration deserves) because its genre supplies a constant, unvarying interpretive context. A general world cannot: ambushed by goblins, meaning is relative to combat; dining at the Faerie Court, whether the place setting has causal complexity becomes very real --- **same world, same objects, different relevant meanings.** Consequences: **(a)** mint-time annotation can only capture *durable* properties, so situational significance must be layered on top --- annotation is plausibly **base + overlay**, not one pass; **(b)** an existing axis already models exactly this shape one layer down --- the render cache keys records on `n` (mark state, `Array<{mark, value}>`) plus perspective and carries `situationId`, documented as "one cache row per distinct render (component + n + perspective)." **Premise corrected 2026-08-05 from [C4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c4-the-place-setting-at-the-faerie-court):** this row previously said the axis is "built and unpopulated," and that is wrong in a way that matters. It is **populated for Rooms** (`Meta::Room.state.marks`, merged by `handleApiStateChange`, defaulted by `computeDefaultMarksForRoom`) and **structurally absent for every other host kind** --- `requestIntake` pins Feature / Knowledge / Object / Character to `{ markValue: [] }` and `allowGeneration: false` **because they have no `Meta::Room` to carry state**, and `findRender` gates generation on `isEphemeraRoomId`. So the pin is not a switch awaiting a flip; it is downstream of a missing carrier. **Consequence: situational meaning below room scale needs a diegetic-state carrier for non-Room hosts, which is AB-7** --- flagged there as possibly out of scope, and now with a second consumer. This question remains that axis raised from *how a thing renders* to *what it means*. | AB-7, AB-15, AB-19, AB-22, AB-24 | **Open** |
| **AB-24** | **A third scheduling tier: ambient context-shift detection.** Proposed 2026-08-05. Beyond blocking on-demand minting and deferred-but-promotable recognition, a **low-immediacy scheduled thinking task** would periodically ask: *has the context or vocabulary for this room / these players shifted such that we should be watching for different kinds of meaning?* **It parameterizes recognition rather than performing it** --- tier 3 sets what tier 2 looks for. Substrate: [`thinking` as the scheduling substrate](#thinking-as-the-scheduling-substrate-shipped-ledger-unbuilt-queue). **Two design constraints worth stating up front. (i) Its failure mode differs in kind:** a missed snare is one missed snare, local and recoverable, while a stale interpretive frame makes tier 2 hunt for the wrong meanings *systematically* until it corrects --- silent and pervasive, which argues for cheap-and-frequent over expensive-and-rare. **(ii) The author already declares most of the frame:** a Faerie Court room is *authored* as a Faerie Court, so this task's job is detecting **deviation** (goblins ambush a room authored as a social space) rather than deriving a frame from nothing --- much cheaper and more verifiable. That second point is AB-15 again; see the note under [Constraint matrix](#constraint-matrix). | AB-15, AB-18, AB-23 | **Open** |
| **AB-25** | **Is the host containment tree actually a tree?** **Note (2026-08-05): AB-26 shows this row shares a substrate with part-connection licensing.** A **nesting** relation does double duty --- boat-in-bay both places the boat in the containment tree *and* licenses a connection across the boundary (over the rail). An **Exit** edge licenses a connection without implying containment either way. So the two rows overlap without being identical, and a nesting representation should be designed for both consumers at once rather than twice. Split out of [Proposal P1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) 2026-08-05 as its load-bearing unknown, and **worth answering even if P1 is rejected** --- any scheme that hosts a whole "above" its parts needs it. **Framing residue flagged 2026-08-09:** *above* is H1-era height language --- a whole hosts its parts **in itself**, per [the whole/part dissolution](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). Nothing this row concludes changes; the phrase would re-teach the dissolved distinction to a reader arriving here first. Area membership is authored blueprint merged at participation order, so containment may be **asset-stack dependent**, while an object's host must be world truth rather than perspective-relative. Sub-questions: can one Room belong to two Areas (canon plus an overlay)? Do Areas nest, and how deep (`AREA#region` beside `AREA#downtown` suggests yes)? What is the root when two rooms share no Area? And since containment is authored **downward** (`StandardAreaData.positionGraph` lists members) with **no** room -> area index anywhere, where does the upward lookup live, given it must run on every part move? **A constraint arrived 2026-08-05 from [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer), and it forbids the shortcut this row is most likely to reach for: hosting height does not correlate with composition depth.** In C6 one part has two wholes hosted at **different heights** --- the rope spans room and garden so it hosts at the Area, while the impromptu timer's members are both in the room so it hosts in the room. So walking up from a part yields parents at inconsistent levels, and **no implementation may treat the parent index as "the next level up"** or index hosting by depth. This is AB-13's ladder-versus-DAG error one layer over, in the place it would look most tempting: *hosting* feels like it ought to be stratified, and it is not. Note the case does **not** bear on whether Room/Area containment is a tree --- that question is untouched and remains this row's core. **A second constraint the same day, and this one widens the row rather than restricting it: the tree is not Room/Area at all.** `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is `Room \| Character`, so characters are hosts, and a rope with one end held by a walker needs an LCA of a room and a character. **Characters must therefore be interior nodes** --- room contains character contains held object --- which this row's framing (Areas, nesting, participation-order merge) does not contemplate. Two consequences: the "is it a tree" question now spans a structure with **two node kinds of different provenance** (authored Area membership versus play-time character presence), and character movement --- the highest-frequency mutation in the system --- becomes a **containment-tree mutation**, so every whole with a part held by the walker re-hosts. Reopens the pre-H1 settled note on AB-8. **Deflation locked 2026-08-06, and the row narrowed with it.** Sibling links living inside the whole's graph gave AB-26 a home *below* the rooms; [locking parts and ports](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) makes that a property of the frame rather than a consequence of an unadopted proposal, so **Area is no longer summoned by part-connection licensing at all** and this row keeps only its own question --- is Room/Area containment a tree, now spanning two node kinds of different provenance. **This does not close the row and does not retire the trigger:** the character-as-interior-node widening is untouched, and AB-25 remains H1's only live falsification test. **PQ-8 is the acknowledged loose end**, and locking raises what being wrong about it costs, since it is now a loose end in something fixed. **Second consequence of the lock, and it promotes this row's importance while narrowing its scope: AB-25 is now the boundary the fractal stops at.** Objects below the room are ported graphs; Room/Area containment is not, and is of different provenance (authored asset-stack merge versus play-time graph mutation), so a walk upward from a part still changes representational regime here. **This is the next boundary that would have to be locked for the scale-invariance claim to be general** --- see [What the lock delivered](#what-the-lock-delivered-for-scale-invariance-and-where-it-stops-assessed-2026-08-06). **A hazard specific to that assessment:** encapsulation means external code cannot see what scale a thing is at, so this asymmetry is now **less visible without being less real**, and nothing will complain about it. Do not read quiet as resolution. The character-as-interior-node widening is the sharpest form of the question --- nothing says a character is a ported graph, and an LCA of a room and a character needs one story, not two. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): this row is not H1's live falsification trigger, because [H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07).** Nothing the row concludes changes --- the containment-tree question never needed H1 and survives its withdrawal intact --- but its **standing** does: a row carrying the initiative's last falsification test reads as urgent, and this one has read that way for three days while owning an ordinary open question. **Fourth instance of the rollback under-covering**, with AB-13, AB-31 and PQ-7, and the sharpest of them: a **falsifier** is the kind of dependency least likely to carry an `H1-dependent` tag, because the tag was tracking rows that would need **re-deriving** and a test needs **retiring**. Status corrected. | AB-4, AB-8, AB-13 | ~~**Open --- narrowed by the locked frame to the containment-tree question proper; still [H1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node)'s only live falsification trigger**~~ **Open --- narrowed by the locked frame to the containment-tree question proper. The falsification-trigger standing is withdrawn 2026-08-10: H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07, and this row's question never depended on it** |
| **AB-26** | **How do parts on different hosts connect? (Adjacency-mediated, not sequenced.)** Corrected 2026-08-05, superseding this plan's earlier claim that extent parts are "ordered along a path." **A sequence is the wrong shape** --- it looks right only because a rope is linear, and a net spanning four rooms, wiring from a generator to a powered door, or two ropes knotted together all branch. This is the same error as AB-13's ladder-vs-DAG, one layer down: a scalar imposed where a graph belongs. **The right shape is a graph of parts whose edges are labeled by the spatial relation licensing them** --- "the clifftop rope end connects to the shaft section *by way of the 'into the abyss' exit*." Four properties follow: branching is free; the edge *explains* the adjacency rather than asserting it; narration gets direction and wording from the exit (as the shipped move compiler already does with `exitName` as an ingredient) rather than from an index; and `pull` becomes propagation over a graph instead of an index walk. **Already scaffolded:** [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#fractal-ludic-graphs-container-scale-and-edges) notes Area edge kinds beyond Exit "may express **non-traversable** spatial facts (e.g. 'north of' without a door)" --- exactly what a rope through a hole in the floor needs. **Two rules to pin: (i)** connections are **explicit but constrained** --- adjacency *licenses* an edge, it does not derive one, since two ropes in adjacent rooms are not connected; **(ii)** an edge **must** reference **world truth** and **never** the perspective-filtered output of `projectRoomExits`, or a rope's physical shape would vary by who is looking at it --- the authored-vs-projected line the graph-roles table already draws, in a new place. **Correction 2026-08-05:** rule (ii) first read "the *authored Exit* edge," which collapsed two independent axes into the one real constraint. **The licensing relation may be of any kind and any provenance:** *kind* --- an Exit edge (a hard-coded connection affordance), or an adjacency/nesting relation (someone goes over the rail of the boat and ends up in the bay); *provenance* --- authored **or improvised**, since an improvised relation is world truth the moment it is minted (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, not a flag). Only *projected* is excluded. **A write-side consumer appeared 2026-08-06 --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in), and it widens this row.** Hauling a rope in moves its far end one room along the path, not straight to the hauler --- so if that is an invariant rather than a convention, **the licensing relation constrains where a part may relocate to**, and this row's adjacency-licensing stops being purely read-side structure. **PQ-12** carries the fork. Note the asymmetry: on the read side adjacency *licenses* a connection that must still be explicit; on the write side it would *restrict* a move, which is a stronger use of the same relation and should not be assumed to follow from it. **Candidate home found 2026-08-06, and it is *below* the rooms rather than above them --- [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings).** Part-connections become **sibling links inside the whole's own graph**, so this row no longer needs an Area-level carrier, which deflates AB-25 in turn (Area was largely being summoned to host things above rooms). **One loose end, recorded as PQ-8:** these edges are labeled by the *licensing spatial relation*, which is exterior world truth --- so a sibling link sits inside the rope while its label points outward, and AB-28's floor-hole repair now has to reach a dependent link buried one level deeper. Ports do not help there. **Title and premise stale as of [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), 2026-08-10; findings intact.** Under locked-frame clause 1 a whole's parts are nodes in **its** graph and are never on different hosts --- the *whole* has ports into different hosts. **The question this row actually answers is how sibling parts inside one whole record the exterior relation licensing their connection**, which is what its own candidate home and PQ-8 already describe. **Rename before graduating**, and see the same correction applied harder at **AB-27**, whose central distinction does not survive it. | AB-8, AB-11, AB-16, AB-25, AB-28, AB-34 | **Open --- shape settled, rules proposed; candidate home inside the whole (PQ-8 open); title reframed 2026-08-10** |
| **AB-27** | **Composition edges look host-relative, and only one half is new.** Observed 2026-08-05 while resolving AB-26. **Parts sharing a host** (a flashlight's battery, bulb, casing) can relate through the **existing** intra-host relational vocabulary --- shipped and working. **Parts on different hosts** (rope segments) cannot, because `applyStepSequenceCore` derives a relational step's shared host and **throws** on mismatch; those need AB-26's adjacency-mediated connection. So the genuinely new edge kind is required **exactly and only where the current model already refuses to go**, which suggests a real joint rather than a parallel mechanism. Decide whether these are two edge kinds or one kind with two validity rules, and what happens at the boundary --- when a co-located assembly is pulled apart across hosts, does a same-host relational edge **become** an adjacency-mediated connection, and is that a migration or a dissolve-and-recreate? **Mostly dissolved 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) --- this row's central distinction is a pre-lock premise.** Locked-frame clause 1 puts every part of a whole in the whole's own graph, so **there is no parts-on-different-hosts case**: `applyStepSequenceCore`'s throw-on-host-mismatch never fires for parts of one whole, and the joint this row locates at the host boundary is not there. The closing question describes an event that does not occur --- [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) established there is no whole-migration under ports, only a port whose egress changes. **What survives is narrower and worth keeping:** do the sibling links inside a whole reuse the shipped intra-host relational vocabulary, or do they need their own kind? | AB-4, AB-16, AB-26 | ~~**Open**~~ **Open --- narrowed 2026-08-10 to whether interior sibling links reuse the shipped relational vocabulary; the cross-host half dissolved with its premise** |
| **AB-28** | **Licensing relations are mutable --- and this is the *shipped* boundary-edge discipline, not a new problem.** Found 2026-08-05 as a consequence of AB-26 admitting improvised adjacency: repair a floor-hole and the relation licensing a rope threaded through it **disappears**. **Recognized the same day as an instance of an existing pattern** --- *a mutation that would sever a relationship is not legal until the severing is made explicit.* `removeObject`/`removeCharacter` assert-and-throw on residual relational edges (`RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError`, BD-33/35) with deliberately **no** silent-strip variant; [`boundaryEdgeOutcomes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) classifies; expansion classifies and the compiler sequences `dissolveRelation` steps **ahead of** the mutation. **The three candidate outcomes are the existing verdict channels:** "you cannot seal it" is `error`, "sealing severs the rope" is `dissolve`, "can you seal a hole with a rope through it?" is `defer`. **Carries over unchanged:** assert-and-throw over silent strip; classify-before-legal; commit-time re-validation on locked graphs; severance narrated in step order via `factsForStep`. **Three real differences (below) and one bifurcation (AB-29).** **A fourth difference, and this row was the last to hear about it --- surfaced 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10).** Under locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 the link that the floor-hole repair must sever is a **sibling link inside the rope's own graph**, labeled by the exterior Exit --- so discovery has to reach from an exterior relation to an interior dependent, and the shipped `boundaryEdgeOutcomes` classifier operates on same-host edges. **AB-26 recorded this in 2026-08-09** (*"AB-28's floor-hole repair now has to reach a dependent link buried one level deeper. Ports do not help there"*) and it never reached this row. **Whether that reach is merely harder or outright illegal is [the open question about clause 2's direction](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-finding-that-is-about-the-lock-rather-than-a-row-locked-frame-clause-2-does-not-state-its-direction)**, on which this row, AB-26, AB-36 and PQ-8 all depend. | AB-17, AB-26, AB-27, AB-29 | **Open --- pattern identified, extensions open; the interior-dependent case added 2026-08-10 and gated on locked-frame clause 2's direction** |
| **AB-29** | **Extending the boundary-edge discipline to licensing relations: three gaps and a bifurcation.** **(1) Discovery runs backwards.** Today: remove a *node*, find edges incident to it --- local, on a graph already held. Here: remove a *relation*, find the part-connections referencing it. That is a reverse lookup from a relation to its dependents, and **nothing indexes it**. It is the parked reverse-edge-reference index on its **third** appearance in this initiative, which makes it the most load-bearing parked item so far. **Fourth appearance 2026-08-06 ([P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)'s PQ-6), at which point it was split out as its own row --- AB-36 --- and promoted from parked to prerequisite.** **(2) The footprint reaches a third host.** Sealing a hole in Room A must lock Room A, Room B (the rope's far end), *and* the Area carrying the license --- all discoverable at plan time, since `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once and cannot be re-entered. **(3) The mutation may not route through the positions kernel at all** --- "repair the hole" is not a `transferMembership`. **The bifurcation, and it is the important part:** **play-driven** license removal has an operation to block or transform, so the shipped discipline applies directly and the conflict is **content**; **asset-withdrawn** license (an overlay deactivates and the adjacency it authored vanishes) has **no operation to make illegal** --- nobody did anything --- and needs the **eviction ladder's** trim-and-repair posture instead, one layer over. Only the first half is the existing pattern. **A third branch, added 2026-08-09 from [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about): asset-*added*.** An improvised negative established in play (*there is no deep fryer*) contradicted by a later authored edit that adds one. **Neither existing posture reaches it** --- there is no operation to make illegal, so the boundary-edge discipline has nothing to act on, and nothing is being *withdrawn*, so the eviction ladder's trim-and-repair has nothing to trim. **And the mechanism the bifurcation would otherwise inherit does not apply either:** layered merge resolves conflicts between layers *of a component that exists*, but existence is not merge output (improvised objects exist by pair row plus placement, authored ones by asset participation), so **a negative fact has no component to be a layer of** and the participation stack cannot rank the two claims. **This is a clearer instance of the play-versus-asset conflict than the licensing-relation cases that opened this row** --- those are second-order consequences of an edit; this is a direct contradiction between two assertions about one fact. Note the mirror direction is the easy one and should not be conflated: an improvised *positive* contradicted by an authored edit leaves a component to evict, which is branch 2 behaving normally. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): this row is the origin of [the clause-2 direction cluster](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-finding-that-is-about-the-lock-rather-than-a-row-locked-frame-clause-2-does-not-state-its-direction), not a fifth member of it.** Gap (1) states that cluster exactly --- a reverse lookup from an **exterior** licensing relation to **interior** part-connections --- and **AB-36 was split out of it**, so PQ-8, AB-26 and AB-28 inherit from here rather than sitting beside this row. **Which way locked-frame clause 2 reads therefore decides gap (1) before it decides any of them.** *Separately, gap (2) carries [Finding 1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#finding-1-parts-on-different-hosts-is-a-pre-lock-premise-and-one-row-is-built-entirely-on-it)'s residue at footprint scale:* *Room B (the rope's far end)* sites a part in a room; under the lock the footprint is the rope's **own graph plus each egress host**, read off the port list --- which makes it **more** computable at plan time, not less. | AB-26, AB-28 | **Open --- third branch (asset-added) opened 2026-08-09, no candidate mechanism** |
| **AB-30** | **Addressable but not a member: what makes something a graph participant at all?** Opened 2026-08-05 out of a retired falsification case (see [Working hypotheses](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#working-hypotheses)). A wall is not *in* a room, it **bounds** rooms --- so asking which host contains it is a category error, and H1's "host at the node containing all its parts" has no purchase because containment is not the relation. This is why a wall cannot migrate down; **not** because it is born spanning. **Two corpus cases need it addressable, from opposite directions, within one session:** [C1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c1-look-at-the-character-standing-here-look-at-the-north-wall) needs the north wall *referrable* ("look at the north wall") and [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends) needs it *relation-targetable* (the chain's more interesting end mounts to it, and is currently unrepresentable). Neither needs it to be a member of anything. **That is a fourth answer AB-2's node / host / projection trichotomy does not offer**, and it is independent of AB-12: whichever way the privileged-level reframe goes, the criterion for what belongs on the graph still needs one. **Candidate answer already scaffolded:** [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#fractal-ludic-graphs-container-scale-and-edges) notes Area edge kinds beyond Exit may carry **non-traversable spatial facts**, so a wall may be better modelled as an **edge** than a node --- which would *explain* the documented Feature narrowing (never membership-moved, never a relation subject, possibly a relation target) rather than asserting it. **The discriminator, and it is the sharpest test available: a wall separating two rooms.** Under the **edge** answer, spanning is free --- an edge between two rooms is inherently about both, and nothing needs a host. Under the **node** answer, the wall must exist in two graphs, which is exactly the multi-host problem H1 abolished for Objects, now needing per-graph duplication and a sync story --- and it would make [P1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node)'s "Features stay multi-host" sub-proposal an unremarked asymmetry rather than a decision. **No separate row for spanning Features:** the spanning case is this row's test, not its own question. **Re-framed 2026-08-10 toward a third answer the row could not state when it was written: the wall is a `PartOf` the room.** **Why the option was unavailable, not overlooked.** This row's founding claim --- *a wall is not **in** a room, it **bounds** rooms, so asking which host contains it is a category error* --- was **correct**, and drove straight to the edge answer because on 2026-08-05 the model had no containment vocabulary **at all**. `PartOf` became sayable on 2026-08-09 (**AB-48**). **The category error was against `In`; it is not an error against `PartOf`.** Reached as the re-check this row was flagged for on [locked-frame clause 3's dependent set](#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09). **The parallel holds on the register's own columns:** *intensional* --- room-ness implies walls as crate-ness implies a lid, where it implies no crowbar; *coarsening sound* --- "mounted to the room's north wall" -> "…to the room", the same shape as "the bag's strap" -> "the bag"; *shard-defining* is **vacuous rather than failed**, since rooms do not move. **It also *explains* the documented Feature narrowing rather than asserting it** --- parts move only with their whole, so *never membership-moved* follows --- which is the bar this row and AB-12 both set for a good answer. **Superseded 2026-08-10, and the bar turned out not to apply: the narrowing is an affordance rule, not a representation rule** (see **AB-12**), so there was never a documented decision here to explain *or* contradict. **The wall needs no exception, because the graph layer imposes no Feature restriction.** The one guard worth keeping --- stopping carry-style absorption from moving scenery --- lives at **AB-41** as *ephemeral wholes may not absorb a Feature*, which is a rule about an **operation** rather than about which edges may exist. **The decisive argument is against the edge candidate, and this row never ran it: the edge answer cannot satisfy [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends).** C5 needs the wall **relation-targetable** --- a chain mounts to it --- and if the wall is an edge between rooms, that requires **an edge terminating on an edge**, a capability nothing in this design has or has proposed. **This row lists C5 as one of its two driving requirements and never checked its own favoured answer against it.** Under wall-as-part the wall is a node and an ordinary endpoint. **The discriminator resolves too, because its cost was priced under a model that is gone.** *A wall separating two rooms* was charged to the node answer as *"per-graph duplication and a sync story"* --- true under H1. Under **AB-8**'s (a)-with-ports framing the wall is **listed** in two room graphs with exactly **one wall graph**: the node appears twice, the interior lives once, *"closer to a presence index than to real multi-hosting."* No duplication, and the port list is the sync story. **What it inherits rather than resolves: [H3 clause 6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice).** Two-room parenthood is *licensed* by the DAG (AB-13; [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer) is the precedent) but H3 clause 6 is **Open with no mechanism** for how a part records which of several wholes claims it. **Licensed is not accounted-for** --- the wall does not get that mechanism for free, though it needs building regardless. **Two cautions.** (i) **This is AB-12's part (b)**, on locked-frame clause 3's exclusion list as *open, not rejected* --- so supplying evidence for it is the correct route, but **Feature-as-part-of-Room must not drag rooms-nesting-in-rooms with it**; a room *having* parts is directionally different from a room *being* a part, and roles-not-kinds cuts slightly against that separation, so decide it rather than assume it.
