(ii) **The provenance seam**, which is where (b)'s risk always lived: walls arrive by authored asset-stack merge, so a room's part-set is authored and improvised parts must stay distinguishable across re-merge --- **AB-29**, already required by [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about), so the same machinery rather than new. **One cost, and it is the edge answer's only remaining advantage: adjacency.** *These two rooms are separated by this wall* is **stated** under wall-as-edge and **derived** from shared parenthood under wall-as-part --- a reachability query rather than a lookup. Likely small, since Exits already carry traversable adjacency and a wall is non-traversable by definition, so the two do not compete --- but **AB-26** owns adjacency licensing and should look. | AB-2, AB-8, AB-12, AB-13, AB-26, AB-29, AB-48, H3 clause 6 | ~~**Open --- candidate answer recorded, discriminator named**~~ **Open --- re-framed 2026-08-10 toward wall-as-`PartOf`; edge candidate found unable to satisfy C5, and the discriminator's cost dissolved by AB-8's re-framing. Not closed:** gated on H3 clause 6's unbuilt shared-part mechanism and on AB-12(b) being decided on its own evidence |
| **AB-31** | **Fission: one whole becomes two, and no row owns it.** Opened 2026-08-05 from [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer), whose entire payoff is the rope **burning through**. The lifecycle cluster has minting (AB-5), reabsorption (AB-17) and decay (AB-6); AB-16 remarks in passing that "cutting a rope makes two ropes" but treats it as a property of homogeneous extent rather than as an operation. **Fission is neither minting nor the inverse of reabsorption** --- it is sideways, and under [H1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) with stored member sets and parent indices it is a concrete multi-step write: mint a second whole, **repartition** the member set, rewrite the parent pointers of the moved parts, and recompute **both** hosts (which may migrate in opposite directions --- in C6 the severed lower length falls into the garden and becomes single-room, while the upper remnant becomes single-room too, so *both* halves migrate *down* out of the Area as a consequence of one event). **Questions this row owns:** does fission mint one new whole or two and retire the original --- and does the answer matter for identity (AB-21) and for anything holding a reference? What happens to a **third** whole whose member set straddles the cut, as C6's impromptu timer does? Is fission always player-caused, or can decay produce it? Does the severed pair retain any relation recording that they were once one thing? **Note the footprint problem is acute here:** both resulting hosts, plus every host of every affected co-parent, must be known at plan time, and `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once. ~~**Partly answered 2026-08-06 by P3**: "does anything holding a reference survive?" --- under ports, **yes by construction**.~~ **Overstated, corrected the same day under [single-use ports](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).** Ports make **interior repartitioning** non-breaking, not **identity changes** --- and fission is an identity change. When the rope becomes `ROPE-upper` and `ROPE-lower`, an external edge naming `OBJECT#ROPE:1` must be rewritten to `OBJECT#ROPE-upper:1`, because the object it names no longer exists. **That is true with or without ports**, so this row gains nothing on reference survival. **Contrast AB-17, where the payoff is real** precisely because reabsorption is interior-only. **What ports do give this row is the footprint half** --- the reverse of what was recorded: a whole's port list enumerates every host referencing it, computable at plan time, which is what `computeStepSequenceFootprint` running once demands. **One uncorrected layer found 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10):** this row's concrete write sketch --- *mint a second whole, repartition the member set, rewrite the parent pointers of the moved parts* --- is scoped **"under H1"** in its own words, H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07, and AB-31 was not among the [rolled-back](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07) rows. **Under locked-frame clause 1 fission is *split one graph into two, each with a root, and re-bind ports*; there are no parent pointers and no member-set repartition.** The row's corrected port findings currently sit on top of that uncorrected sketch, which is more misleading than either state alone. | AB-5, AB-6, AB-13, AB-16, AB-17, AB-21, AB-36 | **Open --- footprint answered under P3; reference survival *not* answered, earlier claim withdrawn; write sketch still H1-era and owed a restatement under locked-frame clause 1** |
| **AB-32** | **What must the composition representation be able to *express*?** Split out of AB-4 on 2026-08-06, which was asking only what the representation *is*. **This is not a new question --- it is the answerable half of an existing one**, and separating them matters practically: requirements can be settled from the fiction with confidence, representation cannot, and one row asking both left the confident half unreachable. **First requirement, answered the same day: yes, a whole can be part of a bigger whole.** Abstractions must ladder up *and* down --- resoundingly, not marginally --- which is the requirement behind AB-0's *Abstraction Fractal* naming. **Note what that does and does not settle.** It does **not** force composition into graph edges: a record-based whole holds a member set of ids, an id can be another whole's, parent index points up and member set points down, so nesting is free and [the non-positional-whole candidate](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#candidate-drop-h1-clause-1b-and-the-whole-chain-with-it) survives it intact. An earlier framing of that candidate named wholes-nest as its decider; **that framing made this very conflation** --- nesting is the requirement, edges-versus-record is the representation. **The actual decider, restated as a requirement: must a relation ever attach to a *whole* rather than to one of its parts?** Relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes. Every case so far says no --- [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends)'s chain relates through its ends, [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s candle sits under a span, and that is the third decomposition trigger doing exactly this work --- but **"the place setting is on the table" is the shape that might not decompose**, and it should be tested rather than assumed. **Second requirement, answered 2026-08-06 and consequential: reference and perception must reach a spanning whole and a local whole by the *same mechanism*.** "The thread in room B" and "the flashlight on the table" must not resolve by different machinery, or the difference propagates into every consumer. **This is the sharpest pressure H1 clause 1b has taken**, and it produced [three positions with their prices](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) plus a narrowed dichotomy --- *single-hosting or lookup-as-the-clean-rule, not both* --- and the finding that **the plan is currently in the losing position by accident.** Note the shape of the win: an argument from requirements settled more than four constructed scenarios did, which is the case for this row existing. **Other requirements to collect here:** must a whole be describable without enumerating its parts; must a part be manipulable without loading its whole; must the representation record *where along* an extent something attaches (yes --- C7); must a relation ever attach to a whole rather than a part (above). **Third requirement, answered 2026-08-06 and it changes the decider's force: a relation must be able to attach to a *part* without that part being a graph node.** [P2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation)'s part-addressed endpoints supply it. Note the interaction with the decider above --- "relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes" assumed endpoints are node references. If endpoints may be **addresses**, then even "the place setting is on the table" does not force the place setting to be a node, and the decider weakens from settling the question to merely pressuring it. **Re-test the decider under P2 before relying on it.** **Retired 2026-08-09 rather than re-tested: the decider cannot discriminate.** See [the whole/part dissolution](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). *"Relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes"* has no force once every whole is a node by the locked frame's clause 1 --- the requirement can come back either way and the representation does not move. **The row half-caught this itself** (the appended note that the decider *"assumed endpoints are node references"*) and did not follow through. **Both answered requirements stand untouched**; what is retired is the unanswered decider, not the row. | AB-1, AB-4, AB-13 | **Open --- three requirements answered, collecting the rest; the fourth-listed decider retired 2026-08-09 as non-discriminating** |
| **AB-35** | **`positionCache`: what is derived, what invalidates it, and what stops it becoming a second truth?** Opened 2026-08-06 from [P2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) clause 2 --- a denormalized read structure holding expanded graphs across multiple host nodes, so that host-nesting does not cost a read per level. **Not documented anywhere today; this is new design, not recall of an existing decision.** **One rule proposed with local precedent: the write path never touches the cache --- invalidate-and-rederive only.** [`mtw-wml`'s schema README](../../../../../packages/mtw-wml/documentation/schema/README.md) records this codebase backing out of denormalizing child data upward as "an enormous source of unnecessary complexity," replaced by **selectors** computing condensed views from the source-of-truth tree. The analogy is limited --- that was authored source, not a runtime cache --- but the discipline transfers exactly, and without it the cache becomes a second truth and the phantom returns as a coherence problem one layer up. **Open sub-questions:** what the invalidation unit is (a host? a subtree? a shard?), whether expansion is eager or on-demand, how it interacts with `internalCache.Positions` which already wraps `get`/`set` at the ephemera boundary, and whether an expanded view is ever *written through* for a read-modify-write --- which the rule forbids and something will eventually want to do. ~~**Scope clarified 2026-08-06, and a second site now exists:** P3's ingress port list is derived data placed deliberately inside `positionGraph`, so the plan has two derived-data sites governed differently.~~ **Withdrawn the same day --- the ingress list moved into this cache, so there is exactly one derived-data site and one rule.** **What the episode leaves behind is worth keeping, though, because the general principle was stated and then not needed:** *derived data may live in truth when the read path demands it, provided a conflict rule and a repair story both exist* --- true, matching the shipped `Meta`-placement dual write, and the reason it did not apply here is that **the read path turned out not to demand it.** **A concrete gain for this row: the ingress list is now its first named consumer**, and it comes with a shape --- an object's live ports in a room are exactly those whose egress host is that room, so this entry is repaired by **recompute, not merge**. That is a materially easier invalidation story than the general case and worth designing the cache's contract around rather than treating as a special case. **A sub-question this row did not have, added 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) --- and it is the one finding that sweep produced that is *new* rather than stale.** This cache is specified as holding **expanded graphs across multiple host nodes**. **An expansion that crosses a port boundary is exactly what locked-frame clause 2 buys encapsulation to prevent, arriving as a performance measure rather than as a decision** --- and the lock's [own recorded risk](#what-the-lock-delivered-for-scale-invariance-and-where-it-stops-assessed-2026-08-06) is that external code cannot see what scale a thing is at, so *"the failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained."* **A flattening cache is the mechanism by which that happens silently. So: does expansion stop at a port --- and if it does not, what preserves the caller's knowledge that it crossed one?** **Answered in candidate form 2026-08-12 by [P6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure), which is this row's first positive content and re-bases the whole row.** The diagnosis P6 starts from is that this row was **unfalsifiable as written**: P2 clause 2 justified the cache entirely by latency, and a cache justified only by speed has no claim in it to argue against --- which is why the row sat at *not started* for six days while everything around it moved. **P6's replacement claim: `ludicCache` is not a faster view of `ludicGraph`; it is the structure serving reference-location, held at the depth the current scene has surfaced.** **The port-boundary question then resolves without needing expansion to stop:** expansion does cross ports, because reference-location must (a player can say "fork"), but **a hit returns a handle rather than a subgraph**, so no caller ever receives a crossed-port structure and nothing structural can be silently concluded from the cache. **The invariant is restated and strengthened rather than merely kept:** *there is exactly one **mutable** truth about structure; attention is immutable, append-only, and answers a different question; the cache is derived from both and authoritative over neither* --- **enforceable by shape** (handle-return makes write-through impossible rather than forbidden) where *the write path never touches the cache* was enforceable only by discipline, and it survives the arrival of attention state instead of being contradicted by it. **Two consequences this row must carry forward.** (i) The *first concrete consumer* credited above --- the ingress list --- **stays, and P6 explains why it was right to put it here.** *(P6's first form claimed the opposite --- that the list is traversal data and would leave the cache, reversing PQ-1 again. **Corrected the same day** by [running it against the axes](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#which-axes-want-the-ingress-list-and-why-pq-1-does-not-reverse-2026-08-12).)* **Traversal never reads the list** --- a room edge naming `ROPE:4` addresses that port directly --- so its only unique contribution is **ports no edge names**, and enumerating referrable things nothing else names is reference-location by definition. **The 2026-08-06 placement was decided on a storage argument (weak 1:1 versus strong N:1) and is now independently confirmed by a job argument**; this row's *first concrete consumer* is better described as **the first worked instance of what the cache is for**. (ii) **Attention is new durable state**, which the first form of the proposal wrongly denied; what keeps it from being a second truth is that it is append-only, written at emit time by code that already knows the answer, and answers a different question. **What stays open is much narrower than what closed:** demotion reduces to a window definition, but only if the window ends --- and a busy hub that never empties has no scene boundary (**PC-1**) | AB-6, AB-33, AB-34; **P6 (PC-1..PC-4)** | **Open, and designable for the first time --- re-based on [P6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure) 2026-08-12.** Justification swapped from latency to attention; port-boundary objection answered by handle-return; invariant restated and strengthened; demotion reduced to a window definition. **Unresolved: PC-1's unbounded window.** **Provisional --- licenses no code** |
| **AB-36** | **The reverse edge-reference index, promoted from parked to prerequisite.** Opened 2026-08-06 on its **fourth** independent appearance in this initiative --- AB-29 counted three and treated it as "the most load-bearing parked item so far"; [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)'s **PQ-6** is the fourth, and it is the one that makes the item blocking rather than expensive. **The need, stated generally: nothing in the system can find the referrers of a thing.** Membership adjacency indexes **node** placement (`getMembershipContainers`), not **edge-only** references on other hosts --- documented as the "same-host incident edges only" limitation in [`ludicGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/AGENT.md), whose parked remedy is "a post-clear sweep of Coyote `gameRooms` graphs, or a reverse edge-reference index." **The four consumers, which want the same index for four unrelated reasons:** (1) stale Exit edges after take-hold, the original; (2) AB-29's licensing-relation removal, where discovery runs backwards from a relation to its dependents; (3) AB-31's fission, where every co-parent's host must be known at plan time and `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once; (4) PQ-6's egress discovery. **Why the fourth changes its status: it takes a shipped safety property with it.** `removeObject` / `removeCharacter` assert-and-throw on residual relational edges with deliberately no silent-strip variant (BD-33/35) --- enforceable today only because edges are same-host and locally discoverable. A cross-host port reference breaks that precondition, so **PQ-7 is not a new risk but the loss of an existing guarantee**, and it is downstream of this row. ~~**Four independent needs is the argument; do not re-derive it a fifth time.**~~ **Demoted 2026-08-06, one exchange after being promoted, and the count that justified the promotion was wrong by half.** [Single-use ports](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) name the graph they exit to, so a whole's port list **is** a reverse index for its own referrers --- **which serves two of the four consumers**: PQ-6 (directly) and AB-31's fission footprint (which needs exactly "every host referencing this whole"). **The other two are untouched and remain the real content of this row:** stale Exit edges after take-hold, and AB-29's backwards discovery from a licensing relation to its dependents --- neither is a port case, and no port list helps either. **The lesson is about the argument, not the index:** "four independent appearances" was doing the work of a proof, and a design change halved it overnight. A recurrence count is evidence that something is needed, never evidence about *what*. **Its second surviving consumer got harder, noted 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10):** AB-29's backwards discovery from a licensing relation to its dependents must now reach a sibling link **inside a whole's graph**, one level deeper than when this row was written. **Whether it may reach there at all is [the open question about clause 2's direction](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-finding-that-is-about-the-lock-rather-than-a-row-locked-frame-clause-2-does-not-state-its-direction)** --- if the strict reading holds, this consumer needs a mediating construct nobody has proposed, and the index alone would not be enough. | AB-28, AB-29, AB-31, AB-34 | **Open --- real but no longer blocking; two consumers, not four; the surviving pair aggravated 2026-08-10 by locked-frame clause 2** |
| **AB-37** | **Whole-level reasoning summaries: who owns them, and are they stored or derived?** **Naming flagged 2026-08-09, conclusions untouched:** *whole-level* implies a summary tier, and there is none --- this row's own finding that *sufficiency is question-relative, so there is no single summary* says summaries are at whatever level is read. Rename before graduating; see [the whole/part dissolution](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09). Opened 2026-08-07 from [C7's nesting question](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07). A nested whole gives an LLM a fact at the right scale --- *a long thread winding through many rooms* --- **sufficient** for questions the flat form can only answer by re-deriving continuity from twenty constituents. **The comparison did not price the summary's upkeep, and that is this row.** Authored, it goes stale the moment the thread changes; derived, the derivation has moved to write time rather than gone away. **This is the mutation axis charging for a reasoning gain** --- the first cross-axis cost [the frame](#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to) has surfaced, and the reason the frame was worth writing. **Distinct from AB-6**, which asks stored-vs-derived about *member sets*; a summary is a different object with a different invalidation shape, and conflating them would hide that. **Two things already known.** (1) **By [the strength test](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06) a summary is N:1 and therefore *strong*** --- it saves reading every constituent, the same class as the membership node and unlike the ingress list that got moved to cache. The plan's own rule would let it live in truth. (2) **Sufficiency is question-relative**, so there is no single summary: *a long thread winding through many rooms* answers *is it still attached* and not *can I climb it*. **That is the row's hard part**, and it is why [the structural half of C7's finding --- relations porting through the boundary --- is load-bearing and the curated summary is not](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07): porting generalises across questions and prose does not. **Do not design the summary before deciding whether porting alone suffices.** **Narrowed 2026-08-07 by [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork) / AB-40(b):** a second, independent case (a place setting losing and regaining a part) reaches for the same stored-vs-derived answer C7 and AB-6's contested note both pointed at --- **settling**, via P4's topology-change rule, neither fully stored nor recomputed at read time. Still does not decide *which* summary or how sufficiency is judged; only narrows the upkeep question. | AB-6, AB-35, AB-19, AB-40, P4 iteration 1 | **Open, `P4-i1-dependent` on the upkeep half only --- porting-only is still the cheaper hypothesis for the summary-content question and has not been tested against a question it fails; upkeep leans toward settling rather than open-ended, conditional on P4's [named rollback trigger](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) not firing** |
| **AB-38** | **Does "Improvisation" name the mechanism or one of AB-14's two triggers?** **Split out of AB-0 on 2026-08-07** when the naming half locked; the ID is new because IDs are stable and never reused, and the question is unchanged --- it is **not** resolved by *Abstraction Fractal*, which answers the concept's name and says nothing about the mechanism's. **The collision, restated once so this row stands alone.** [Improvisation is the objects lane's mechanism, generalized](#improvisation-is-the-objects-lanes-mechanism-generalized) establishes that minting an unauthored component is a **shipped** mechanism raised to a general one. But AB-14 also uses **Improvisation** for the **downward, operation-driven trigger**, paired with **Recognition** as the upward one. **The word does both jobs** --- the same conflation AB-14 was restructured to remove --- and if Improvisation is the mechanism then Improvisation and Recognition are **not peers**: Recognition is a trigger that invokes it, and operational necessity is the other trigger. **Two ways out, unchanged:** **(a)** Improvisation and Recognition stay the two triggers and the shared mechanism takes its own name; **(b)** Improvisation names the mechanism and the downward trigger is renamed. **Why it did not ride out on AB-0's lock, deliberately:** the corpus evidence that settled the concept name (no privileged level, roles not kinds) bears on *levels*, not on *triggers*, so nothing that resolved AB-0 touches this. Resolving it by momentum is the move the lock's own warning forbids. **One thing has changed since it was raised.** The corpus now tags every case by trigger, and **three of eleven fire *neither*** --- [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system) and [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) because they are **authored**, [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) because it is **lifecycle**. That is evidence the trigger vocabulary is incomplete *independently* of what the mechanism is called, and it leans (a): a mechanism named separately from its triggers can gain a third trigger without renaming anything. **A lean, not a decision.** | AB-14, AB-19, AB-21 | **Open --- inherited live from AB-0; (a) leans on new evidence** |
| **AB-39** | **When is a causal state answer evaluated, and against which snapshot?** Opened 2026-08-07 from [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable), which established that *is the door powered* is **reachability through intact edges**, not a lookup --- so something must run it, at some point, over some version of the graph. **Distinct from the two rows it will be mistaken for.** **AB-6** asks stored-vs-derived about **member sets**; this asks when a *computation over* the members runs. **AB-35** governs `positionCache` derivation and invalidation for **positional** expansion; a causal answer invalidates on a different event (any edge cut anywhere on the path, not a host change) and has no natural cache unit. Conflating either would hide the difference. **The hard constraint is already known:** `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs **once**, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered --- so an evaluation whose result determines *which hosts an operation touches* must complete before the footprint is fixed. A door that opens only if powered is exactly that shape. **One existing pattern is likely the answer and should be tested before anything is invented:** this codebase already re-checks across snapshots, evaluating at dry-run and again at commit, and treats the recheck as a **safety property rather than duplicated work**. If a causal answer is cheap to recompute, that pattern applies directly. **The open sub-questions:** what the evaluation unit is (a system? a path? a query?), whether a fault short-circuits the walk, what an answer means when the graph is mid-transaction, and --- from C12's finding 3 --- **whether an unreachable address returns *unknown* rather than the whole's answer**, since coarsening a reachability fact is unsound rather than merely coarse. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche C)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): written in the pre-lock vocabulary the day after the lock.** *Reachability through intact edges*, run *over some version of **the** graph* --- under locked-frame clause 1 there is no *the* graph, and a causal path crossing a whole's boundary is a path through a port, which is what bounds this row's own *what is the evaluation unit* sub-question. **The row carries no port, H3 or PQ dependency at all.** It also splits [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable)'s finding 3 in two: *not connected* and *not addressable* are different unreachables, and returning the whole's answer is unsound for the first and exactly right for the second. | AB-6, AB-35, AB-1, P4 iteration 1 | **Open, `P4-i1-dependent` in part --- largely absorbed by [P4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat) the same week:** under settling the answer is *against the last settled state, plus pending impulses*, which iteration 1 alone supplies. The row's live residue, **PS-1** and **PS-3**, is about the reachability mechanism, not the cascade's staging, and stays open at any iteration depth. **Do not close it into P4** --- P4-iteration-1 is now an accepted Prototype rather than an unpromoted proposal, but it is still a bet with code behind it and a [named rollback trigger](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07); if it fires, this question returns intact |
| **AB-41** | **Ephemeral / virtual abstractions.** Opened 2026-08-07 from [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up). Construct an H3-shaped whole (root, parts, ports) **in memory, for the duration of one operation, never written to the graph** --- the same computation `computeCarryClosure` already does, retyped as AB-3's already-anticipated collapse rather than a bespoke fragment. **Answers AB-18's "a carry closure?" scope candidate with a concrete trigger**: the operation itself (pick up), not arrangement detection. **Dissolves the vacuous-abstraction threshold question C14 opened with**: constructing the ephemeral form is always cheap and safe, so there is no firing threshold to tune --- the open question moves entirely to *promotion* (ephemeral to durable), which C14 argues is [P4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)'s narrative heat's job (iteration 3, not built) rather than a new mechanism. **Distinct from AB-30**: AB-30's wall is addressable-never-a-member *permanently*, as an edge; this is addressable-never-a-member *for one operation's duration*, as a whole. **Noted, not developed:** the same construct plausibly serves [C3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c3-the-snare-trap-coyote-cannot-name)'s reasoning-context packaging problem, a use on the reasoning axis rather than the mutation axis this row was opened from. **Clause added 2026-08-10: an ephemeral whole may not absorb a Feature, and this row is where the Feature narrowing's guard belongs.** **The loophole it closes, and it is specific to *ephemeral* wholes:** *"features move only with their whole"* is trivially defeated by **wrapping a whole around the feature, moving the whole, and dissolving it.** A **durable** whole containing a mountain is a modelling act someone performed and is separately governed; an **ephemeral** one is minted per-operation, for free, by the engine --- so the exploit is mechanical rather than authorial, and the guard belongs at the mint. **Behaviour: boundary, not error.** When absorption reaches a Feature the Feature stays put and the edge to it is handled by `boundaryEdgeOutcomes` (`dissolve`/`carry`/`defer`) rather than throwing --- reusing shipped discipline instead of adding a failure mode, and letting *take the book off the wall-mounted shelf* simply work with the shelf left behind. **Two representation-layer rules were considered for this job and both declined, because they guard the wrong layer.** **(A) *Features are never the subject of a spatial relation.*** Faithful to the mechanism --- `computeCarryClosure` absorbs the **subject** of an `On` edge when the target moves (`classify('On','target') = 'carry'`, `otherId = edge.from`) --- but it restricts **edge legality** to prevent an **operation**, and its fidelity is contingent on the transfer table, so a future kind that carries subjects differently would silently break it. **(B) *Features may be subjects only toward Room / Area / Feature targets.*** Declined on a sharper failure: **mobility is a per-instance fact, not a kind fact.** A ship's cabin is a `Room` and moves; a boulder is an `Object` and does not. B **permits exactly what it exists to prevent** in the moving-room case, which makes it a lossy proxy --- [a model change wearing dirt's clothing](#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) rather than the clean answer computed faster. **The accounting that actually separated them, recorded because the first pass got it wrong:** both proposals carry the *same shape* of dirt --- a partition plus a rule naming one component kind --- so counting enumerations discriminates nothing, and *"A adds no kind enumeration"* was simply false. **The test is fidelity, not quantity**, and on fidelity A was exact-but-fragile and B lossy. **Both are moot under this clause**, which needs neither partition: the graph stays uniform and the guard sits at the one site that absorbs. **What this clause does not close --- the second hinge.** An LLM-proposed mutation could improvise a **durable** whole around the mountain and move that; no ephemeral abstraction is involved, so this gate never fires. **That is the P5/licence surface and it stays open** --- the ephemeral gate closes the *mechanical* exploit, not the *reasoning* one, and must not be cited as a complete answer. **Convenient landing:** this row is [Phase 5 candidate (b)](#recommended-order), so the gate is exercised in the first slice built, and it is safely **additive** against that slice's acceptance bar (*today's carry-closure coverage passes unchanged*) --- Features are not graph nodes today, so no existing test can cover Feature absorption and the gate cannot regress the bar it is judged against. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche C)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): inherits [Finding 3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#finding-3-mint-transfers-members-across-a-boundary-the-shipped-code-can-only-sever-carry-or-defer), and this is the first slice that will be built.** An ephemeral whole is *an H3-shaped whole (root, parts, **ports**)* minted around objects that already exist, so its boundary needs port bindings under locked-frame clause 2 exactly as a durable one's does --- and `boundaryEdgeOutcomes` (`dissolve`/`carry`/`defer`), named here as the absorption mechanism, is the closed union with no port outcome. **Softer than AB-5** (nothing is written, so the ports need not persist) **and sharper** (the carry closure *is* the behaviour, so a wrong classification is a wrong committed move). The 2026-08-10 Feature guard sits on the same mechanism and inherits whatever the fourth outcome becomes. | AB-3, AB-6, AB-18, AB-30, H3 clause 3, P4 | **Open --- newly named; construction (via H3's whole shape) has a clear answer, promotion criterion is deferred to P4 iteration 3 and not yet actionable** |
| **AB-42** | **Are established negatives ludic-graph nodes, or a collection beside the graph?** Opened 2026-08-09 from conversation, following [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about), which established that the **negative record is the one thing storage lacks** --- established-present mints rows and *not established* is correctly represented by nothing, so this row is the whole of the storage question. **Two candidates. (a) A negative collection** owned by ~~the room or the whole~~ **its containing host** --- the obvious shape, cheap, and no new node kind. **Corrected 2026-08-09: *the room or the whole* was not a fork.** A room is a whole of its contents and a whole is a host of its parts, so that was one slot asked from two directions --- see [the whole/part dissolution](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-wholepart-dissolution-and-its-unswept-consequences-2026-08-09), which this row and [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about) reintroduced two days after it was locked. **The correction removes a phantom axis, not a candidate:** (a)-versus-(b), collection versus node, is untouched and is this row's real question. **(b) Negatives are nodes in the ludic graph**, carrying ordinary relation-kind edges like any other node. **The argument for (b) is one this plan has already made, one level up, and it is the strongest thing on the table.** [P2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge)'s conceptual error is *hosting creates a phantom edge*: membership expressed by **containment in a collection** rather than by an edge, leaving a link that is "not merely unlabeled but **untypeable** --- the edge could not be written into `edges` even without a kind, because one endpoint has no node to be." **A negative list is that shape exactly**, a scalar sibling of `nodes`/`edges` holding facts that cannot be edge endpoints --- so (a) recreates the phantom this plan diagnosed and set out to remove, one scale down and *after* the diagnosis. **The discriminating requirement is causal, and it is what opened the row:** absence has measurable causal effect --- a flashlight gives no light, deducible from *no battery or power source* **without knowing the flashlight's other details**. Under [P4](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#retraction-dissolves-under-a-dataflow-reading-and-that-reading-is-the-ludic-native-one)'s dataflow reading, consequence propagates **along edges between nodes holding values**; a negative that is not a node cannot be an endpoint, so the cascade must consult a second structure that is not the graph --- the phantom again, now on the hot path. **[H3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) clause 5 says the same from the boundary side:** ports bind any relation kind crossing a boundary, and a negative *inside* a whole with consequence *outside* it (no battery, flashlight dark, read from the room) needs an interior end for a port to bind to. A collection entry has none. **Two real costs on (b), neither fatal and both unpriced.** **(i) Accumulation, and it cuts against the initiative's own purpose.** Negatives are cheaper to create than positives --- *no* costs a sentence, *yes* costs a mint --- so they accrue faster, and every traversal, summary and LLM packet pays for them. A kitchen carrying five knives and five hundred *there is no X* nodes is **worse** reasoning context than one carrying five knives, which inverts the bounding the abstraction layer exists to provide. **(ii) A negative is not an `OBJECT#`** --- no `shortName`, no body, no placement --- and node kinds are tag-discriminated `'Character' \| 'Object'` today, so (b) needs a third tag. **It may be the first thing this initiative has found that genuinely cannot be an Object**, which *pressures* AB-12's everything-is-an-Object reframe rather than being absorbed by it. **Restated 2026-08-09 under [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), and the question is now about *licences* rather than about negatives.** **Both recorded costs move favourably.** *Accumulation* collapses --- one licence per entity whose content narrows, rather than one node per negative, and a licence **coarsens** where independent negative nodes do not. *Node-kind* becomes principled rather than awkward: a **licence** node is an ordinary modelling construct where a **negative** node was a third tag with no natural reading, so the pressure on AB-12 is real but no longer strange. **And P5 supplies a second argument for (b)** independent of P2's phantom: a licence is *manipulable* --- widened, narrowed, delegated --- and a thing that changes in play wants to be an edge endpoint. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche C)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): *nodes in the ludic graph* --- locked-frame clause 1 makes **which graph** a second question, and it partly prices cost (i).** A negative established inside a whole is a node in **that whole's** graph and is invisible to traversal, summary and packet-building at the containing level, so *every traversal, summary and LLM packet pays for them* holds only at the negative's own scale. **It does not rescue this row's own example** --- a kitchen's five hundred *there is no X* nodes sit in the kitchen's graph, which is the level doing the reasoning --- so the accumulation objection narrows to *negatives established at the scale you are reasoning at* rather than dissolving. | AB-12, AB-43, AB-44, AB-21, P2, P4, P5, H3 clause 5 | **Open --- (b) strengthened; both costs cheaper under P5, and the question is now "how is a licence represented," not "how is a negative represented"** |
| **AB-43** | **What is an established negative keyed by, when its referent does not exist?** Opened 2026-08-09. **This is [AB-21](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s identity obligation in its hardest form.** *"Is there a deep fryer"* and *"is there a deep-fat fryer"* must reach the **same** negative, or the world answers inconsistently and the record does no work at all --- but there is no id to key on, because nothing exists. `stableKey` lives on `Meta::Object`, and there is no `Meta::Object`. **Candidates, none assessed:** a normalized phrase; a semantic vector (the objects lane already carries `EMBEDDING#IMPROMPTU`, so this **inherits improvisation gap 3** --- whether a `shortName`-derived vector is the right text --- in a harder form, since there is no `shortName` either); a term from a closed type vocabulary; or keying **the question** rather than the thing. **Note the closed-vocabulary trap explicitly:** an enumerated list is only correct where the vocabulary is genuinely enumerable, and *things a player might ask about* is not --- so a type-term answer needs a fallback rather than a table. **Deliberately separate from AB-42, and must not be used to decide it:** both horns need this, since a collection entry needs a key exactly as much as a node needs an identity. **A cost shared by both options cannot discriminate between them**, and folding it into the representation row would make the cheaper-looking option look cheaper than it is. **Split 2026-08-09 by [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), and half of it dissolves.** A **blanket** licence limit is keyed by **the entity it attaches to** --- an id that exists --- so the hard version of this question never arises there; the row's original framing was a category error, since a licence limit is keyed by its *holder*, not by what it denies. **What remains is the narrowing case, and it is better-posed than it was:** not *give identity to a nonentity*, which cannot be done, but *describe a closed region of licence space*, which is an ordinary predicate/description problem. Comparably hard, but no longer ill-formed --- and the closed-vocabulary trap below applies to it unchanged. | AB-21, AB-42, AB-18, AB-38, P5 | **Open for narrowing licences only --- the blanket case is dissolved by P5; question reshaped from identity to description** |
| **AB-44** | **Do all negatives carry causal force, or only some?** Opened 2026-08-09, and it is plausibly **upstream of AB-42 rather than beside it.** *There is no deep fryer* looks **inert**: nothing downstream reasons from it, and it exists only to keep the world's answers stable across repeated asking. *There is no battery* is **load-bearing**: a consequence follows from it and propagates. **This is [AB-19](#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s description/mechanism split arriving in a place it was not written for**, after [C16](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c16-the-bare-table-and-the-room-that-is-just-a-mess) already used it to decide the messy room --- the plan has taken repeated arrival as weak evidence of a real joint before, and should here too. **If it holds, negatives are not one kind and AB-42 may have a per-kind answer rather than a global one:** a mechanism-negative earns a node because something depends on it; a description-negative may not, which would also bound AB-42's accumulation cost to the smaller set. **It also structures lifecycle, which is otherwise homeless.** An inert negative is a candidate for expiry --- [PS-10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s *how does a lead die on purpose* --- while a load-bearing one **cannot** silently vanish without the cascade depending on it going wrong. **Do not settle decay for negatives before this row.** **One warning against the easy reading, on AB-19's own precedent:** *load-bearing* must not become a compile-fictional-physics judgment computed from structure. AB-19 already rejected deciding consequence deterministically rather than by an LLM step. What this row asks is whether the **representation** differs by kind --- not whether the system can decide which negatives matter. **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche C)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): locked-frame clause 2 offers a discriminator for this exact question, and this row's own warning is why it cannot simply be taken.** Whether anything **outside** a whole can depend on a negative is decided by whether a **port binds it**, so load-bearing is boundary-relative: a negative may be load-bearing inside its whole and inert beyond it. **That reshapes the question to *at what scale is this negative load-bearing*, and the lifecycle half with it** --- an unbound negative is expiry-eligible at the containing level without being expiry-eligible inside. **But port-binding is structure**, and AB-19 rejected deciding consequence deterministically from structure, so this is a candidate for the representation question rather than for the judgment this row forbids. | AB-19, AB-42, AB-9, PS-10 | **Open --- newly opened; likely upstream of AB-42, and the lifecycle question is parked inside it rather than given its own row.** **Widened 2026-08-09 by [C18](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c18-three-place-settings-and-only-two-of-them-may-have-a-napkin): the inert/load-bearing split does not cover *licence* negatives (AB-45), which are neither.** **Then narrowed the same day by [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item), which is a better outcome than the widening:** if every negative is a recorded licence narrowing, this row is no longer asking *which negatives are one thing versus another* but the sharper **does closing licence for X also assert that X is absent?** Licence-closure is then **universal** and causal force **variable** --- the split survives, on a smaller and better-defined surface. **The authoring exception is what keeps the two from simply collapsing:** an author may add a deep fryer to a kitchen whose licence is closed, so closure cannot entail absence in general | 
| **AB-45** | **What licenses improvisation inside a whole --- and how is the absence of that licence recorded?** Opened 2026-08-09 from [C18](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c18-three-place-settings-and-only-two-of-them-may-have-a-napkin). **The case, in one line:** three `PlaceSetting` wholes --- authored, minted from a coarse envelope, and recognized over five existing objects --- are **structurally identical**, and the first two may improvise a napkin while the third may not. **So licence is not derivable from the graph and must be recorded.** **The discriminator, sharper than provenance:** a whole with a coarse **intensional** description (*a place setting*, *what a tavern needs*) has open constituents; a whole that is **extensional** --- constituted by enumerating members, its name applied afterward --- licenses nothing further. **Improvisation gap 2 already states this** while describing a shortfall: *"its body is nearly empty and its member set is the content."* **What the record must not say, and this is the row's main trap:** *there is nothing else on this table* is a **completeness claim about the fiction**, and asserting it establishes a negative nobody established --- C17's closed-world defect one scale down, on the construct built to prevent it. The correct content denies the **whole's generative capacity**, not the napkin's existence: the napkin stays *not established* and remains improvisable by any other licensed route. **Consequently "blanket negative" is probably the wrong name** --- it is a licence statement, and calling it a negative invites the completeness reading --- **and naming it is part of this row.** **Not covered by the negatives cluster:** AB-43 has no referent to key (it denies an open class), AB-44's inert/load-bearing split contains neither option (it has no in-world causal consequence and is not inert --- it constrains the *system*), and AB-42 may answer it as a **property of the root node** rather than a node, which is a second independent route to that row's per-kind answer. **One collision to resolve rather than inherit:** AB-14 holds that *"`ASSET#IMPROVISATION` is a merge layer, so provenance is invisible downstream."* **Licence is provenance-determined, so provenance cannot be fully invisible** --- this is the second dent in that claim after [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-7-the-negative-record-is-where-authored-and-improvised-truth-actually-collide-and-the-merge-stack-cannot-arbitrate-it) Finding 7, and the two should be settled together. **The intensional reading may soften it:** what must survive downstream is arguably *does this whole have an intensional description*, which is a property of the whole rather than a provenance tag --- if so the merge-layer claim holds and only the vocabulary needs widening. **Not asserted; it is the row's leading candidate.** **Reframed the same day, and the row is now secondary to [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item).** Treating licence as a **first-class graph item** rather than reasoning about it in the abstract resolves three of this row's four difficulties outright --- see P5's repair table --- and **inverts the framing recorded above**: licence is the primary construct and negatives are the shadow it casts, rather than licence being an odd third kind of negative. **The provenance collision is closed by it** (licence is carried by the whole; provenance only sets its initial value), and **the naming question is answered in substance**: *blanket negative* is the wrong name because it is a licence narrowed to zero. **What survives as this row's own work:** the exact vocabulary, and whether *licence closed for X* and *X is established absent* are one fact or two --- see P5's second open item. | AB-9, AB-14, AB-19, AB-29, AB-42, AB-43, AB-44, **P5** | **Open --- three of four difficulties resolved by P5; vocabulary and the closure-versus-absence identity remain** |
| **AB-46** | **Narrative weight: how much investment stands behind an established fact, and where is it carried?** Opened 2026-08-09 from [Narrative integrity is in service to reincorporation](#narrative-integrity-is-in-service-to-reincorporation-2026-08-09). **The requirement:** if narrative integrity serves **reincorporation of participant contributions**, then what a contradiction costs depends on **who invested in the thing contradicted** --- authored, worked-for-in-play, or improvised in passing. A tavern flipping well-stocked to poorly-stocked is near-costless when the system improvised both states and **expensive** when an author wrote it that way or players hauled the supplies in. **Weight is therefore the dial the damping rule needs**: protect high-weight commitments, let low-weight ones drift. **Where it goes:** *alongside or within the permissive structures* --- a licence record carrying not only what was established but how much stands behind it. **Why it does not fight [P5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#licence-transfer-import-is-a-copy-and-provenance-is-deliberately-not-traced-2026-08-09)'s no-cascade decision, which is the first thing to check and it passes:** weight is a **value carried on the record**, not a pointer to its source. Copy, not reference --- the same discipline licence transfer already adopted, so recording weight requires no provenance graph and no cascade. **The raw material already exists in storage:** authored (participation stack / WML), player-driven (kernel-committed step sequences and their events), system-improvised (`ASSET#IMPROVISATION`). **This row is not "how do we know" but "what scale, carried where, and consumed by whom."** **Three jobs and two directions, all landing on one scalar --- which is the row's best evidence that the quantity is real rather than an annotation invented per problem.** **(1)** How much contradicting a fact **strains** narrative integrity. **(2)** The **effort required to overturn** it --- and, [symmetrically for things not yet established, the effort required to *establish* them](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#correction-a-mystery-caps-a-minimum-effort-not-a-maximum-time-2026-08-09), which is what a mystery's minimum-investment floor is. **One quantity measured forward and backward, covering the whole lifecycle rather than half of it.** **(3)** Whether a contradiction involving it may be **deferred** --- [registering a mystery is cheap only where nobody is vested](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#how-fiction-actually-resolves-a-contradiction-it-registers-a-mystery-2026-08-09), so weight is what says whether *I wonder what's going on there* is an intriguing loose end or an unresolved social tension. **Concrete semantics supplied 2026-08-09 by [C21](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-4-two-constructs-one-enforcement-point--settled-after-two-reversals-in-a-day): weight is the *effort required to overturn*.** Pleasingly symmetric with *how much investment stands behind it* --- the more that went in, the more it takes to undo --- and it gives the scale an operational reading rather than a comparative one. **It also converts refusals into hooks:** *nobody has ever done this; it is possible, but a significant and long-term quest* is a weight statement, and it is the response that distinguishes a weighted world fact from a genre boundary (**AB-47**), which no effort overturns. **Bound established by the same case:** a genre boundary is **not on this scale at all**, so weight is not the universal currency --- do not model it as one with an infinite value. **Open sub-questions:** is weight a scalar or a provenance tag; does it decay (a contribution from a year ago versus last session --- and note [PS-10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) asks a structurally similar question for parked impulses); does player-driven outrank authored or the reverse; and does weight belong only on licence records or on any established fact. **Deliberately not designed now** --- the requirement is recorded so it is not rediscovered, and the plan has no consumer able to act on it until damping is being built. | AB-14, AB-29, AB-45, AB-47, P5, PS-4, PS-10 | **Open --- requirement recorded, deliberately undesigned; compatibility with P5's no-cascade checked and holds; semantics narrowed 2026-08-09 to *effort to overturn*, and bounded away from genre boundaries by AB-47** |
| **AB-49** | **`positionGraph` will be lying --- does the naming consequence need handling before Phase 6?** **Split out of [AB-1](#settled-register) on 2026-08-14**, which created the debt and recorded it without a row. AB-1's resolution puts causal edges on the position substrate, so `EphemeraPositionGraph`, the lane name, and the graduated concepts vocabulary all describe something **narrower than what they hold**. [The naming consequence](#the-naming-consequence-positiongraph-will-be-lying) states the problem in full and leaves it open. **The question this row asks is only *when*:** AB-1 fixes it as a minimum Phase 6 obligation, and whether a rename or a vocabulary widening is owed sooner --- before the relation-kind register stabilizes, before anything graduates to `AGENT.concepts.md` under the current name --- is undecided. **A rename is the cheap half; the expensive half is that the concepts tier has already graduated vocabulary under the narrow reading.** | AB-1, the relation-kind register | **Open --- newly split out; the problem is fully stated and only its scheduling is undecided** |
| **AB-50** | **Can a mutation step address a node inside another object's interior graph?** **Split out of [AB-40](#settled-register)(a) on 2026-08-14**, where it was carried as *"not a design fork"* and parked --- a classification [the back-sweep](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) withdrew without giving it a home. The move itself is [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork)'s: a part's node leaving `PlaceSetting`'s interior graph and landing in the character's, which **must stay synchronous**, since a player needs to reference and use the fork the instant it is taken. **Why it is a fork after all:** *direct addressing of interior nodes from outside* is [locked-frame clause 2](#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06)'s forbidden sentence **verbatim**, but that clause's scope over **mutation** rather than relation is unstated --- so this is either already answered *no*, or it is the case that forces the clause's scope to be decided. **Parked extension points is the wrong home**, as the back-sweep found: what is missing is the **behaviour**, not the code. **Also [Finding 5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#finding-5-ab-48s-two-containment-kinds-are-carry-in-both-directions-and-one-of-those-directions-absorbs-the-container)'s breaking case**, so it is load-bearing in two directions at once. | AB-34, AB-48, locked-frame clause 2, Finding 5 | **Open --- newly split out; no candidate answer being weighed, and the first job is deciding whether locked-frame clause 2 already answers it** |
| **AB-51** | **Write authority for social contracts: is out-of-character table negotiation a fourth provenance?** **Split out of [AB-47](#settled-register) on 2026-08-14**, which settles the representation --- two constructs, one enforcement point --- and explicitly does not settle this. **A world fact changes by authoring or by play; a social contract changes by out-of-character table negotiation**, which separates from *authored* only in multiplayer, and that is exactly where it matters. **Second part, carried here rather than given its own row because it has the same owner: the out-of-character response surface.** AB-47 establishes that both constructs have a legible out-of-character response --- *persuade other **players*** for a genre boundary, *a significant and long-term quest* for a weighted fact --- and that the genre-boundary case wants an escalation channel to moderators. Neither surface is designed. **One caution inherited: blast-radius reduction assumes a moderator eventually arrives**, which weakens as scale grows. **Explicitly not in scope:** the table's social contract itself, and the moderation practice around it. | AB-46, P5, PL-1 | **Open --- newly split out; provenance question is a genuine fork, response surface is undesigned rather than contested** |
| **AB-53** | **Must containment run root-to-part, or may `In`/`PartOf` edges run node-to-node inside a graph?** Split off [AB-4](#settled-register) 2026-08-19 rather than re-opening it, per the precondition that a live sub-question gets its own ID | Locked-frame clause 3 --- **but far more weakly than it looks, which is why this is a row and not a lock-lift.** Clause 3 fixes that the root is *relation-endpoint-**capable***; it does not say the root is the *only* legal containment endpoint. **Its own negative statement cuts the other way:** *"a root node is not a privileged kind of node ... a node occupying the root position in one graph."* Root-to-part gives the root a structural capability no other node has, which is at least adjacent to the privilege that statement disclaims --- worth checking against rather than asserting either way. Also constrained by AB-48 (**untouched**: two non-exclusive kinds is not in question) | **Open, and it blocks nothing today --- but it gates two things that are close.** Opened 2026-08-19 from implementation, not from the corpus. **Motivating case:** `ludicCache` is expected to author containment **nesting** rather than a flat root-to-child fan, which root-to-part cannot represent within one graph. **Two consequences already traced in code** ([LD-11](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), [LD-12](AGENT.ludicGraphPorts.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)): (a) root-to-part is what makes a containment edge *always* a boundary edge under transfer --- drop it and `applyTransferSet`'s **internal-edge** path becomes reachable, which moves an edge intact with the classifier never consulted, so LD-11 would be settling only half its own question; (b) root-to-part yields a **star** containment topology, making cycles structurally unrepresentable --- which is the real reason nothing checks `PartOf` cycles today. **Per [What would re-open a locked clause](#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause), a corpus case belongs in the Discussion record if this is to move by that standard** --- the ludicCache requirement is currently an implementation expectation, not a written case, and no one has written it up |

### Settled register

**Thirteen rows graduated 2026-08-14.** A resolved design row is not deleted --- deleting it strands every live row that argues from it. **The answer stays here; the derivation moved** to [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#settled-decisions-full-record), keyed by the same IDs.

**The Verdict cell is what a live row may cite.** It is written to be self-sufficient: if an open row's reasoning depends on something, that something is in the verdict, not behind the link. **Follow the link only to ask *why*** --- options weighed and rejected, supersession layers, the corpus argument.

**Settled is not closed forever.** Where a row carries a named re-open trigger, the verdict states it.

| ID | Question | Verdict | Settled | Derivation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **AB-0** | Is *Abstraction* the right name, and is it one name or several? | **The *Abstraction Fractal*** --- earned below the room, aspirational above it. **There is no `AbstractionFractal` type, entity or record**, and that negative is part of the lock. Three departures from a strict fractal are carried deliberately: **DAG not tree, finite depth, aspirational above the room.** Falsifiable by AB-1, which resolved compatibly. The live half was split out as **AB-38** before locking | 2026-08-07 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-0--naming-the-concept) |
| **AB-1** | One concept or several? Do composition, functional-state aggregate and multi-host extent share a structure? | **One substrate, distinct relation kinds above it** --- not one type with facets, and not merely a shared motivation. Covers all three facets. **Emphatically not one type:** *powers* is directed where part-of is containment, and a door attached to a power system is not part of it. **Two costs accepted, both since given resolution paths by P4 iteration 1:** AB-6's stored-versus-derived tension and AB-39's evaluation timing. **Named re-open trigger:** if [`diegeticLogic`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/diegeticLogic/AGENT.md)'s commitment to structure *without* a fully simulated physical space changes, this row re-opens. **This does not license a simulator.** Left a naming debt, now **AB-49** | 2026-08-07 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-1--one-concept-or-several) |
| **AB-2** | Node, host, or projection? | **Node and host collapse into one thing**, under locked-frame clause 3. ***Projection* is a read-side layer, not a third representation**, and belongs to AB-9. Closed via the clause-3 dependent set, which is what that set was enumerated for | 2026-08-09 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-2--node-host-or-projection) |
| **AB-3** | Does `EphemeraPositionGraph` gain a root concept? | **Yes --- uniformly across host kinds**, via locked-frame clause 3. Arrived independently five times. **`CarryClosureFragment` is the precedent, not the casualty** --- it does not collapse | 2026-08-09 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-3--a-root-concept-for-the-position-graph) |
| **AB-4** | What is the level-crossing relation? | **A new edge kind, `PartOf`** --- root to part, inside the whole's own graph, per AB-48. **Both of this row's forks were false.** The record option died with locked-frame clause 1; the endpoint-address horn was retracted by P2 itself on the day it was proposed. **Kind and endpoint granularity are independent axes** --- *edges versus endpoint-addressing* was a category error, and both are needed for different jobs. **Held-and-present needs no separate mechanism:** containment kinds take qualified endpoints, so `Theseus -Held-> thread:1` and `room -In-> thread:2` are both true, of different parts, in different graphs. **Named re-open trigger, added 2026-08-19 --- and it is scoped to three words of this verdict.** *"Root to part"* is now questioned by [AB-53](#open-decisions-design--plan-only): the trigger is a containment arrangement that root-to-part cannot represent within one graph, of which `ludicCache`'s expected **nesting** is the first candidate. **Everything else in this verdict stands unchanged** --- `PartOf` as a distinct edge kind, both forks being false, kind and endpoint granularity as independent axes, and the held-and-present example above, which does not depend on the restriction. **What a citing row may still assume:** that the level-crossing relation is an edge kind. **What it may not:** that only the root may sit at a containment edge's `from` end | 2026-08-09 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-4--the-level-crossing-relation) |

Not a continuous derivation, and **not a presentation-time inference**, which is rejected outright because it is the option that makes description a write path. **At the compile layer** as an abstract op (`establishWhole` / `dissolveWhole`), so `MutationKernelStep` stays closed. **Establish and dissolve are exact inverses**, and dissolve transfers members rather than deleting them. **Player-visible by design, always as a consequence of a committed write.** **A minted whole takes an `OBJECT#`** via the improvisation two-step --- which is what gives it a name to render, an embedding, and a render-cache host. **Two producers over one mechanism** (on-demand, recognition), so the kernel never learns which trigger fired. **Description mints nothing** except under AB-9's committed active-seeking carve-out. Two verifications parked; scope and trigger are AB-18's, plausibility is AB-19's | 2026-08-10 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-5--latent-vs-materialized-and-who-mints) |

The labeled-edge half was handed to AB-48. **Discharges AB-3's root-node trigger** | 2026-08-09 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-33--the-host-link-as-a-labeled-edge) |
| **AB-34** | Making addressing work in all directions: ports on the host boundary | **Ports are the mechanism**, and a host stays authoritative over what its interior exposes --- an outside edge terminates at a port rather than reaching through. **The shape claims are locked; only the calibration is provisional**, under [P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings). **Fourteen sub-questions live in the proposals file as PQ-1..PQ-14, none blocking** --- PQ-13 is the one needing a decision before anything downstream. **Both properties this row said to check first are among them:** declared-versus-derived is **PQ-9**, port-addressable-as-a-part is **PQ-5** (leaning yes, making the scheme recursive and requiring a cycle guard) | 2026-08-10 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-34--ports-on-the-host-boundary) |
| **AB-40** | The detach/reattach step kind | **Split; the design question this table owned is (b), and it is answered.** What `PlaceSetting`'s summary says once the fork is gone is **P4's topology-change rule**: the `part` edge's deletion emits its own impulse, exactly as a cut cable does, and a **settling wave rather than a synchronous step** regenerates the summary. **This does not need to be fast** --- the character already knows they hold the fork. `P4-i1-dependent`: **reverts to Open if P4's named rollback trigger fires.** (a), the structural move, is now **AB-50** | 2026-08-07 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-40--detach-and-reattach) |
| **AB-47** | Are flat genre-forbids the same construct as fact-derived permission narrowing? | **Two constructs, one enforcement point.** A bare genre-forbid is a **social contract** --- out-of-character, inviolable rather than weighted, with no diegetic explanation and none to be invented --- and it constrains **narration**, not only world-state. **The unification fails on cost, not on principle:** *a licence with weight infinite* makes **six consumers each branch on whether the weight is infinite**, and a sentinel that changes behaviour at every read site is two constructs with extra steps. **The shared half is the mint gate:** you cannot stop a player assembling a tube, powder and shot and asking for a gun, but you can **refuse to mint the abstraction**, after which the system steadily refuses to reason about the assembly --- and the refusal is **structurally silent**, since an unminted abstraction produces no packet. **Gives AB-46 its semantics: weight is the effort required to overturn.** Write authority and the response surface are now **AB-51** | 2026-08-09 | [record](AGENT.abstractionLayers.discussion.planning.md#ab-47--genre-forbids-and-fact-derived-narrowing) |
